\widetilde{r}=(\widetilde{r}_{+},\widetilde{r}_{-})
in the form

\widetilde{r}(\varphi)=\mathbf{A}\big(i_{0}(\varphi)\big),

where the application
\mathbf{A}
is given in (
4.12
). The reversibility and traveling properties of
i_{0}
read

\widetilde{r}(-\varphi,-x)=\widetilde{r}(\varphi,x)\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\widetilde{r}(\varphi,x+y)=\widetilde{r}(\varphi-\vec{\jmath}\,y,x).

In addition, one has the following estimates, for any
s\geqslant s_{0},

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{r}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d}1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.2)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s}+\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{0}}\max_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{\ell}\|_{s}.

(6.3)

(ii)

The linearized operator
\mathscr{L}_{\omega}
in (
6.1
) admits the following expression

\mathscr{L}_{\omega}=\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\big(\mathcal{L}^{(0)}-\varepsilon\partial_{x}\mathcal{R}\big)\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\,,\qquad\mathcal{R}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}T_{\mathbb{K}_{+}^{(d)}}&T_{\mathbb{K}_{+}^{(o)}}\\
T_{\mathbb{K}_{-}^{(o)}}&T_{\mathbb{K}_{-}^{(d)}}\end{pmatrix},

(6.4)

where
\mathcal{L}^{(0)}
is the operator defined by

\mathcal{L}^{(0)}\triangleq\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathbb{I}_{2}-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}{\rm d}_{\widetilde{r}}\big(\mathcal{J}\nabla H(\widetilde{r})\big),\qquad\mathbb{I}_{2}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}\textnormal{Id}&0\\
0&\textnormal{Id}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\textnormal{Id}\triangleq\textnormal{Id}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})},

(6.5)

whereas, for any
(\kappa,\nu)\in\{-,+\}\times\{d,o\},
the operator
T_{\mathbb{K}_{\kappa}^{(\nu)}}
is an integral operator in the form

T_{\mathbb{K}_{\kappa}^{(\nu)}}[\rho](a;\varphi,x)=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\rho(a;\varphi,x^{\prime})\mathbb{K}_{\kappa}^{(\nu)}(a;\varphi,x,x^{\prime}){\rm d}x^{\prime},

whose kernel

\mathbb{K}_{\kappa}^{(\nu)}
is momentum and reversibility preserving, that is

\displaystyle\mathbb{K}_{\kappa}^{(\nu)}(a;-\varphi,-x,-x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{K}_{\kappa}^{(\nu)}(a;\varphi,x,x^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\mathbb{K}_{\kappa}^{(\nu)}(a;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{K}_{\kappa}^{(\nu)}(a;\varphi,x+y,x^{\prime}+y)

and satisfies the following estimates for any
s\geqslant s_{0},

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{K}_{\kappa}^{(\nu)}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d}1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+2}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.6)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathbb{K}_{\kappa}^{(\nu)}\|_{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s+2}+\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{0}+2}\max_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{\ell}\|_{s+2}.

(6.7)

Remark 6.1
.

The operator
{\mathcal{L}}^{(0)}
in (
6.5
) is defined on a dense subspace of
{\bf L}^{2}
, even though, by the Hamiltonian nature of the nonlinear system, the linearized operator preserves the average in space. We operate in this way because in the end we are interested in proving the existence of a right inverse to the operator
{\mathscr{L}}_{\omega}
in (
6.4
), which acts trivially on the average in space.

Our next goal is to give a new expression of the linear operator
\mathcal{L}^{(0)}
in (
6.5
), more suitable for our following analysis. In particular, we highlight its homogeneous expansion up to smoothing pseudo-differential operators.

The linear operator
\mathbf{Q}
in (
4.2
) admits the following pseudo-differential formulation

\mathbf{Q}=\mathbb{I}_{2,0}+\begin{pmatrix}\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)&\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)\\
\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)&\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)\end{pmatrix},\qquad\mathbb{I}_{2,0}=\begin{pmatrix}\textnormal{Id}_{L_{0}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}&0\\
0&\textnormal{Id}_{L_{0}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\end{pmatrix},\qquad

where
\textnormal{Id}_{L_{0}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\triangleq\textnormal{Op}\big(\chi(\xi)\big)
denotes the identity operator of
L_{0}^{2}(\mathbb{T})
and the Fourier multipliers
\mathtt{K}_{\pm}(a,D)
are associated with the following symbol

\displaystyle\forall\,\xi\in\mathbb{R},\quad\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,\xi)

\displaystyle\triangleq\chi(\xi)\frac{1-\sqrt{1-b^{2}(a,\xi)}}{\sqrt{1-b^{2}(a,\xi)}},\qquad\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,\xi)\triangleq\chi(\xi)\frac{b(a,\xi)}{\sqrt{1-b^{2}(a,\xi)}}

(6.8)

\displaystyle\forall\,|\xi|\geqslant\tfrac{1}{2},\quad b(a,\xi)

\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{1}{2a|\xi|\left(\sqrt{a^{2}\xi^{2}+1}+a|\xi|+\frac{1}{2a|\xi|}\right)}\in(0,1).

(6.9)

We recall that the cut-off function
\chi
is defined in (
3.22
). Actually, we have the following more precise (but still rough) bound

\sup_{|\xi|\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\atop a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]}b(a,\xi)\leqslant\frac{2}{2+a_{0}^{2}}<1.

(6.10)

Note that
b(a,\xi)
can also be written

\forall\,|\xi|\geqslant\tfrac{1}{2},\quad b(a,\xi)=1-2a\xi\mathtt{r}(a,\xi),

where
\mathtt{r}(a,\xi)
is the symbol introduced in (
3.25
). Then, proceeding as in the proof of Lemma
3.1
-
(iv)
, we obtain the following estimates

\forall\,(k,\ell)\in\mathbb{N}^{2},\quad\sup_{|\xi|\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\atop a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]}\langle\xi\rangle^{\ell+2}\left|\partial_{a}^{k}\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}b(a,\xi)\right|\leqslant C(a_{0},k,\ell).

(6.11)

This implies in particular that
\mathtt{K}_{\pm}(a,D)\in OPS^{-2}.
In addition, since
{\mathtt{K}}_{\pm}(a,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}
and is independent of
(\varphi,x)
, then, according to Proposition
A.3
-
(vi)
, the operators
{\mathtt{K}}_{\pm}(a,D)
are reversibility and momentum preserving.
By (
4.2
) and (
3.30
), we also have

\mathbf{Q}^{-1}=\mathbb{I}_{2,0}+\begin{pmatrix}\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)&-\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)\\
-\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)&\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)\end{pmatrix}.

Recalling the notations (
3.17
)-(
3.18
), linearizing (
4.4
) gives

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}{\rm d}_{\widetilde{r}}\big(\mathcal{J}\nabla H(\widetilde{r})\big)[\rho]

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathbf{Q}^{-1}{\rm d}_{r}\big(\mathcal{J}\nabla\mathcal{E}\big)(\mathbf{Q}\widetilde{r})[\mathbf{Q}\rho]

(6.12)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Q}^{-1}\mathcal{J}\mathbf{M}_{\varepsilon\mathbf{Q}\widetilde{r}}\mathbf{Q}\rho

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Q}^{-1}\mathcal{J}\mathbf{M}_{0}\mathbf{Q}\rho+\mathbf{Q}^{-1}\mathcal{J}\big(\mathbf{M}_{\varepsilon\mathbf{Q}\widetilde{r}}-\mathbf{M}_{0}\big)\mathbf{Q}\rho.

Besides, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{\varepsilon\mathbf{Q}\widetilde{r}}-\mathbf{M}_{0}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\varepsilon r_{+}&0\\
0&-\varepsilon r_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{r}_{+}+\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)\widetilde{r}_{+}+\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)\widetilde{r}_{-}&0\\
0&-\widetilde{r}_{-}-\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)\widetilde{r}_{+}-\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)\widetilde{r}_{-}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\triangleq\varepsilon\begin{pmatrix}f_{+}(\widetilde{r})&0\\
0&-f_{-}(\widetilde{r})\end{pmatrix}.

Note that

f_{-}(\widetilde{r}_{+},\widetilde{r}_{-})=f_{+}(\widetilde{r}_{-},\widetilde{r}_{+}).

The following estimates hold true for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
,

\displaystyle\|f_{\pm}(\widetilde{r})\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d}\|\widetilde{r}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.13)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}f_{\pm}\|_{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d}\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s}.

(6.14)

Moreover, if
\widetilde{r}(-\varphi,-x)=\widetilde{r}(\varphi,x)=\widetilde{r}(\varphi-\vec{\jmath}x)
(i.e.
\widetilde{r}
is a reversible traveling wave), then

\displaystyle f_{\pm}(\widetilde{r})(a;-\varphi,-x)

\displaystyle=f_{\pm}(\widetilde{r})(a;\varphi,x),

(6.15)

\displaystyle\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad f_{\pm}(\widetilde{r})(a;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,x)

\displaystyle=f_{\pm}(\widetilde{r})(a;\varphi,x+y).

(6.16)

Then,

\mathbf{Q}^{-1}\mathcal{J}\big(\mathbf{M}_{\varepsilon\mathbf{Q}\widetilde{r}}-\mathbf{M}_{0}\big)\mathbf{Q}=-\varepsilon\partial_{x}\mathbf{F}-\varepsilon\widetilde{\mathbf{R}},

(6.17)

where

\mathbf{F}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}f_{+}(\widetilde{r})\cdot&0\\
0&f_{-}(\widetilde{r})\cdot\end{pmatrix},\qquad\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}=\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{R}_{+,0}^{(d)}&\widetilde{R}_{+,0}^{(o)}\vskip 5.69046pt\\
\widetilde{R}_{-,0}^{(o)}&\widetilde{R}_{-,0}^{(d)}\end{pmatrix},

(6.18)

with

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}_{+,0}^{(d)}

\displaystyle\triangleq\partial_{x}\Big(\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)f_{+}(\widetilde{r})+f_{+}(\widetilde{r})\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)+\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)f_{+}(\widetilde{r})\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)-\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)f_{-}(\widetilde{r})\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)\Big),

(6.19)

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}_{-,0}^{(d)}

\displaystyle\triangleq\partial_{x}\Big(\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)f_{-}(\widetilde{r})+f_{-}(\widetilde{r})\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)+\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)f_{-}(\widetilde{r})\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)-\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)f_{+}(\widetilde{r})\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)\Big),

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}_{+,0}^{(o)}

\displaystyle\triangleq\partial_{x}\Big(f_{+}(\widetilde{r})\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)-\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)f_{-}(\widetilde{r})-\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)f_{-}(\widetilde{r})\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)+\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)f_{+}(\widetilde{r})\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)\Big),

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}_{-,0}^{(o)}

\displaystyle\triangleq\partial_{x}\Big(f_{-}(\widetilde{r})\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)-\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)f_{+}(\widetilde{r})-\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)f_{+}(\widetilde{r})\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)+\mathtt{K}_{+}(a,D)f_{-}(\widetilde{r})\mathtt{K}_{-}(a,D)\Big).

Combining (
6.12
) and (
6.17
), recalling also the definition of
\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)
in (
4.3
), yields

\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}{\rm d}_{\widetilde{r}}\big(\mathcal{J}\nabla H(\widetilde{r})\big)=-{\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)-\varepsilon\partial_{x}\mathbf{F}-\varepsilon\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}.

Therefore, the linearized operator
\mathcal{L}^{(0)}
in (
6.5
) at a general state writes

\mathcal{L}^{(0)}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathbb{I}_{2}-\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}{\rm d}_{\widetilde{r}}\big(\mathcal{J}\nabla H(\widetilde{r})\big)=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathbb{I}_{2}+{\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)+\varepsilon\partial_{x}\mathbf{F}+\varepsilon\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}.

(6.20)

Actually, the previous expression is not well-adapted for our further reduction scheme. In the next proposition, we provide a more suitable expression, where the remainder is decomposed into a diagonal/off-diagonal homogeneous expansion up to smoothing pseudo-differential operators (see Definition
A.7
).

Proposition 6.2
.

For any
M\in\mathbb{N}^{*},
the linearized operator
\mathcal{L}^{(0)}
defined in (
6.20
) admits the decomposition

\mathcal{L}^{(0)}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathbb{I}_{2}+{\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)+\varepsilon\partial_{x}\mathbf{F}+\mathbf{R}_{0}^{(d)}+\mathbf{R}_{0}^{(o)}+\mathbf{S}_{0,M},

with the following properties:

(i)

The matrix pseudo-differential operator
\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)
is defined in (
4.3
);

(ii)

The multiplication matrix operator
{\bf F}
is defined in (
6.18
), with entries satisfying (
6.15
)-(
6.16
) and the estimates (
6.13
)-(
6.14
);

(iii)

The operators
\mathbf{R}_{0}^{(d)}
and
\mathbf{R}_{0}^{(o)}
admit an homogeneous expansion of degree
-1
up to order
1-M
(see Definition
A.7
-
(ii)
) in the form

\mathbf{R}_{0}^{(d)}=\begin{pmatrix}\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{M-1}\mathfrak{r}_{+,0,p}^{(d)}\partial_{x}^{-p}&0\\
0&\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{M-1}\mathfrak{r}_{-,0,p}^{(d)}\partial_{x}^{-p}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\mathbf{R}_{0}^{(o)}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{M-1}\mathfrak{r}_{+,0,p}^{(o)}\partial_{x}^{-p}\\
\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{M-1}\mathfrak{r}_{-,0,p}^{(o)}\partial_{x}^{-p}&0\end{pmatrix},

with for any
(\kappa,\nu,p)\in\{-,+\}\times\{d,o\}\times\llbracket 1,M-1\rrbracket,
the function
\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,0,p}^{(\nu)}=\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,0,p}^{(\nu)}(a;\varphi,x)\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,0,p}^{(\nu)}(a;-\varphi,-x)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{p+1}\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,0,p}^{(\nu)}(a;\varphi,x),

(6.21)

\displaystyle\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,0,p}^{(\nu)}(a;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,x)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,0,p}^{(\nu)}(a;\varphi,x+y).

(6.22)

In particular, the operators
\mathbf{R}_{0}^{(d)}
and
\mathbf{R}_{0}^{(o)}
are real, reversible and momentum preserving. Moreover, for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
,

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,0,p}^{(\nu)}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\|\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{0}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.23)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,0,p}^{(\nu)}\|_{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{0}(M)}

(6.24)

for some
\sigma_{0}(M)>0
;

(iv)

The operator
\mathbf{S}_{0,M}
is real, reversible, momentum preserving and in
OPS^{-M}
. It satisfies the following estimates for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{S}_{0,M}\|_{-M,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M,\alpha}\varepsilon\|\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{0}(\alpha,M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.25)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathbf{S}_{0,M}\|_{-M,s,\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M,\alpha}\varepsilon\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{0}(\alpha,M)}

(6.26)

for some
\sigma_{0}(\alpha,M)>0.

Proof.

The proof consists in a reformulation of the remainder term
\varepsilon\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}
given by (
6.18
)-(
6.19
). Prooceeding as in the proof of Lemma
3.1
-
(iv)
, we expand the symbol (
6.8
) using Taylor’s formulas and obtain, for any
M\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
\kappa\in\{-,+\},
a decomposition in the form

\mathtt{K}_{\kappa}(a,D)=\sum_{p=1}^{\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{M}}c_{\kappa,p}(a)\partial_{x}^{-2p}+S_{\kappa,M}(a,D),

(6.27)

where

\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{M}\triangleq\max\big\{p\in\mathbb{N}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\ \,-2p>-M\big\}=\begin{cases}\big\lfloor\tfrac{M}{2}\big\rfloor-1,&\textnormal{if }M\equiv 0\,\,[2],\\
\big\lfloor\tfrac{M}{2}\big\rfloor,&\textnormal{if }M\equiv 1\,\,[2],\end{cases}

each
c_{\kappa,p}(a)
is a constant depending on
\kappa,p
and smooth (rational) in
a
, and where the operator
S_{\kappa,M}(a,D)
is a Fourier multiplier in
OPS^{-M}.
With these decompositions in hand, one applies Proposition
A.3
-
(i)
and
Proposition
A.4
-
(i)
to (
6.19
) and writes, for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
and
\nu\in\{d,o\},

\varepsilon\widetilde{R}_{\kappa,0}^{(\nu)}=\sum_{p=1}^{M-1}\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,0,p}^{(\nu)}\partial_{x}^{-p}+S_{\kappa,0,M}^{(\nu)},

where the
\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,0,p}^{(\nu)}
are (modulo multiplicative constants) finite sum and products of
f_{+}(\widetilde{r}),f_{-}(\widetilde{r})
and their (space) derivatives up to a certain finite order depending on
M.
Therefore, there exists some
\sigma_{0}(M)>0
such that, using in particular (
6.13
)-(
6.14
) and (
6.2
),

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,0,p}^{(\nu)}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\max_{\kappa^{\prime}\in\{-,+\}}\|f_{\kappa^{\prime}}(\widetilde{r})\|_{s+\sigma_{0}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\|\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{0}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,0,p}^{(\nu)}\|_{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\max_{\kappa^{\prime}\in\{-,+\}}\|\Delta_{12}f_{\kappa^{\prime}}\|_{s+\sigma_{0}(M)}\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{0}(M)}.

The traveling and reversibility properties (
6.21
)-(
6.22
) are obtained after tedious computations from (
6.15
)-(
6.16
), (
6.19
) and (
6.27
), Proposition
A.4
-
(i)
. By applying Remark
A.1
, one readily has from (
6.19
), (
6.15
), (
6.16
) and the fact that
\mathtt{K}_{\pm}(a,D)
are reversibility and momentum preserving, that the remainders
\widetilde{R}_{\kappa,0}^{(\nu)}
is reversible and momentum preserving. By difference with what preceeds, so is
S_{\kappa,0,M}^{(\nu)}.
In addition, from (
A.46
), we obtain for some
\sigma_{0}(\alpha,M)>0
,

\displaystyle\|S_{\kappa,0,M}^{(\nu)}\|_{-M,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M,\alpha}\varepsilon\|\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{0}(\alpha,M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.28)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}S_{\kappa,0,M}^{(\nu)}\|_{-M,s,\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M,\alpha}\varepsilon\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{0}(\alpha,M)}.

(6.29)

Then, we define

\mathbf{S}_{0,M}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}S_{+,0,M}^{(d)}&S_{+,0,M}^{(o)}\vskip 5.69046pt\\
S_{-,0,M}^{(o)}&S_{-,0,M}^{(d)}\end{pmatrix}.

The estimates (
6.28
)-(
6.29
) give (
6.25
)-(
6.26
). This ends the proof of Proposition
6.2
.
∎

6.2
Block-decoupling up to sufficiently smoothing remainders

In this section, we transform the linearized operator
\mathcal{L}^{(0)}
described in Proposition
6.2
through an iterative procedure in order to make the off-diagonal operator
{\bf R}_{0}^{(o)}
being smoothing up to a large enough order
M\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
that may be chosen later, see (
6.179
).

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
M\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. There exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
small enough such that, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, there exist real, reversibility and momentum preserving matrix operators
\mathbf{X}_{0},\mathbf{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{X}_{M-1}
in the form

\mathbf{X}_{0}=0,\qquad\forall\,m\in\llbracket 1,M-1\rrbracket,\quad\mathbf{X}_{m}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathfrak{p}_{+,m}\partial_{x}^{-m-1}\\
\mathfrak{p}_{-,m}\partial_{x}^{-m-1}&0\end{pmatrix},

(6.30)

with, for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
and
m\in\llbracket 1,M-1\rrbracket,
the function
\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m}\triangleq\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m}(a,\omega;\varphi,x)\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m}(a,\omega;-\varphi,-x)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{m+1}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m}(a,\omega;\varphi,x),

(6.31)

\displaystyle\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m}(a,\omega;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,x)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m}(a,\omega;\varphi,x+y)

(6.32)

and the estimates, for some
\sigma_{m}(M)\triangleq\sigma_{m}(s_{0},q,d,M)>0
and for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
,

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\|\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{m}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.33)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m}\|_{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\Big(\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{m}(M)}+\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s_{0}+\sigma_{m}(M)}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|\widetilde{r}_{k}\|_{s+\sigma_{m}(M)}\Big),

(6.34)

such that the following holds. Denoting

\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{0}\triangleq\mathbb{I}_{2},\qquad\forall\,m\in\llbracket 1,M-1\rrbracket,\quad\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{m}\triangleq\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}+\mathbf{X}_{1}\big)\circ\ldots\circ\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}+\mathbf{X}_{m}\big),

(6.35)

we have, for any
m\in\llbracket 1,M-1\rrbracket
,

\mathcal{L}^{(m)}\triangleq\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{m}^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{(0)}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{m}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathbb{I}_{2}+{\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)+\varepsilon\partial_{x}\mathbf{F}+\mathbf{R}_{m}^{(d)}+\mathbf{R}_{m}^{(o)}+\mathbf{S}_{m,M},

(6.36)

with the following properties:

(i)

The operator
\mathbf{R}_{m}^{(d)}
admits an homogeneous expansion of degree
-1
up to order
1-M
in the form

\mathbf{R}_{m}^{(d)}=\begin{pmatrix}\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{M-1}\mathfrak{r}_{+,m,p}^{(d)}\partial_{x}^{-p}&0\\
0&\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{M-1}\mathfrak{r}_{-,m,p}^{(d)}\partial_{x}^{-p}\end{pmatrix},

(6.37)

with for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
and
p\in\llbracket 1,M-1\rrbracket,
the function
\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(d)}\triangleq\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(d)}(a,\omega;\varphi,x)\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(d)}(a,\omega;-\varphi,-x)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{p+1}\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(d)}(a,\omega;\varphi,x),

(6.38)

\displaystyle\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(d)}(a,\omega;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,x)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(d)}(a,\omega;\varphi,x+y).

(6.39)

In particular, the operator
\mathbf{R}_{m}^{(d)}
is real, reversible and momentum preserving. Moreover, for any
s\in[s_{0},S],

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(d)}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\|\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{m}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.40)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(d)}\|_{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\big(\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{m}(M)}+\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s_{0}+\sigma_{m}(M)}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|\widetilde{r}_{k}\|_{s+\sigma_{m}(M)}\big).

(6.41)

(ii)

The operator
\mathbf{R}_{m}^{(o)}
admits an homogeneous expansion of degree
-m-1
up to order
1-M
in the form

\mathbf{R}_{m}^{(o)}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\displaystyle\sum_{p=m+1}^{M-1}\mathfrak{r}_{+,m,p}^{(o)}\partial_{x}^{-p}\\
\displaystyle\sum_{p=m+1}^{M-1}\mathfrak{r}_{-,m,p}^{(o)}\partial_{x}^{-p}&0\end{pmatrix},

(6.42)

with for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
and
p\in\llbracket m+1,M-1\rrbracket,
the function
\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(o)}\triangleq\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(o)}(a,\omega;\varphi,x)\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(o)}(a,\omega;-\varphi,-x)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{p+1}\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(o)}(a,\omega;\varphi,x),

(6.43)

\displaystyle\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(o)}(a,\omega;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,x)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(o)}(a,\omega;\varphi,x+y).

(6.44)

In particular, the operator
\mathbf{R}_{m}^{(o)}
is real, reversible and momentum preserving. Moreover, for any
s\in[s_{0},S],

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(o)}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\|\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{m}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.45)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(o)}\|_{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\big(\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{m}(M)}+\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s_{0}+\sigma_{m}(M)}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|\widetilde{r}_{k}\|_{s+\sigma_{m}(M)}\big).

(6.46)

(iii)

The matrix operator
\mathbf{S}_{m,M}
is real, reversible, momentum preserving and in
OPS^{-M}
. It satisfies the following estimates, for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{S}_{m,M}\|_{-M,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M,\alpha}\varepsilon\|\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{m}(\alpha,M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.47)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathbf{S}_{m,M}\|_{-M,s,\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M,\alpha}\varepsilon\big(\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{m}(\alpha,M)}+\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s_{0}+\sigma_{m}(\alpha,M)}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|\widetilde{r}_{k}\|_{s+\sigma_{m}(\alpha,M)}\big)\,\,\,\,

(6.48)

for some
\sigma_{m}(\alpha,M)\triangleq\sigma_{m}(s_{0},q,d,\alpha,M)>0.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
m\in\llbracket 0,M-1\rrbracket
. The case
m=0
follows from Proposition
6.2
. Assume that the step
m\in\llbracket 0,M-2\rrbracket
is already settled and we have to prove the claims at the step
m+1.
Consider an a priori unknown invertible transformation
\phi_{m+1}
in the form

\Phi_{m+1}\triangleq\mathbb{I}_{2}+\mathbf{X}_{m+1},\qquad\mathbf{X}_{m+1}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathfrak{p}_{+,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}\\
\mathfrak{p}_{-,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}&0\end{pmatrix}.

(6.49)

Then, the following identity holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(m)}\Phi_{m+1}

\displaystyle=\Phi_{m+1}\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathbb{I}_{2}+{\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)+\varepsilon\partial_{x}\mathbf{F}+\mathbf{R}_{m}^{(d)}\big)

(6.50)

\displaystyle+\big[\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathbb{I}_{2}+{\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)+\varepsilon\partial_{x}\mathbf{F}\,,\,\mathbf{X}_{m+1}\big]+\mathbf{R}_{m}^{(o)}

\displaystyle+\big[\mathbf{R}_{m}^{(d)}\,,\,\mathbf{X}_{m+1}\big]+\mathbf{R}_{m}^{(o)}\mathbf{X}_{m+1}+\mathbf{S}_{m,M}\Phi_{m+1}.

Our purpose is to properly choose the operator
\mathbf{X}_{m+1}
to get rid of the anti-diagonal terms of order
-m-1
. Such terms appear in the second line in (
6.50
). One has

\displaystyle\big[\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathbb{I}_{2}+{\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)+\varepsilon\partial_{x}\mathbf{F},\mathbf{X}_{m+1}\big]=\begin{pmatrix}0&A_{+,m+1}\\
A_{-,m+1}&0\end{pmatrix},

where for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\},

\displaystyle A_{\kappa,m+1}

\displaystyle\triangleq[\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi},\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}]+\kappa[{\rm i}\Omega(a,D),\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}]_{\rm a}

(6.51)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\Big(\partial_{x}\,f_{\kappa}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}-\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-1}f_{-\kappa}\Big);

we recall the notations
[A,B]
and
[A,B]_{\rm a}
to respectively designate the commutator and anti-commutator between operators
A
and
B
, namely

[A,B]\triangleq AB-BA,\qquad[A,B]_{\rm a}\triangleq AB+BA.

We fix
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
and we separately analyze the contributions to
A_{\kappa,m+1}
in (
6.51
).

\blacktriangleright

Contribution from
[\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi},\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}]
.

A direct computation shows that

\displaystyle[\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi},\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}]=(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1})\partial_{x}^{-m-2}.

(6.52)

\blacktriangleright

Contribution from
[{\rm i}\Omega(a,D),\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}]_{\rm a}
.

Next, by the expansion in (
3.24
) (recalling also the notation in (
3.23
)), we write

\displaystyle[{\rm i}\Omega(a,D),\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}]_{\rm a}

\displaystyle=[a\partial_{x},\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}]_{\rm a}+\sum_{p=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{M}}\alpha_{p}a^{1-2p}[\partial_{x}^{1-2p},\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}]_{\rm a}

(6.53)

\displaystyle\quad+[S_{M}(a,D),\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}]_{\rm a}.

(6.54)

One readily has

\displaystyle[a\partial_{x},\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}]_{\rm a}=2a\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-1}+a(\partial_{x}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1})\partial_{x}^{-m-2}.

(6.55)

Now, from Proposition
A.4
-
(iii)
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{M}}\alpha_{p}a^{1-2p}[\partial_{x}^{1-2p},\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}]_{\rm a}

(6.56)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{M}}\alpha_{p}a^{1-2p}\bigg(2\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-1-2p}+\sum_{p_{2}=1}^{M-m-2p-2}C_{p_{2},1-2p}(\partial_{x}^{p_{2}}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1})\partial_{x}^{-m-1-2p-p_{2}}+\mathfrak{R}_{\kappa,M,p}\bigg),

(6.57)

where, for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
and any
\alpha\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\llbracket 1,\mathfrak{N}_{M}\rrbracket}\|\mathfrak{R}_{\kappa,M,p}\|_{-M,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M,\alpha}\|\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\|_{s+\sigma_{m+1}(\alpha,M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\llbracket 1,\mathfrak{N}_{M}\rrbracket}\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{R}_{\kappa,M,p}\|_{-M,s,\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M,\alpha}\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\|_{s+\sigma_{m+1}(\alpha,M)},

for some
\sigma_{m+1}(\alpha,M)>\sigma_{m}(\alpha,M).
We mention that in what follows, we may keep writing
\sigma_{m+1}(\alpha,M)
to denote a loss a derivatives at step
m+1
for the smoothing remainders. Hence, its value may change (increase) from one line to another but it still remains finite and independent of
s
.
Collecting (
6.55
), (
6.57
) into (
6.54
), we compactly write

\displaystyle[{\rm i}\Omega(a,D),\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}]_{\rm a}

\displaystyle=2a\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-1}+\sum_{p=m+2}^{M-1}\hat{\mathfrak{p}}_{\kappa,m+1,p}\partial_{x}^{-p}+\mathfrak{S}_{\kappa,M,1}

(6.58)

with
\hat{\mathfrak{p}}_{\kappa,m+1,p}
related to
\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}
and its derivatives up to a finite order depending on
M
, and with
\mathfrak{S}_{\kappa,M,1}
related to
\mathfrak{R}_{\kappa,M,p}
,
p\in\llbracket 1,\mathfrak{N}_{M}\rrbracket
, and
[S_{M}(a,D),\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}]_{\rm a}
. They satisfy

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\llbracket m+2,M-1\rrbracket}\|\hat{\mathfrak{p}}_{\kappa,m+1,p}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\|\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\|_{s+\sigma_{m+1}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\llbracket m+2,M-1\rrbracket}\|\Delta_{12}\hat{\mathfrak{p}}_{\kappa,m+1,p}\|_{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\|_{s+\sigma_{m+1}(M)},

with
\sigma_{m+1}(M)>\sigma_{m}(M)
which may also vary from line to line as
\sigma_{m+1}(\alpha,M)
. The remainder also satisfies

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{S}_{\kappa,M,1}\|_{-M,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M,\alpha}\|\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\|_{s+\sigma_{m+1}(\alpha,M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{S}_{\kappa,M,1}\|_{-M,s,\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M,\alpha}\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\|_{s+\sigma_{m+1}(\alpha,M)}.

\blacktriangleright

Contribution from
\partial_{x}\,f_{\kappa}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}-\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-1}f_{-\kappa}
.

We now analyze the last term in (
6.51
). Note that

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\,f_{\kappa}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}-\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-1}f_{-\kappa}

\displaystyle=\big(\partial_{x}(f_{\kappa}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1})\big)\partial_{x}^{-m-2}+f_{\kappa}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-1}

(6.59)

\displaystyle\quad-\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}f_{-\kappa}\partial_{x}^{-m-1}-\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}[\partial_{x}^{-m-1},f_{-\kappa}].

(6.60)

Using Proposition
A.4
-
(i)
, we write

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\,f_{\kappa}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-2}-\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-1}f_{-\kappa}

(6.61)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\big(f_{\kappa}-f_{-\kappa}\big)\partial_{x}^{-m-1}+\sum_{p=m+2}^{M-1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{\kappa,m+1,p}\partial_{x}^{-p}+\mathfrak{S}_{\kappa,M,2},

(6.62)

where
\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{\kappa,m+1,p}
is (up to multiplicative constants) sum and products of
f_{+},f_{-},\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}
and their derivatives up to a finite order depending on
M.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\llbracket m+2,M-1\rrbracket}\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{\kappa,m+1,p}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\max_{\kappa^{\prime}\in\{-,+\}}\|f_{\kappa^{\prime}}\|_{s+\sigma_{m+1}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{p}_{k,m+1}\|_{s+\sigma_{m+1}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\llbracket m+2,M-1\rrbracket}\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{\kappa,m+1,p}\|_{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\max_{\kappa^{\prime}\in\{-,+\}}\|\Delta_{12}f_{\kappa^{\prime}}\|_{s+\sigma_{m+1}(M)}+\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\|_{s+\sigma_{m+1}(M)},

and

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{S}_{\kappa,M,2}\|_{-M,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M,\alpha}\|\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\|_{s+\sigma_{m+1}(\alpha,M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{S}_{\kappa,M,2}\|_{-M,s,\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M,\alpha}\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\|_{s+\sigma_{m+1}(\alpha,M)}.

\blacktriangleright

Expansion of
A_{\kappa,m+1}
.

Combining the expansions (
6.52
), (
6.58
) and (
6.62
) into (
6.51
), we end up with

\displaystyle A_{\kappa,m+1}

\displaystyle=\big(2a\kappa+\varepsilon(f_{\kappa}-f_{-\kappa})\big)\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-1}

(6.63)

\displaystyle+(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1})\partial_{x}^{-m-2}+\sum_{p=m+2}^{M-1}(\kappa\hat{\mathfrak{p}}_{\kappa,m+1,p}+\varepsilon\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{\kappa,m+1,p})\partial_{x}^{-p}+\kappa\mathfrak{S}_{\kappa,M,1}+\varepsilon\mathfrak{S}_{\kappa,M,2}.

(6.64)

In order to get rid of the terms of order
\partial_{x}^{-m-1}
in (
6.50
), with
{\bf R}_{m}^{(o)}
as in (
6.42
), we make the following choice

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}(a,\omega;\varphi,x)\triangleq-\frac{\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,m+1}^{(o)}(a,\omega;\varphi,x)}{2a\kappa+\varepsilon\big(f_{\kappa}(\widetilde{r})(a,\omega;\varphi,x)-f_{-\kappa}(\widetilde{r})(a,\omega;\varphi,x)\big)}\cdot

(6.65)

By the induction hypotheses (
6.43
)-(
6.44
) together with (
6.15
)-(
6.16
), we get

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}(a,\omega;-\varphi,-x)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{m}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}(a,\omega;\varphi,x),

(6.66)

\displaystyle\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}(a,\omega;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,x)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}(a,\omega;\varphi,x+y).

(6.67)

This implies, by virtue of Lemma
A.4
, the reversibility and momentum preserving properties of
\mathbf{X}_{m+1}
in (
6.49
). Using product and composition laws of Lemma
A.1
, (
6.13
)-(
6.14
) and (
6.45
)-(
6.46
) together with (
6.2
) and (
5.4
) (with
\sigma_{\rm max}>\sigma_{m}(M)
), we get

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\|\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{m}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.68)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\|_{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\big(\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s+\sigma_{m}(M)}+\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{r}\|_{s_{0}+\sigma_{m}(M)}\max_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\|\widetilde{r}_{\ell}\|_{s+\sigma_{m}(M)}\big).

(6.69)

With the choice (
6.65
), we infer

\big[\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathbb{I}_{2}+{\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)+\varepsilon\partial_{x}\mathbf{F}\,,\,\mathbf{X}_{m+1}\big]+\mathbf{R}_{m}^{(o)}={\bf B}_{m+1}^{(o)}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}0&B_{+,m+1}\\
B_{-,m+1}&0\end{pmatrix},

(6.70)

where, for any
\kappa\in\{+,-\},
the operator
B_{\kappa,m+1}
is in
OPS^{-m-2}
and writes

\displaystyle B_{\kappa,m+1}\triangleq(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1})\partial_{x}^{-m-2}+\sum_{p=m+2}^{M-1}(\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,m,p}^{(o)}\partial_{x}^{-p}+\kappa\hat{\mathfrak{p}}_{\kappa,m+1,p}+\varepsilon\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{\kappa,m+1,p})\partial_{x}^{-p}+\kappa\mathfrak{S}_{\kappa,M,1}+\varepsilon\mathfrak{S}_{\kappa,M,2}.

(6.71)

According to the explicit expressions (
6.49
) and (
6.65
), then using (
A.28
), Proposition
A.3
-
(i)
-
(iii)
-
(iv)
together with (
6.68
), (
6.2
) and (
5.4
), we infer

\|\mathbf{X}_{m+1}\|_{-m-2,s_{0},0}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim_{q,d}\max_{\kappa\in\{-,+\}}\|\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m+1}\|_{s_{0}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim_{q,d}\varepsilon.

Thus, the map
\Phi_{m+1}=\mathbb{I}_{2}+\mathbf{X}_{m+1}
is also invertible by the Neumann series argument, satisfying

\displaystyle\Phi_{m+1}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}+\mathbf{X}_{m+1}\big)^{-1}=\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}-\mathbf{X}_{m+1}\big)\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}-\mathbf{X}_{m+1}^{2}\big)^{-1}

(6.72)

\displaystyle=\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}-\mathbf{X}_{m+1}^{2}\big)^{-1}-{\bf X}_{m+1}\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}-\mathbf{X}_{m+1}^{2}\big)^{-1}\,,

where
\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}-\mathbf{X}_{m+1}^{2}\big)^{-1}
is also well defined by means of Neumann series.
We set

\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{m+1}\triangleq\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{m}\circ\Phi_{m+1}=\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{m}\circ\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}+\mathbf{X}_{m+1}\big),\qquad\mathcal{L}^{(m)}\triangleq\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{m}^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{(0)}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{m}.

(6.73)

Therefore, by (
6.50
), (
6.70
) and (
6.73
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(m+1)}

\displaystyle\triangleq\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{m+1}^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}^{(0)}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{m+1}=\Phi_{m+1}^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{(m)}\Phi_{m+1}

(6.74)

\displaystyle=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathbb{I}_{2}+{\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)+\varepsilon\partial_{x}\mathbf{F}+{\bf R}_{m}^{(d)}+\Phi_{m+1}^{-1}\big({\bf B}_{m+1}^{(o)}+[{\bf R}_{m}^{(d)},{\bf X}_{m}]+{\bf R}_{m}^{(o)}{\bf X}_{m+1}+{\bf S}_{m,M}\Phi_{m+1}\big)

(6.75)

\displaystyle=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathbb{I}_{2}+{\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)+\varepsilon\partial_{x}\mathbf{F}+\mathbf{R}_{m+1}^{(d)}+\mathbf{R}_{m+1}^{(o)}+\mathbf{S}_{m+1,M},

(6.76)

where, by using (
6.72
) and (
6.49
) to split between the diagonal and off-diagonal contributions, and by expanding in homogeneinity up to order
1-M
, the operators
\mathbf{R}_{m+1}^{(d)},

\mathbf{R}_{m+1}^{(o)}
and
\mathbf{S}_{m+1,M}
are defined through the following relations

\displaystyle{\bf R}_{m+1}^{(d)}+{\widetilde{{\bf S}}}_{m+1,M}^{(d)}

\displaystyle\triangleq{\bf R}_{m}^{(d)}+\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}-\mathbf{X}_{m+1}^{2}\big)^{-1}{\bf R}_{m}^{(o)}{\bf X}_{m+1}-{\bf X}_{m+1}\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}-\mathbf{X}_{m+1}^{2}\big)^{-1}\big({\bf B}_{m+1}^{(o)}+[{\bf R}_{m}^{(d)},{\bf X}_{m}]\big),

(6.77)

\displaystyle{\bf R}_{m+1}^{(o)}+{\widetilde{{\bf S}}}_{m+1,M}^{(o)}

\displaystyle\triangleq\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}-\mathbf{X}_{m+1}^{2}\big)^{-1}\big({\bf B}_{m+1}^{(o)}+[{\bf R}_{m}^{(d)},{\bf X}_{m}]\big)-{\bf X}_{m+1}\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}-\mathbf{X}_{m+1}^{2}\big)^{-1}{\bf R}_{m}^{(o)}{\bf X}_{m+1},

(6.78)

\displaystyle{\bf S}_{m+1,M}

\displaystyle\triangleq{\widetilde{{\bf S}}}_{m+1,M}^{(d)}+{\widetilde{{\bf S}}}_{m+1,M}^{(o)}+\Phi_{m+1}^{-1}{\bf S}_{m,M}\Phi_{m+1},

(6.79)

with
{\bf R}_{m+1}^{(d)}
and
{\bf R}_{m+1}^{(o)}
satisfying the homogeneous expansions up to order
1-M
in (
6.37
) and (
6.42
) at the step
m+1
, respectively.
The symmetry relations (
6.38
)-(
6.39
), (
6.43
)-(
6.44
) at the step
m+1
and
{\bf S}_{m+1,M}
being real, reversible and momentum preserving follow by (
6.77
)-(
6.79
), (
6.70
), (
6.66
)-(
6.67
), Proposition
6.2
-
(i)
,
(ii)
, Remark
A.1
, Lemma
A.4
, Proposition
A.4
and the induction hypotheses on
{\bf R}_{m}^{(d)}
,
{\bf R}_{m}^{(o)}
,
{\bf S}_{m,M}
. The estimates (
6.40
)-(
6.41
), (
6.45
)-(
6.41
) and (
6.47
)-(
6.48
) at the step
m+1
follow by (
6.77
)-(
6.79
), (
6.70
), (
6.68
)-(
6.69
) and the induction hypotheses on
{\bf R}_{m}^{(d)}
,
{\bf R}_{m}^{(o)}
,
{\bf S}_{m,M}
. This concludes the proof of Proposition
6.3
.
∎

As a consequence of the previous proposition, we have the following result.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
M\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. There exists a real, reversibility and momentum preserving, invertible matrix operator
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}
satisfying the tame estimates for any
s\in[s_{0},S],

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\pm 1}-\mathbb{I}_{2}\|_{-2,s,0}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\sigma(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big),

(6.80)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}\|_{-2,s,0}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\big(\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s+\sigma(M)}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\sigma(M)}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{0}+\sigma(M)}\big)

(6.81)

for some
\sigma(M)>0
. In addition, denoting
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star}
the
\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T})
-adjoint of the transformation
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1},
we have that
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star}
is a real, reversibility and momentum preserving operator satisfying the following estimates for any
s\in[s_{0},S],

\displaystyle\|(\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star})^{\pm 1}-\mathbb{I}_{2}\|_{-2,s,0}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\sigma(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big),

(6.82)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}(\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star})^{\pm 1}\|_{-2,s,0}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\big(\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s+\sigma(M)}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\sigma(M)}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{0}+\sigma(M)}\big).

(6.83)

Moreover, we have

\mathcal{L}^{(M-1)}\triangleq\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{(0)}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathbb{I}_{2}+{\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)+\varepsilon\partial_{x}\mathbf{F}+\mathbf{R}_{[-1]}^{(d)}+\mathbf{S}_{M},

(6.84)

where
\mathbf{R}_{[-1]}^{(d)}
and
\mathbf{S}_{M}
are real, reversible and momentum preserving remainders. The operator
\mathbf{R}_{[-1]}^{(d)}
admits an homogeneous expansion of degree
-1
up to order
1-M
in the form

\mathbf{R}_{[-1]}^{(d)}=\begin{pmatrix}\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{M-1}\mathfrak{r}_{+,p}^{(d)}\partial_{x}^{-p}&0\\
0&\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{M-1}\mathfrak{r}_{-,p}^{(d)}\partial_{x}^{-p}\end{pmatrix},

(6.85)

with for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
and
p\in\llbracket 1,M-1\rrbracket,
the function
\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}\triangleq\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}(a,\omega;\varphi,x)\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}(a,\omega;-\varphi,-x)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{p+1}\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}(a,\omega;\varphi,x),

(6.86)

\displaystyle\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}(a,\omega;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,x)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}(a,\omega;\varphi,x+y).

(6.87)

Moreover, for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
and
s\in[s_{0},S],

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\llbracket 1,M-1\rrbracket}\|\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\sigma(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big),

(6.88)

\displaystyle\max_{p\in\llbracket 1,M-1\rrbracket}\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}\|_{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\big(\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s+\sigma(M)}+\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{0}}\max_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{\ell}\|_{s+\sigma(M)}\big)

(6.89)

for some
\sigma(M)\triangleq\sigma(s_{0},q,d,M)>0.

The matrix operator
\mathbf{S}_{M}
is in
OPS^{-M}
and satisfies the following estimates for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{S}_{M}\|_{-M,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M,\alpha}\varepsilon\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\sigma(\alpha,M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big),

(6.90)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathbf{S}_{M}\|_{-M,s,\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M,\alpha}\varepsilon\big(\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s+\sigma(\alpha,M)}+\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{0}}\max_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{\ell}\|_{s+\sigma(\alpha,M)}\big)

(6.91)

for some
\sigma(\alpha,M)\triangleq\sigma(s_{0},q,d,\alpha,M)>0.

Proof.

The claims follows directly from Proposition
6.3
, noting that, for
m=M-1
, the operator in (
6.36
) coincides with the one in (
6.84
), having
{\bf R}_{M-1}^{(o)}\equiv 0
and setting
{\bf R}_{[-1]}^{(d)}\triangleq{\bf R}^{(d)}_{M-1}
and
{\bf S}_{M}\triangleq{\bf S}_{M-1,M}
. In view of (
6.35
), the estimate (
6.80
) for
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}
is a consequence of Proposition
A.3
-
(i)
-
(iii)
-
(iv)
, (
6.30
), (
6.33
), (
6.2
) and (
5.4
) provided that
\sigma_{\rm max}
is chosen large enough. The analogous estimate for
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{-1}
follows with a similar argument making appeal to Neumann series. In addition, we note that, from (
6.35
), we have

\Delta_{12}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}=\sum_{m=1}^{M-1}\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}+\mathbf{X}_{1}(i_{1})\big)\circ\ldots\circ\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}+\mathbf{X}_{m-1}(i_{1})\big)\circ\Delta_{12}\mathbf{X}_{m}\circ\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}+\mathbf{X}_{m+1}(i_{2})\big)\circ\ldots\circ\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}+\mathbf{X}_{M-1}(i_{2})\big).

And one sees from (
6.30
) that

\Delta_{12}\mathbf{X}_{m}=\begin{pmatrix}0&(\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{p}_{+,m})\partial_{x}^{-m-1}\\
(\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{p}_{-,m})\partial_{x}^{-m-1}&0\end{pmatrix}.

Therefore, the estimate (
6.81
) follows from Proposition
A.3
-
(i)
-
(iii)
-
(iv)
, (
6.33
), (
6.34
), (
6.2
), (
6.3
) and (
5.4
) provided that
\sigma_{\rm max}
is chosen large enough. Let us now turn to the estimates for the adjoint. In view of (
6.35
), one has

\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star}=\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}+\mathbf{X}_{M-1}^{\star}\big)\circ\ldots\circ\big(\mathbb{I}_{2}+\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\star}\big).

A simple computation using the explicit expression (
6.30
) shows that for any
m\in\llbracket 1,M-1\rrbracket,

\mathbf{X}_{m}^{\star}=\begin{pmatrix}0&(-1)^{m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-1}\mathfrak{p}_{+,m}\\
(-1)^{m+1}\partial_{x}^{-m-1}\mathfrak{p}_{-,m}&0\end{pmatrix}.

From this explicit expression, one can invoke Proposition
A.3
-
(i)
-
(iii)
-
(iv)
, (
6.33
) and (
6.2
) to get

\|\mathbf{X}_{m}^{\star}\|_{-m-1,s,0}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim_{s,q,d}\max_{\kappa\in\{-,+\}}\|\mathfrak{p}_{\kappa,m}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim_{q,d}\varepsilon\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\sigma(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big).

The estimates (
6.82
) and (
6.83
) for
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star}
follow immediately proceeding as before for
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}
, with the estimates for the inverse adjoint obtained via Neumann series. Finally, the estimates (
6.88
)-(
6.89
) and (
6.90
)-(
6.91
) follow by (
6.40
)-(
6.41
) (setting
{\mathfrak{r}}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}\triangleq{\mathfrak{r}}_{\kappa,M-1,p}^{(d)}
for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
,
p=\llbracket 1,M-1\rrbracket
), (
6.47
)-(
6.48
) and Proposition
6.1
-
(i)
. This achieves the proof of Proposition
6.4
.
∎

6.3
Transport reduction and Egorov argument

In this section, we straighten the first order vector field
\omega\cdot\partial_{{\varphi}}\mathbb{I}_{2}+\big(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{smallmatrix}\big)a\partial_{x}+\varepsilon\partial_{x}{\bf F}
in the operator
\mathcal{L}^{(M-1)}
described in (
6.84
) to a constant coefficients one, up to an arbitrarily small operator. We achieve this by conjugating the operator
{\mathcal{L}}^{(M-1)}
with a diffeomorphism of the torus, which is determined in the same spirit as in
[
60
, Prop. 6.2]
. The conjugation of the terms expanded in homogeneity up to order
M-1
is computed via Egorov Theorem in Proposition
A.6
. We remark that new
-M
-smoothing remainders do not belong anymore to the class of pseudo-differential operators, but rather to a more general class of operators of
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-tame operators presented in Section
A.3.2
and first introduced in
[
17
]
.

First, we present the reduction of the transport operator.

Proposition 6.5
.

Fix also
\upsilon\in(0,\tfrac{1}{q+2}].
For any
(\mu_{2},s_{1},s_{h},\sigma_{1})
satisfying

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mu_{2}\geqslant 4\tau_{1}q+6\tau_{1}+3,&s_{1}\geqslant s_{0},\\
s_{h}\geqslant\max(s_{1},\tfrac{3}{2}\mu_{2}+s_{0}+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}+3),&\sigma_{1}\triangleq s_{0}+8\tau_{1}q+12\tau_{1}+12,\end{array}

(6.92)

there exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that, if (recalling (
6.188
))

\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}

(6.93)

and if (
5.4
) holds with
s_{0}+\sigma_{\rm max}>s_{h}+\sigma_{1},

then the following hold true:

(i)

There exist
\mathtt{c}_{\pm}\in W^{q,\gamma,\infty}(\mathcal{O},\mathbb{C})
satisfying, for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\},

\displaystyle\big\|(a,\omega)\mapsto\mathtt{c}_{\kappa}(a,\omega;i_{0})-\kappa a\big\|^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{q,d}\varepsilon,

(6.94)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathtt{c}_{\kappa}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d}\varepsilon\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{0}+\sigma_{1}};

(6.95)

(ii)

There exist
\beta_{\pm}\in W^{q,\gamma,\infty}(\mathcal{O},H^{s}({\mathbb{T}}))
and
{\widehat{\beta}}_{\pm}\in W^{q,\gamma,\infty}(\mathcal{O},H^{s}({\mathbb{T}}))
related to each other by the relation

x\mapsto z\triangleq x+\beta_{\pm}(a,\omega;{\varphi},x)\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad z\mapsto x\triangleq z+{\widehat{\beta}}_{\pm}(a,\omega;{\varphi},z)

(6.96)

satisfying

\displaystyle\beta_{\kappa}(a,\omega;-\varphi,-x)

\displaystyle=-\beta_{\kappa}(a,\omega;\varphi,x),

(6.97)

\displaystyle\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\beta_{\kappa}(a,\omega;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,x)

\displaystyle=\beta_{\kappa}(a,\omega;\varphi,x+y)

(6.98)

and

\displaystyle\widehat{\beta}_{\kappa}(a,\omega;-\varphi,-z)

\displaystyle=-\widehat{\beta}_{\kappa}(a,\omega;\varphi,z),

(6.99)

\displaystyle\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\widehat{\beta}_{\kappa}(a,\omega;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,z)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\beta}_{\kappa}(a,\omega;\varphi,x+z).

(6.100)

Moreover, the following estimates hold, for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\beta}_{\kappa}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\beta_{\kappa}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\sigma_{1}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big),

(6.101)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{s_{1}}+\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{s_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s_{1},q,d}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{1}+\sigma_{1}};

(6.102)

(iii)

For any
\kappa\in\{-,+\},
the transformation
\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}
, defined as

\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}\rho\triangleq\big(1+\partial_{x}\beta_{\kappa}\big)(\mathcal{B}_{\kappa}\rho),\qquad(\mathcal{B}_{\kappa}\rho)(a,\omega;\varphi,x)\triangleq\rho\big(a,\omega;\varphi,x+\beta_{\kappa}(a,\omega;\varphi,x)\big)

is invertible, with inverse given by

\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}^{-1}\rho=\big(1+\partial_{x}\widehat{\beta}_{\kappa}\big)(\mathcal{B}_{\kappa}^{-1}\rho),\qquad(\mathcal{B}_{\kappa}^{-1}\rho)(a,\omega;\varphi,x)\triangleq\rho\big(a,\omega;\varphi,x+\widehat{\beta}_{\kappa}(a,\omega;\varphi,x)\big).

Moreover, the maps
\mathcal{B}_{\kappa}^{\pm 1},{\mathscr{B}}_{\kappa}^{\pm 1}
are reversibility and momentum preserving, and satisfy, for any
s\in[s_{0},S]

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}_{\kappa}^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d}\|\rho\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\beta_{\kappa}\|_{s+\sigma_{1}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{s_{0}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.103)

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{B}_{\kappa}^{\pm 1}-\textnormal{Id})\rho\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|(\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}^{\pm 1}-\textnormal{Id})\rho\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d}\|\beta_{\kappa}\|_{s_{0}+\sigma_{1}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{s+1}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\beta_{\kappa}\|_{s+\sigma_{1}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{s_{0}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.104)

\displaystyle\|(\Delta_{12}\mathcal{B}_{\kappa}^{\pm 1})\rho\|_{s_{1}}+\|(\Delta_{12}\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}^{\pm 1})\rho\|_{s_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s_{1},q,d}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{1}+\sigma_{1}}\|\rho\|_{s_{1}+1};

(6.105)

(iv)

For any
(a,\omega)\in{\mathcal{O}}
belonging to the Borel set

\mathcal{O}_{\textnormal{\tiny{T}},\infty}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})\triangleq\bigcap_{\kappa\in\{-,+\}}\bigcap_{{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0)\}\atop\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0}}\bigg\{(a,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\big|\omega\cdot\ell+j\mathtt{c}_{\kappa}(a,\omega;i_{0})\big|>\frac{\gamma^{\upsilon}}{\langle\ell\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\bigg\},

(6.106)

the following identity holds, for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
,

\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}^{-1}\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{x}\big(\big[\kappa a+\varepsilon f_{\kappa}(\widetilde{r})\big]\cdot\big)\Big)\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\mathtt{c}_{\kappa}\partial_{x}.

(6.107)

Proof.

We apply
[
60
, Prop. 6.2]
to obtain the existence of
\mathtt{c}_{\pm}
,
\beta_{\pm}
satisfying the estimates (
6.94
), (
6.95
), (
6.101
), (
6.102
), the symmetry properties (
6.97
)-(
6.99
), and, for any
(a,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}_{\textnormal{\tiny{T}},\infty}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
as in (
6.106
) and for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\},
the conjugation (
6.107
) holds.
The only difference with
[
60
, Prop. 6.2]
is the momentum preserving properties (
6.98
), (
6.100
) for
\beta_{\kappa}
and
{\widehat{\beta}}_{\kappa}
. These conditions are easily obtained by the initial traveling wave property (
6.16
) and a slight modification of the iterative proof in the given reference to provide a quasi-periodic traveling wave at each step. Indeed, along the scheme we use the preservation of the momentum condition
\vec{j}\cdot\ell+j=0
inside the Cantor sets while solving the homological equation at each step, see (
6.106
) for the final Cantor set. The space-time projectors are replaced by the traveling projectors defined in (
A.8
). Note that the momentum condition also allows to modify the non-resonance lower bound in the Cantor set with respect to the given reference. This approach has been first developed in
[
14
, Appendix A]
.
∎

We are now ready to conjugate the full operator
{\mathcal{L}}^{(M-1)}
in (
6.84
).

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
M\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
and
(\mu_{2},s_{1})
as in (
6.92
) and assume the smallness condition (
6.93
) of Proposition
6.5
. We define the matrix transformation

\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}\mathscr{B}_{+}&0\\
0&\mathscr{B}_{-}\end{pmatrix}.

(6.108)

Then, in restriction to the Borel set
\mathcal{O}_{\textnormal{\tiny{T}},\infty}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
in (
6.106
), the following identity holds

\mathfrak{L}^{(0)}\triangleq\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{(M-1)}\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathbb{I}_{2}+{\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)+\begin{pmatrix}(\mathtt{c}_{+}-a)\partial_{x}&0\\
0&(\mathtt{c}_{-}+a)\partial_{x}\end{pmatrix}+\mathcal{R}_{[-1]}^{(d)}+\mathcal{S}_{M},

(6.109)

where:

(i)

The constants
{\mathtt{c}}_{\pm}
are as in Proposition
6.5
-
(i)
;

(ii)

The remainder
\mathcal{R}_{[-1]}^{(d)}
is real, reversible and momentum preserving. It admits an homogeneous expansion of degree
-1
up to order
1-M
in the form

\mathcal{R}_{[-1]}^{(d)}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{M-1}\mathfrak{d}_{+,p}^{(d)}\,\partial_{x}^{-p}&0\\
0&\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{M-1}\mathfrak{d}_{-,p}^{(d)}\,\partial_{x}^{-p}\end{pmatrix},

where, for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
and any
p\in\llbracket 1,M-1\rrbracket,
the function
\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}\triangleq\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}(a,\omega;\varphi,x)
satisfies the symmetry properties

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}(a,\omega;-\varphi,-x)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{p+1}\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}(a,\omega;\varphi,x),

(6.110)

\displaystyle\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}(a,\omega;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,x)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}(a,\omega;\varphi,x+y),

(6.111)

and the following estimates, for any
s\in[s_{0},S],

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big),

(6.112)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}\|_{s_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{1}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}(M)}

(6.113)

for some
\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}(M)>0;

(iii)

The operator
\mathcal{S}_{M}
is real, reversible and momentum preserving. Moreover, for any
l\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
, with
|l|\leqslant l_{0}
and any
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
with
n_{1}+n_{2}\leqslant M-(q+l_{0})-1
, the operator
\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}\mathcal{S}_{M}\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}
is
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-tame with tame constants satisfying, for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}\mathcal{S}_{M}\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}}(s)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}(M,l_{0})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big),

(6.114)

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}\partial_{{\varphi}}^{l}\Delta_{12}\mathcal{S}_{M}\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{H}^{s_{1}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{1}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}(M,l_{0})}

(6.115)

for some
\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}(M,l_{0})>0.

Proof.

According to (
6.84
) in Proposition
6.4
, (
6.107
) in Proposition
6.5
-
(iv)
and (
6.108
), we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{(M-1)}\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}

\displaystyle=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\mathbb{I}_{2}+\begin{pmatrix}\mathtt{c}_{+}\partial_{x}&0\\
0&\mathtt{c}_{-}\partial_{x}\end{pmatrix}+\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{-1}\bigg[{\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)-\begin{pmatrix}a\partial_{x}&0\\
0&-a\partial_{x}\end{pmatrix}\bigg]\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}

(6.116)

\displaystyle+\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{[-1]}^{(d)}\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}+\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{M}\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}.

Our purpose now is to study the conjugation effect on the lower order terms.

\blacktriangleright

Action on the negative orders of the equilibrium Fourier multiplier:
Observe, in view of (
4.3
) and (
3.28
), that

{\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)-\begin{pmatrix}a\partial_{x}&0\\
0&-a\partial_{x}\end{pmatrix}={\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{\textnormal{neg},M+1}(a,D)+\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}_{M+1}(a,D),

(6.117)

where

{\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{\textnormal{neg},M+1}(a,D)\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}{\rm i}\Omega_{\textnormal{neg},M+1}(a,D)&0\\
0&-{\rm i}\Omega_{\textnormal{neg},M+1}(a,D)\end{pmatrix},

with, recalling the notations (
3.23
)-(
3.24
),

{\rm i}\Omega_{\textnormal{neg},M+1}(a,D)\triangleq\sum_{p=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{M+1}}\alpha_{p}a^{1-2p}\partial_{x}^{1-2p}\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}_{M+1}(a,D)\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}S_{M+1}(a,D)&0\\
0&-S_{M+1}(a,D)\end{pmatrix}.

(6.118)

Applying Egorov’s Theorem (Proposition
A.6
), we get for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\},
and
p\in\{1,...,\mathfrak{N}_{M+1}\},

\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}^{-1}\circ\partial_{x}^{1-2p}\circ\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}=\sum_{k=0}^{M-2p}\mathfrak{p}_{1-2p-k}^{[p,\kappa]}\partial_{x}^{1-2p-k}+\mathfrak{R}_{M,p,\kappa}^{[1]},

(6.119)

with by virtue of Lemma
A.4
and Proposition
6.5
-
(iii)
, for any
k\in\llbracket 0.M-2p\rrbracket,
the functions
\mathfrak{p}_{1-2p-k}^{[p,\kappa]}\triangleq\mathfrak{p}_{1-2p-k}^{[p,\kappa]}(a,\omega;\varphi,x)
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{1-2p-k}^{[p,\kappa]}(a,\omega;-\varphi,-x)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}\mathfrak{p}_{1-2p-k}^{[p,\kappa]}(a,\omega;\varphi,x),

(6.120)

\displaystyle\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\mathfrak{p}_{1-2p-k}^{[p,\kappa]}(a,\omega;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,x)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{p}_{1-2p-k}^{[p,\kappa]}(a,\omega;\varphi,x+y),

(6.121)

and the operator
\mathfrak{R}_{M,p,\kappa}^{[1]}
is real, reversible and momentum preserving.
In addition, according to (
A.57
) and (
6.101
), for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
(here and in the sequel, the value of
\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}(M)>0
may increase from line to line),

\big\|\mathfrak{p}_{1-2p}^{[p,\kappa]}-1\big\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\sup_{k\in\llbracket 1,M-2p\rrbracket}\big\|\mathfrak{p}_{1-2p-k}^{[p,\kappa]}\big\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big)

(6.122)

and, by (
A.59
) and (
6.102
)

\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,M-2p\rrbracket}\big\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{p}_{1-2p-k}^{[p,\kappa]}\big\|_{s_{1}}\lesssim_{s_{1},q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{1}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}(M)}.

(6.123)

Moreover, from (
A.58
)-(
A.60
) and (
6.101
)-(
6.102
), one has for any
l\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|l|\leqslant l_{0}
and
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
with
n_{1}+n_{2}\leqslant M-(q+l_{0})+2p-1
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}\mathfrak{R}_{M,p,\kappa}^{[1]}\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}}(s)\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}(M,l_{0})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big),

(6.124)

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{R}_{M,p,\kappa}^{[1]}\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{H}^{s_{1}})}\lesssim_{s_{1},q,d,M}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{1}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}(M,l_{0})}

(6.125)

for some
\sigma(M,l_{0})>0.
Combining (
6.118
) and (
6.119
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}^{-1}{\rm i}\Omega_{\textnormal{neg},M+1}(a,D)\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{M+1}}\sum_{k=0}^{M-2p}\alpha_{p}a^{1-2p}\mathfrak{p}_{1-2p-k}^{[p,\kappa]}\partial_{x}^{1-2p-k}+\sum_{p=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{M+1}}\alpha_{p}a^{1-2p}\mathfrak{R}_{M,p,\kappa}^{[1]}

(6.126)

\displaystyle={\rm i}\Omega_{\textnormal{neg},M+1}(a,D)+\sum_{p=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{M+1}}\alpha_{p}a^{1-2p}\big(\mathfrak{p}_{1-2p}^{[p,\kappa]}-1\big)\partial_{x}^{1-2p}

\displaystyle+\sum_{p=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{M+1}}\sum_{k=1}^{M-2p}\alpha_{p}a^{1-2p}\mathfrak{p}_{1-2p-k}^{[p,\kappa]}\partial_{x}^{1-2p-k}+\sum_{p=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{M+1}}\alpha_{p}a^{1-2p}\mathfrak{R}_{M,p,\kappa}^{[1]}.

\blacktriangleright

Action on the diagonal remainder term:
Using (
6.108
) and (
6.85
), we write

\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{[-1]}^{(d)}\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}=\begin{pmatrix}\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{M-1}\mathscr{B}_{+}^{-1}\mathfrak{r}_{+,p}\partial_{x}^{-p}\mathscr{B}_{+}&0\\
0&\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{M-1}\mathscr{B}_{-}^{-1}\mathfrak{r}_{-,p}\partial_{x}^{-p}\mathscr{B}_{-}\end{pmatrix}.

(6.127)

Employing one more time Egorov’s Theorem in Proposition
A.6
, we get for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
and any
p\in\llbracket 1,M-1\rrbracket,

\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}^{-1}\mathfrak{r}_{\kappa,p}\partial_{x}^{-p}\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}=\sum_{p_{2}=0}^{M-1-p}\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}_{\kappa,p,p_{2}}\partial_{x}^{-p-p_{2}}+\mathfrak{R}_{M,p,\kappa}^{[2]},

(6.128)

where, according to (
A.57
), (
6.101
), (
6.88
) and (
A.59
), (
6.102
), (
6.89
), we have for any
s\in[s_{0},S],

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}_{k,p,0}-\mathfrak{r}_{k,p}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\max_{p_{2}\in\llbracket 1,M-1-p\rrbracket}\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}_{k,p,p_{2}}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big),

(6.129)

\displaystyle\max_{p_{2}\in\llbracket 0,M-1-p\rrbracket}\|\Delta_{12}\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}_{k,p,p_{2}}\|_{s_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s_{1},q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{1}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}(M)}.

(6.130)

In addition, by virtue of (
6.86
)-(
6.87
), Lemma
A.4
and Proposition
6.5
-
(iii)
, for any
p_{2}\in\llbracket 0,M-1-p\rrbracket,
the function
\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}_{\kappa,p,p_{2}}^{(d)}\triangleq\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}_{\kappa,p,p_{2}}^{(d)}(a,\omega;\varphi,x)\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}_{\kappa,p,p_{2}}(a,\omega;-\varphi,-x)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{p+p_{2}+1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}_{\kappa,p,p_{2}}(a,\omega;\varphi,x),

(6.131)

\displaystyle\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}_{\kappa,p,p_{2}}(a,\omega;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,x)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}_{\kappa,p,p_{2}}(a,\omega;\varphi,x+y),

(6.132)

and the operator
\mathfrak{R}_{M,p,\kappa}^{[2]}
is real, reversible and momentum preserving.
Moreover, from (
A.58
), (
A.60
), (
6.88
), (
6.89
), (
6.101
) and (
6.102
) for any
l\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|l|\leqslant l_{0}
and
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
with
n_{1}+n_{2}\leqslant M-(q+l_{0})+p
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}\mathfrak{R}_{M,p,\kappa}^{[2]}\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}}(s)\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}(M,l_{0})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big),

(6.133)

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{R}_{M,p,\kappa}^{[2]}\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{H}^{s_{1}})}\lesssim_{s_{1},q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{1}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{0}(M,l_{0})}.

(6.134)

\blacktriangleright

Conclusion:

The decomposition (
6.109
) is obtained by putting together (
6.116
), (
6.126
), (
6.127
) and (
6.128
), setting, for
\kappa\in\{-,+\},

\displaystyle\sum_{p=1}^{M-1}\mathfrak{d}_{\kappa,p}^{(d)}\partial_{x}^{-p}

\displaystyle\triangleq\sum_{p=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{M+1}}\alpha_{p}a^{1-2p}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{1-2p}^{[p,\kappa]}-1\right)\partial_{x}^{1-2p}+\sum_{p=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{M+1}}\sum_{k=1}^{M-2p}\alpha_{p}a^{1-2p}\mathfrak{p}_{1-2p-k}^{[p,\kappa]}\partial_{x}^{1-2p-k}

(6.135)

\displaystyle+\sum_{p_{2}=0}^{M-1-p}\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}_{\kappa,p,p_{2}}\partial_{x}^{-p-p_{2}}

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{M}

\displaystyle\triangleq\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{M}\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}+\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{R}_{M,+}&0\\
0&\mathfrak{R}_{M,-}\end{pmatrix},

(6.136)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{M,\kappa}

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}^{-1}S_{M+1}(a,D)\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}-S_{M+1}(a,D)+\sum_{p=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{M+1}}\alpha_{p}a^{1-2p}\mathfrak{R}_{M,p,\kappa}^{[1]}+\sum_{p=1}^{M-1}\mathfrak{R}_{M,p,\kappa}^{[2]}.

(6.137)

Putting together (
6.135
), (
6.120
), (
6.121
), (
6.131
) and (
6.132
), we obtain (
6.110
)-(
6.111
). The operator
\mathcal{S}_{M}
is real, reversible and momentum preserving by virtue of (
6.136
)-(
6.137
), Lemma
3.1
-(iv), Proposition
6.5
-
(iii)
and because
\mathfrak{R}_{M,p,\kappa}^{[1]},\mathfrak{R}_{M,p,\kappa}^{[2]}
are real reversible and momentum preserving.
According to the definition (
6.135
), the estimates (
6.112
)-(
6.113
) follow from (
6.122
)-(
6.123
) and (
6.129
)-(
6.130
).
Now, we shall obtain the estimates (
6.114
)-(
6.115
). First observe that

\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}^{-1}S_{M+1}(a,D)\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}-S_{M+1}(a,D)=(\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}^{-1}-\textnormal{Id})S_{M+1}(a,D)\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}+S_{M+1}(a,D)(\mathscr{B}_{\kappa}-\textnormal{Id}).

Moreover, for any
l\in{\mathbb{N}}^{d}
with
|l|\leqslant l_{0}
and any
n_{1}+n_{2}+l_{0}+q\leqslant M
, we have by Leibniz’s rule

\displaystyle\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{-1}\boldsymbol{S}_{M}\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}=\sum_{(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3})\in(\mathbb{N}^{d})^{3}\atop|l_{1}|+|l_{2}|+|l_{3}|=|l|}c_{l_{1},l_{2},l_{3}}\big(\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l_{1}}\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{-1}\langle D\rangle^{-n_{1}}\big)\big(\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l_{2}}\mathbf{S}_{M}\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}\big)\big(\langle D\rangle^{-n_{2}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l_{3}}\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}\big).

The
c_{l_{1},l_{2},l_{3}}
are real constants coming from Leibniz’s formula. Note that the estimate (
6.104
) loses one derivative in the test function and that is the reason why we had to push the expansion of the equilibrium symbol up to order
M+1
in (
6.117
). With this in hand, it suffices to combine (
6.136
), (
6.137
), (
6.103
), (
6.90
), Proposition
A.3
-
(v)
, (
6.104
), (
6.101
), (
6.124
) and (
6.133
) to get (
6.114
). Similarly, the estimate (
6.105
) also loses one derivative in the test function. Therefore, for
n_{1}+n_{2}+l_{0}+q+1\leqslant M
, the estimate (
6.115
) follows by an analogous argument from (
6.136
), (
6.137
), (
6.91
), (
6.103
), (
6.101
), (
6.105
), (
6.124
), (
6.125
), (
6.133
) and (
6.134
). This concludes the proof of Proposition
6.6
.
∎

6.4
Reduction of the operator
\mathscr{L}_{\omega}

The purpose of this section is to study the action of the previous transformations on of the linear operator
\mathscr{L}_{\omega}
in (
6.4
), acting only on the normal directions.
Recall that in Sections
6.2
,
6.3
, the linear operator
\mathcal{L}^{(0)}
in Proposition
6.2
has been conjugated, under the real, reversibility and momentum preserving map

\mathscr{M}\triangleq\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}},

(6.138)

for any
(a,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}_{\textnormal{\tiny{T}},\infty}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
as in (
6.106
), into the real, reversible and momentum preserving operator

\mathfrak{L}^{(0)}\triangleq\mathscr{M}^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{(0)}\mathscr{M}=\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{-1}\mathcal{L}^{(0)}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}},

(6.139)

where the maps
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}
,
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}
are defined respectively in Proposition
6.4
and Proposition
6.6
. The operator
\mathfrak{L}^{(0)}
is defined for any
(a,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}
. As we shall discuss now, a similar conjugation result holds for the projected operator
\mathscr{L}_{\omega}
in (
6.4
), acting in the normal subspace
\mathbf{H}_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}
. For this aim, we introduce the map

\mathscr{M}_{\perp}\triangleq\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{M}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}.

(6.140)

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
M\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
s_{1}
as in (
6.92
). There exists
\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}(M)\triangleq\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}(q,d,M)>0
such that, assuming (
5.4
) with
s_{0}+\sigma_{\rm max}\geqslant s_{1}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}(M)
, the following holds.

(i)

The map
\mathscr{M}
in (
6.138
) is a real, reversibility and momentum preserving invertible transformation satisfying the following estimates, for all
s\in[s_{0},S]
,

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{M}^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\|\rho\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{s_{0}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.141)

\displaystyle\|(\mathscr{M}^{\pm 1}-\mathbb{I}_{2})\rho\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\big(\|\rho\|_{s+1}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{s_{0}+1}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big),

(6.142)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathscr{M}^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{s_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s_{1},q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{1}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}(M)}\|\rho\|_{s_{1}+1};

(6.143)

(ii)

The map
\mathscr{M}_{\perp}
in (
6.140
) is a real, reversibility and momentum preserving invertible transformation satisfying the following estimates, for all
s\in[s_{0},S]
,

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\|\rho\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{s_{0}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.144)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{s_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s_{1},q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{1}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}(M)}\|\rho\|_{s_{1}+1}.

(6.145)

Proof.

(i)
The map
\mathscr{M}
is real, reversibility and momentum preserving because
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}
are, see (
6.138
), Proposition
6.4
and Proposition
6.5
-
(iii).
The estimates (
6.141
) follow from (
6.138
), (
6.80
), (
6.103
), (
6.101
) and (
5.4
). By virtue of (
6.138
), (
6.35
) and (
6.108
), we write

\mathscr{M}-\mathbb{I}_{2}=(\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}-\mathbb{I}_{2})\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}+(\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}-\mathbb{I}_{2}),\qquad\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}-\mathbb{I}_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathscr{B}_{+}-\textnormal{Id}&0\\
0&\mathscr{B}_{-}-\textnormal{Id}\end{pmatrix}.

(6.146)

Therefore, the estimate (
6.142
) for
\mathscr{M}
follows from (
6.146
), (
6.80
), Proposition
A.3
-
(v)
, (
6.104
) and (
6.101
). The inverse is given by

\mathscr{M}^{-1}=\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{-1}=\mathbb{I}_{2}+(\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{-1}-\mathbb{I}_{2})\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{-1}+(\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{-1}-\mathbb{I}_{2}),\qquad\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{-1}-\mathbb{I}_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathscr{B}_{+}^{-1}-\textnormal{Id}&0\\
0&\mathscr{B}_{-}^{-1}-\textnormal{Id}\end{pmatrix}.

(6.147)

Therefore, the estimate (
6.142
) for
\mathscr{M}^{-1}
follows from (
6.147
), (
6.80
), Proposition
A.3
-
(v)
, (
6.104
) and (
6.101
). Besides, from (
6.146
), we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}\mathscr{M}

\displaystyle=(\Delta_{12}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1})\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}(i_{2})+\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}(i_{1})(\Delta_{12}\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}),

(6.148)

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}\mathscr{M}^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\Delta_{12}\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{-1})\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{-1}(i_{2})+\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}(i_{1})(\Delta_{12}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{-1}).

(6.149)

Thus, the estimates (
6.143
) follow from (
6.148
), (
6.149
), (
6.80
), (
6.81
), Proposition
A.3
-
(v)
, (
6.103
), (
6.101
), (
6.105
) and (
5.4
).

(ii)
By definition, (
6.140
) the map
\mathscr{M}_{\perp}
is a real, reversibility and momentum preserving because
\mathscr{M}
is. The estimate (
6.144
) for
\mathscr{M}_{\perp}
is a consequence of (
6.80
), Proposition
A.3
-
(v)
and (
6.103
). Then, writing

\Delta_{12}\mathscr{M}_{\perp}=\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}(\Delta_{12}\mathscr{M})\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp},

the estimate (
6.145
) for
\mathscr{M}_{\perp}
is obtained from (
6.143
). Regarding the same estimates for
\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}
, it is a bit more involved. For this, we easily adapt
[
57
, Lemma 9.6]
to our matrix case, allowing us to prove that
\mathscr{M}_{\perp}
is indeed invertible with inverse explicitly given by

\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}\rho=\mathscr{M}^{-1}\rho-\sum_{m\in\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\big\langle\rho\,,\,\big((\mathscr{M}^{\star})^{-1}-\mathbb{I}_{2}\big)g_{m}\big\rangle_{\mathbf{L}_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\mathscr{M}^{-1}\vec{e}_{m},

(6.150)

where
\mathscr{M}^{\star}
is the
\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T})
-adjoint of
\mathscr{M},
for any
m\in\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0},

\displaystyle\forall\,x\in\mathbb{T},\quad\vec{e}_{m}(x)\triangleq\begin{cases}\big(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\big)e^{{\rm i}mx},&\textnormal{if }m\in\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{+},\\
\big(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\big)e^{{\rm i}mx},&\textnormal{if }m\in\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{-}\end{cases}

and under the notation

\mathbf{M}\triangleq\Big(\big\langle\vec{e}_{m},(\mathscr{M}^{\star})^{-1}\vec{e}_{k}\big\rangle_{\mathbf{L}_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\Big)_{(m,k)\in\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}^{2}},\qquad\mathbf{M}^{-1}\triangleq\big(\alpha_{m,k}\big)_{(m,k)\in\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}^{2}},

we have

g_{m}(a,\omega;\varphi,x)\triangleq\sum_{k\in\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\overline{\alpha_{m,k}(a,\omega;\varphi)}\vec{e}_{k}(x).

In view of (
6.138
), we have

\displaystyle(\mathscr{M}^{\star})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star})^{-1}=(\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star})^{-1}(\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{\star})^{-1}=(\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}.

We have used Lemma
A.5
-
(iii)
to write that

(\boldsymbol{\mathscr{B}}^{\star})^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathscr{B}_{+}^{\star}&0\\
0&\mathscr{B}_{-}^{\star}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{B}_{+}^{-1}&0\\
0&\mathcal{B}_{-}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{B}_{+}&0\\
0&\mathcal{B}_{-}\end{pmatrix}\triangleq\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}.

Therefore, we can write

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}^{\star}=\mathbb{I}_{2}+(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}^{-1}-\mathbb{I}_{2})\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star}+(\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star}-\mathbb{I}_{2}),

(6.151)

\displaystyle(\mathscr{M}^{\star})^{-1}=\mathbb{I}_{2}+\big((\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star})^{-1}-\mathbb{I}_{2}\big)\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}+(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}-\mathbb{I}_{2})

(6.152)

and

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}\mathscr{M}^{\star}

\displaystyle=(\Delta_{12}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}^{-1})\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star}(i_{2})+\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}^{-1}(i_{1})(\Delta_{12}\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star}),

(6.153)

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}(\mathscr{M}^{\star})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\big(\Delta_{12}(\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star})^{-1}\big)\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}^{-1}(i_{2})+(\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{M-1}^{\star})^{-1}(i_{1})(\Delta_{12}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}).

(6.154)

Similarly to the point
(i),
using (
6.82
), (
6.83
), Proposition
A.3
-
(v)
, (
6.103
), (
6.104
), (
6.105
) and (
5.4
), we find for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
,

\displaystyle\|((\mathscr{M}^{\star})^{\pm 1}-\mathbb{I}_{2})\rho\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\big(\|\rho\|_{s+1}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{s_{0}+1}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big),

(6.155)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}(\mathscr{M}^{\star})^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{s_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s_{1},q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{1}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}(M)}\|\rho\|_{s_{1}+1}

(6.156)

for some
\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}(M)>0.
As a consequence, the matrices
\mathbf{M}
and
\mathbf{M}^{-1}
are perturbations of the identity matrix. Quantitatively, the
\alpha_{m,k}
satisfy the following estimates for any
s\in[s_{0},S]

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{m,k}-\delta_{m,k}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}(M)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big),

(6.157)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\alpha_{m,k}\|_{s_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s_{1},q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{1}+\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}(M)}.

(6.158)

Consequently, the estimate (
6.144
) for
\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}
follows from (
6.150
), (
6.141
), (
6.155
) and (
6.157
). Also, from (
6.150
), we can write

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}\rho

\displaystyle=\Delta_{12}\mathscr{M}^{-1}\rho-\sum_{m\in\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\big\langle\rho\,,\,\Delta_{12}(\mathscr{M}^{\star})^{-1}g_{m}\big\rangle_{\mathbf{L}_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\mathscr{M}^{-1}(i_{2})\vec{e}_{m}

(6.159)

\displaystyle-\sum_{m\in\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\big\langle\rho\,,\,\big((\mathscr{M}^{\star})^{-1}(i_{1})-\mathbb{I}_{2}\big)g_{m}\big\rangle_{\mathbf{L}_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\Delta_{12}\mathscr{M}^{-1}\vec{e}_{m}.

Hence, the estimate (
6.145
) for the inverse is obtained combining (
6.159
), (
6.141
), (
6.143
), (
6.155
), (
6.156
) and (
6.158
). This ends the proof of Lemma
6.1
.
∎

Now, let us consider the operator
\mathscr{L}_{\perp}
defined by

\mathscr{L}_{\perp}\triangleq\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}\mathscr{L}_{\omega}\mathscr{M}_{\perp},

(6.160)

with
\mathscr{M}_{\perp}
as in (
6.140
). The next proposition provides the structure of the operator
\mathscr{L}_{\perp}
in (
6.160
).

Proposition 6.7
.

(Reduction of
\mathscr{L}_{\omega}
up to smoothing operators)
. Let
M\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
s_{1}
as in (
6.92
). The operator
\mathscr{L}_{\perp}
in (
6.160
) is well-defined for the whole set of parameters
\mathcal{O},
real, reversible and momentum preserving. Moreover, in restriction to the set
\mathcal{O}_{\textnormal{\tiny{T}},\infty}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
in (
6.106
), the operator
\mathscr{L}_{\omega}
has the form

\mathscr{L}_{\perp}\triangleq\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}+{\rm i}\,\mathscr{D}^{[0]}+\mathscr{R}^{[0]},

(6.161)

with the following properties:

(i)

The operator
\mathscr{D}^{[0]}
is diagonal, real, reversible and momentum preserving of the form

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}^{[0]}=\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{D}^{[0]}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathscr{D}_{+}^{(0)}&0\\
0&\mathscr{D}_{-}^{(0)}\end{pmatrix},

(6.162)

where, for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
, the operator
\mathscr{D}_{\kappa}^{[0]}=\Pi_{\kappa}^{\perp}\mathscr{D}_{\kappa}^{[0]}\Pi_{\kappa}^{\perp}
is the diagonal operator

\mathscr{D}_{\kappa}^{[0]}\triangleq\underset{j\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa}}{{\rm diag}}\big\{\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[0]}\big\},\qquad\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[0]}(a,\omega;i_{0})\triangleq\kappa\Omega_{j}(a)+\big(\mathtt{c}_{\kappa}(a,\omega;i_{0})-\kappa a\big)j\in{\mathbb{R}},

(6.163)

with
{\mathtt{c}}_{\kappa}(a,\omega;i_{0})
satisfying the estimates (
6.94
)-(
6.95
) in Proposition
6.6
;

(ii)

The operator
\mathscr{R}^{[0]}=\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}^{[0]}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}
is real, reversible and momentum preserving. Moreover, for any
l_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
M\geqslant q+l_{0}+2
, there exists a constant
\aleph(M,l_{0})\triangleq\aleph(q,d,M,l_{0})>0
such that, assuming (
3.17
) with
s_{0}+\sigma_{\rm max}\geqslant s_{1}+\aleph(M,l_{0})
,
and for any
l\in{\mathbb{N}}^{d}
,
|l|\leqslant l_{0}
, the operators
\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}\mathscr{R}^{[0]}\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}[\partial_{{\varphi}}^{l}\mathscr{R}^{[0]},\partial_{x}]\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}
are
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-tame with tame constants satisfying, for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
,
