\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}\mathscr{R}^{[0]}\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}(s),\,\mathfrak{M}_{\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}[\partial_{{\varphi}}^{l}\mathscr{R}^{[0]},\partial_{x}]\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}(s)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s,q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\aleph(M,l_{0})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big).

(6.164)

Furthermore, for any
l\in{\mathbb{N}}^{d}
,
|l|\leqslant l_{0}
,

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}\Delta_{12}\mathscr{R}^{[0]}\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{H}_{\perp}^{s_{1}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s_{1},q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{1}+\aleph(M,l_{0})},

(6.165)

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}[\partial_{\varphi}^{l}\Delta_{12}\mathscr{R}^{[0]},\partial_{x}]\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{H}_{\perp}^{s_{1}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s_{1},q,d,M}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{1}+\aleph(M,l_{0})}.

Proof.

In view of (
6.160
), (
6.4
). (
6.140
), using the facts that

\mathbb{I}_{2}=\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}+\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp},\qquad(\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp})^{2}=\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp},\qquad\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}=\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}=0

and that from Propositions
6.4
and
6.6
, in restriction to the set
\mathcal{O}_{\textnormal{\tiny{T}},\infty}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
, the following holds

\mathcal{L}^{(0)}\mathscr{M}=\mathscr{M}\mathfrak{L}^{(0)},

we find

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\perp}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\big({\mathcal{L}}^{(0)}-\varepsilon\partial_{x}{\mathcal{R}}\big)\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{M}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}

(6.166)

\displaystyle=\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}{\mathcal{L}}^{(0)}\mathscr{M}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}-\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}^{(0)}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\mathscr{M}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}-\varepsilon\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{x}{\mathcal{R}}\mathscr{M}_{\perp}

(6.167)

\displaystyle=\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{M}{\mathfrak{L}}^{(0)}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}-\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}^{(0)}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}(\mathscr{M}-\mathbb{I}_{2})\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}-\varepsilon\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{x}{\mathcal{R}}\mathscr{M}_{\perp}

(6.168)

\displaystyle=\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{M}(\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}+\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}){\mathfrak{L}}^{(0)}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}-\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}^{(0)}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}(\mathscr{M}-\mathbb{I}_{2})\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}-\varepsilon\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{x}{\mathcal{R}}\mathscr{M}_{\perp}

(6.169)

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathfrak{L}^{(0)}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{R}_{\rm f},

(6.170)

where
\mathscr{R}_{\rm f}
is the infinitely smooth operator

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{\rm f}

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}(\mathscr{M}-\mathbb{I}_{2})\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\mathfrak{L}^{(0)}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}-\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathcal{L}^{(0)}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}(\mathscr{M}-\mathbb{I}_{2})\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}-\varepsilon\mathscr{M}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\partial_{x}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{M}_{\perp}.

(6.171)

The operator
{\mathscr{R}}_{\rm f}
in (
6.171
) is
infinitely smoothing due to the presence of the finite dimensional projection operator
\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}
in the first two terms (finite rank contributions, therefore infinitely smoothing) and since
{\mathcal{R}}
in Proposition
6.1
-
(ii)
is a matrix integral operator with smooth kernels.
Let
l_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
and
M\geqslant q+l_{0}+1
. There exists
\aleph(M,l_{0})\triangleq\aleph(q,d,M,l_{0})>0
such that, for any
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, with
n_{1}+n_{2}\leqslant M-(q+l_{0})-1
and any
l\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
, with
|l|\leqslant l_{0}
, the operator
\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}\mathscr{R}_{\rm f}\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}
is
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-tame, with a tame constant satisfying, for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
and any
s_{1}
as in (
6.92
),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}\mathscr{R}_{\rm f}\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}}(s)\lesssim_{s,q,d,M,l_{0}}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\big(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{s+\aleph(M,l_{0})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big),

(6.172)

\displaystyle\|\langle D\rangle^{m_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}\Delta_{12}\mathscr{R}_{\rm f}\langle D\rangle^{m_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{H}_{\perp}^{s_{1}})}\lesssim_{s_{1},q,d,M,l_{0}}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{1}+\aleph(M,l_{0})}.

(6.173)

The estimate (
6.172
) follows by (
6.171
), (
6.140
), (
6.138
), (
6.109
), Lemma
6.1
and Proposition
6.1
-
(ii)
. The estimate (
6.173
) follows similarly by using (
6.142
), (
6.143
), (
6.144
), (
6.145
), Propositions
6.2
and
6.6
. Finally, the fact that
{\mathscr{R}}_{\rm f}
is real, reversible and momentum preserving follows by Lemma
6.1
, Proposition
6.6
, Proposition
6.2
and Proposition
6.1
-
(ii)
.
Combining (
6.109
) and (
6.170
) we get, in restriction to the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\textnormal{\tiny{T}},\infty}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
in (
6.106
),

\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathfrak{L}^{(0)}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{R}_{\rm f}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}+{\rm i}\,\mathscr{D}^{[0]}+\mathscr{R}^{[0]},

where

{\rm i}\,\mathscr{D}^{[0]}\triangleq\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\bigg({\rm i}\boldsymbol{\Omega}(a,D)+\begin{pmatrix}(\mathtt{c}_{+}-a)\partial_{x}&0\\
0&(\mathtt{c}_{-}+a)\partial_{x}\end{pmatrix}\bigg)\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}

and

\mathscr{R}^{[0]}\triangleq\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}\Big({\mathcal{R}}_{[-1]}^{(d)}+{\mathcal{S}}_{M}\Big)\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{R}_{\rm f}.

(6.174)

The estimates (
6.164
) follow from (
6.174
), Proposition
A.3
-
(i)
-
(iii)
-
(iv)
-
(v)
, (
6.112
), (
6.114
) and (
6.172
) with
(n_{1},n_{2})=(\tfrac{1}{2},\tfrac{1}{2})
. The estimates (
6.165
) follow from (
6.174
), (
6.113
), (
6.115
) and (
6.173
).
∎

6.5
Diagonalization of the linearized operator

In this section we diagonalize the operator

\mathscr{L}^{[0]}\triangleq\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}+{\rm i}\,\mathscr{D}^{[0]}+\mathscr{R}^{[0]}.

(6.175)

The diagonalization is performed as a KAM iterative scheme. Recall that the operator
\mathscr{L}^{[0]}
is defined for all
(a,\omega)\in{\mathcal{O}}
and acting on
{\bf H}_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0}}^{\perp}
(see (
4.11
)), where
{\mathscr{D}}^{[0]}
is as in (
6.162
), with
{\mathscr{D}}_{\kappa}^{[0]}
acting on
H_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0,\kappa}}^{\perp}
,
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
, and

{\mathscr{R}}^{[0]}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}{\mathscr{R}}_{+}^{[0,d]}&{\mathscr{R}}_{+}^{[0,o]}\\
{\mathscr{R}}_{-}^{[0,o]}&{\mathscr{R}}_{-}^{[0,d]}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\begin{aligned} &{\mathscr{R}}_{\kappa}^{[0,d]}:H_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0,\kappa}}^{\perp}\to H_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0,\kappa}}^{\perp},\\
&{\mathscr{R}}_{\kappa}^{[0,o]}:H_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0,-\kappa}}^{\perp}\to H_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0,\kappa}}^{\perp},\end{aligned}

(6.176)

which satisfies (
6.164
), (
6.165
) in Proposition
6.7
: here we denoted

H_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0,\kappa}}^{\perp}\triangleq\Big\{\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\setminus\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{\kappa}}h_{j}{\bf e}_{j}\in L_{0}^{2}({\mathbb{T}})\Big\},\quad\kappa\in\{-,+\}.

(6.177)

From now on, we fix
s_{1}\triangleq s_{0}
and we define the constants

\displaystyle{\mathtt{b}}\triangleq\left[\tfrac{3}{2}{\mathtt{a}}\right]+1\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*},\qquad{\mathtt{a}}\triangleq{\rm max}\{2\tau_{2}+1,\mu_{2}\},\qquad\tau>\tau_{1},\qquad\tau_{2}\triangleq\tau(q+1)+q,

(6.178)

where
[\cdot]
denotes the integer part function. We now fix the constant
M
large enough, namely

M\triangleq q+s_{0}+{\mathtt{b}}+2\in\mathbb{N}.

(6.179)

We also set

\mu({\mathtt{b}})\triangleq\aleph(M,s_{0}+{\mathtt{b}}),

(6.180)

where the constant
\aleph(M,l_{0})
is given in Proposition
6.7
, with
l_{0}=s_{0}+{\mathtt{b}}
.

Lemma 6.2

(Smallness of
{\mathscr{R}}^{[0]}
)
.

Assume (
5.4
) with
\sigma_{\rm max}\geqslant\mu({\mathtt{b}})
. Then the operators

{\mathscr{R}}^{[0]},\quad\partial_{{\varphi}_{k}^{s_{0}}}{\mathscr{R}}^{[0]},\quad[\partial_{{\varphi}_{k}^{s_{0}}}{\mathscr{R}}^{[0]},\partial_{x}],\quad\partial_{{\varphi}_{k}^{s_{0}+{\mathtt{b}}}}{\mathscr{R}}^{[0]},\quad[\partial_{{\varphi}_{k}^{s_{0}+{\mathtt{b}}}}{\mathscr{R}}^{[0]},\partial_{x}],\quad k\in\llbracket 1,d\rrbracket,

(6.181)

are
{\mathcal{D}}^{q}
-tame and, defining

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{[0]}(s)

\displaystyle\triangleq\max_{\kappa\in\{-,+\}\atop\delta\in\{o,d\}}\max_{k\in\llbracket 1,d\rrbracket}\bigg\{{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\kappa}^{[0,\delta]}\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}(s),{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}[{\mathscr{R}}_{\kappa}^{[0,\delta]},\partial_{x}]\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}(s),

(6.182)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{{\varphi}_{k}^{s_{0}}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\kappa}^{[0,\delta]}\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}(s),{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}[\partial_{{\varphi}_{k}^{s_{0}}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\kappa}^{[0,\delta]},\partial_{x}]\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}(s)\bigg\},

(6.183)

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{[0]}(s,{\mathtt{b}})

\displaystyle\triangleq\max_{\kappa\in\{-,+\}\atop\delta\in\{o,d\}}\max_{k\in\llbracket 1,d\rrbracket}\bigg\{{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{{\varphi}_{k}^{s_{0}+{\mathtt{b}}}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\kappa}^{[0,\delta]}\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}(s),{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}[\partial_{{\varphi}_{k}^{s_{0}+{\mathtt{b}}}}{\mathscr{R}}_{\kappa}^{[0,\delta]},\partial_{x}]\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}(s)\bigg\},

(6.184)

we have, for all
s\in[s_{0},S]
,

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}(s,{\mathtt{b}})

\displaystyle\triangleq\max\big\{\mathbb{M}^{[0]}(s),\mathbb{M}^{[0]}(s,{\mathtt{b}})\big\}\leqslant C(S)\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\big(1+\|{\mathfrak{I}}_{0}\|_{s+\mu({\mathtt{b}})}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}\big),

(6.185)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}(s_{0},{\mathtt{b}})

\displaystyle\leqslant C(S)\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}.

(6.186)

Moreover, for all
l\in{\mathbb{N}}^{d}
, with
|l|\leqslant s_{0}+{\mathtt{b}}
,

\displaystyle\|\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{{\varphi}}^{l}\Delta_{12}{\mathscr{R}}^{[0]}\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|_{{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{H}_{\perp}^{s_{0}})},\ \|\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{{\varphi}}^{l}\Delta_{12}[{\mathscr{R}}^{[0]},\partial_{x}]\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|_{{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{H}_{\perp}^{s_{0}})}\leqslant C(S)\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{0}+\mu({\mathtt{b}})},

(6.187)

for some constant
C(S)\triangleq C(q,d,S)>0
.

Proof.

Recalling (
6.183
), (
6.184
), the bounds (
6.186
), (
6.187
) follow by (
6.179
) and (
6.180
), and by (
6.164
), (
6.165
) in Proposition
6.7
.
∎

The purpose of this subsection is to prove the reducibility of
{\mathscr{L}}^{[0]}
by using a KAM iteration along the scale
(N_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{-1\}}
given by

N_{-1}\triangleq 1,\qquad\forall\,n\in\mathbb{N},\quad N_{n}\triangleq N_{0}^{\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)^{n}},\qquad N_{0}\geqslant 2.

(6.188)

We have the following proposition.

Proposition 6.8

(Diagonalization of
{\mathscr{L}}^{[0]}
: the KAM iteration)
.

Let
({\mathtt{a}},{\mathtt{b}},\tau_{2},\mu({\mathtt{b}}))
as in (
6.178
), (
6.180
). There exist
\tau_{3}>\tau_{2}+1+{\mathtt{a}}
and
\delta=\delta(s_{0},\tau,d)\in(0,1)
such that, for all
S>s_{0}
, there is
N_{0}\triangleq N_{0}(S,{\mathtt{b}})\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that, if

N_{0}^{\tau_{3}}{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}(s_{0},{\mathtt{b}})\gamma^{-1}\leqslant\delta\,,

(6.189)

then, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the following properties hold true:

(S1)_{n}

There exists a real, reversible and momentum preserving operator in the form

\mathscr{L}^{[n]}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}+{\rm i}\,\mathscr{D}^{[n]}+\mathscr{R}^{[n]},

(6.190)

with

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}^{[n]}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}\mathscr{D}_{+}^{[n]}&0\\
0&\mathscr{D}_{-}^{[n]}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\forall\,\kappa\in\{-,+\},\quad\mathscr{D}_{\kappa}^{[n]}\triangleq\mathtt{diag}\big(\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[n]}\big)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa}},

(6.191)

defined for all
(a,\omega)\in{\mathcal{O}}
, where
\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[n]}
are
q
-times differentiable real functions

\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[n]}(a,\omega;i_{0})\triangleq\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[0]}(a,\omega;i_{0})+\mathtt{r}_{j,\kappa}^{[n]}(a,\omega;i_{0})\in{\mathbb{R}},

(6.192)

with
\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[0]}(a,\omega;i_{0})
as in Proposition
6.7
-
(i)
, satisfying
{\mathtt{r}}_{j,\kappa}^{[0]}\triangleq 0
and, for
n\geqslant 1
,
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
and any
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\setminus\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{\kappa}
,

{\mathtt{r}}_{-j,\kappa}^{[n]}=-{\mathtt{r}}_{j,\kappa}^{[n]}

(6.193)

and

\displaystyle|j|\big\|{\mathtt{r}}_{j,\kappa}^{[n]}\big\|^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}\leqslant C(S,{\mathtt{b}})\varepsilon\gamma^{-1},\qquad|j|\big\|\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[n]}-\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[n-1]}\big\|^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}\leqslant C(S,{\mathtt{b}})\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-2}^{-{\mathtt{a}}},

(6.194)

for some constant
C(S,{\mathtt{b}})\triangleq C(q,d,S,{\mathtt{b}})>0
.

The remainder
{\mathscr{R}}^{[n]}
is a real, reversible and momentum preserving operator, with

{\mathscr{R}}^{[n]}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}{\mathscr{R}}_{+}^{[n,d]}&{\mathscr{R}}_{+}^{[n,o]}\vskip 2.84544pt\\
{\mathscr{R}}_{-}^{[n,o]}&{\mathscr{R}}_{-}^{[n,d]}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\begin{aligned} &{\mathscr{R}}_{\kappa}^{[n,d]}:H_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0,\kappa}}^{\perp}\to H_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0,\kappa}}^{\perp},\\
&{\mathscr{R}}_{\kappa}^{[n,o]}:H_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0,-\kappa}}^{\perp}\to H_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0,\kappa}}^{\perp},\end{aligned}

(6.195)

and the operator
\braket{\partial_{{\varphi}}}^{{\mathtt{b}}}{\mathscr{R}}^{[n]}
are
{\mathcal{D}}^{q}
-
(-1)
-modulo-tame, with modulo-tame constants

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{M}}_{n}^{\sharp}(s)\triangleq{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathscr{R}}^{[n]}\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s),\qquad{\mathfrak{M}}_{n}^{\sharp}(s,{\mathtt{b}})\triangleq{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\braket{\partial_{{\varphi}}}^{{\mathtt{b}}}{\mathscr{R}}^{[n]}\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s),

(6.196)

which satisfy, for some constant
C_{*}(s_{0},{\mathtt{b}})\triangleq C_{*}(q,d,s_{0},{\mathtt{b}})>0
, for all
s\in[s_{0},S]
,

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{M}}_{n}^{\sharp}(s)

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}(s_{0},{\mathtt{b}}){\mathfrak{M}}_{0}(s,{\mathtt{b}})N_{0}^{{\mathtt{a}}}N_{n}^{-{\mathtt{a}}},

(6.197)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{M}}_{n}^{\sharp}(s,{\mathtt{b}})

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}(s_{0},{\mathtt{b}})\big(2-\tfrac{1}{n+1}\big){\mathfrak{M}}_{0}(s,{\mathtt{b}}).

(6.198)

We define the sets
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathtt{KAM},0}^{\gamma,\tau}(i_{0})\triangleq\mathcal{O}
and, for
n\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathtt{KAM},n}^{\gamma,\tau}(i_{0})\triangleq\bigcap_{\kappa\in\{-,+\}}\bigcap_{(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa})^{2}\atop\underset{|\ell|\leqslant N_{n-1}}{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0}}\bigg\{(a,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}\,\textnormal{ s.t. }\big|\omega\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[n-1]}(a,\omega;i_{0})-\mu_{j^{\prime},\kappa}^{[n-1]}(a,\omega;i_{0})\big|>\frac{\gamma\langle j-j^{\prime}\rangle}{\langle\ell\rangle^{\tau}}\bigg\}

(6.199)

\displaystyle\cap\bigcap_{(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{+})\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{-})\atop\underset{|\ell|\leqslant N_{n-1}}{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0}}\bigg\{(a,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}\,\textnormal{ s.t. }\big|\omega\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,+}^{[n-1]}(a,\omega;i_{0})-\mu_{j^{\prime},-}^{[n-1]}(a,\omega;i_{0})\big|>\frac{\gamma\braket{j,j^{\prime}}}{\langle\ell\rangle^{\tau}}\bigg\}.

(6.200)

There exists a real, reversibility preserving and momentum preserving map, defined for all
(a,\omega)\in{\mathcal{O}}
, of the form
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{n}=\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}+{\bf X}^{[n]}
, where
{\bf X}^{[0]}\triangleq 0
and, for
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

{\bf X}^{[n]}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}X_{+}^{[n,d]}&X_{+}^{[n,o]}\vskip 2.84544pt\\
X_{-}^{[n,o]}&X_{-}^{[n,d]}\end{pmatrix},\quad\begin{aligned} &X_{\kappa}^{[n,d]}:H_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0,\kappa}}^{\perp}\to H_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0,\kappa}}^{\perp},\\
&X_{\kappa}^{[n,o]}:H_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0,-\kappa}}^{\perp}\to H_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0,\kappa}}^{\perp},\end{aligned}

(6.201)

such that, for all
(a,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathtt{KAM},n}^{\gamma,\tau}

the following identity holds

\mathscr{L}^{[n]}=\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{n}^{-1}\mathscr{L}^{[0]}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{n}.

(6.202)

The operator
{\bf X}^{[n]}
is
{\mathcal{D}}^{q}
-
(-1)
-modulo-tame satisfying, for all
s\in[s_{0},S]
,

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf X}^{[n]}\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s)

\displaystyle\leqslant C(q)C_{*}(s_{0},{\mathtt{b}})\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{\tau_{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}\big(1+2C(q)N_{0}^{\bar{\mathtt{a}}}N_{j}^{-\bar{\mathtt{a}}}\big){\mathfrak{M}}_{0}(s,{\mathtt{b}}),

(6.203)

and, for
n\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}({\bf X}^{[n]}-{\bf X}^{[n-1]})\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s)

\displaystyle\leqslant 2C^{2}(q)C_{*}(s_{0},{\mathtt{b}})\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{{\mathtt{a}}}N_{n-1}^{-\bar{\mathtt{a}}}\prod_{j=1}^{n-2}\big(1+2C(q)N_{0}^{\bar{\mathtt{a}}}N_{j}^{-\bar{\mathtt{a}}}\big){\mathfrak{M}}_{0}(s,{\mathtt{b}}),

(6.204)

where
\bar{\mathtt{a}}\triangleq{\mathtt{a}}-\tau_{2}>0
(see (
6.178
)) and
\prod_{j=1}^{-1}=\prod_{j=1}^{0}\triangleq 1
.

(S2)_{n}

Let
i_{1}(a,\omega)
and
i_{2}(a,\omega)
are two reversible and momentum preserving tori such that
{\mathscr{R}}^{[n]}(i_{1})
,
{\mathscr{R}}^{[n]}(i_{2})
satisfy (
6.186
), (
6.187
). Then, for all
(a,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}
,

\displaystyle\big\|\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\Delta_{12}{\mathscr{R}}^{[n]}|\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|_{{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{H}_{\perp}^{s_{0}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{S,{\mathtt{b}}}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{-{\mathtt{a}}}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{0}+\mu({\mathtt{b}})},

(6.205)

\displaystyle\big\|\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\braket{\partial_{{\varphi}}}^{{\mathtt{b}}}\Delta_{12}{\mathscr{R}}^{[n]}|\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|_{{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{H}_{\perp}^{s_{0}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{S,{\mathtt{b}}}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{0}+\mu({\mathtt{b}})}.

(6.206)

Furthermore, for
n\geqslant 1
,
k\in\{-,+\}
and any

\displaystyle|j|\big\|\Delta_{12}({\mathtt{r}}_{j,\kappa}^{[n]}-{\mathtt{r}}_{j,\kappa}^{[n-1]})\big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim C(S,{\mathtt{b}})\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{-{\mathtt{a}}}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{0}+\mu({\mathtt{b}})},

(6.207)

\displaystyle|j|\big\|\Delta_{12}{\mathtt{r}}_{j,\kappa}^{[n]}\big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim C(S,{\mathtt{b}})\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{s_{0}+\mu({\mathtt{b}})}.

(6.208)

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
n\in\mathbb{N}.
First, we prove that
(S1)_{n}
,
(S2)_{n}

\blacktriangleright

Proof of
(S1)_{0}
-
(S2)_{0}
.
The property (
6.192
) holds at

by Proposition

-
(i)
with

.
The estimates (
6.197
), (
6.198
) at

follow by the following claim (with the same argument as in (
6.196
) and Lemma
6.2
, arguing as in Lemma 8.4 in
[
13
]
.
Take
R\in\big\{{\mathscr{R}}_{+}^{[0,d]},{\mathscr{R}}_{+}^{[0,o]},{\mathscr{R}}_{-}^{[0,o]},{\mathscr{R}}_{-}^{[0,d]}\big\}.
From the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we infer

\displaystyle\||\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\partial_{\varphi}\rangle^{{\mathtt{b}}}\partial_{\lambda}^{k}R\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}|\rho\|_{s}^{2}

(6.209)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}}\langle\ell,j\rangle^{2s}\bigg(\sum_{(\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}}\langle j\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\ell-\ell^{\prime}\rangle^{{\mathtt{b}}}|(\partial_{\lambda}^{k}R)(\ell-\ell^{\prime})|\langle j^{\prime}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\,|\rho_{\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}}|\bigg)^{2}

(6.210)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}}\langle\ell,j\rangle^{2s}\bigg(\sum_{(\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}}\langle j\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\ell-\ell^{\prime}\rangle^{s_{0}+{\mathtt{b}}}\langle j-j^{\prime}\rangle|(\partial_{\lambda}^{k}R)(\ell-\ell^{\prime})|\langle j^{\prime}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\,|\rho_{\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}}|\frac{1}{\langle\ell-\ell^{\prime}\rangle^{s_{0}}\langle j-j^{\prime}\rangle}\bigg)^{2}

(6.211)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s_{0}}\sum_{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}}\langle j\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\ell,j\rangle^{2s}\sum_{(\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}}\langle\ell-\ell^{\prime}\rangle^{2(s_{0}+{\mathtt{b}})}\langle j-j^{\prime}\rangle^{2}|(\partial_{\lambda}^{k}R)(\ell-\ell^{\prime})|^{2}\langle j^{\prime}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}|\rho_{\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}}|^{2}.

(6.212)

Let us mention that the matrix representation of the commutator
[R,\partial_{x}]
is given by
\big({\rm i}(j-j^{\prime})R_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell-\ell^{\prime})\big)_{(\ell,\ell^{\prime},j,j^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{Z})^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}}.
Therefore, by (
A.21
) and (
6.185
), we find

\displaystyle\gamma^{2|k|}\sum_{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}}\langle\ell,j\rangle^{2s}\langle j\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\ell-\ell^{\prime}\rangle^{2(s_{0}+{\mathtt{b}})}\langle j-j^{\prime}\rangle^{2}|(\partial_{\lambda}^{k}R)(\ell-\ell^{\prime})|^{2}\langle j^{\prime}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s_{0},{\mathtt{b}}}\sum_{(\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}}|\rho_{\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}}|^{2}\big({\mathfrak{M}}_{0}^{2}(s_{0},{\mathtt{b}})\langle\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle^{2s}+{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}^{2}(s,{\mathtt{b}})\langle\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle^{2s_{0}}\big).

Combining the last two estimates, we deduce that

\||\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\partial_{\varphi}\rangle^{{\mathtt{b}}}\partial_{\lambda}^{k}R\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}|\rho\|_{s}^{2}\lesssim_{s_{0},{\mathtt{b}}}\gamma^{-2|k|}\sum_{(\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}}|\rho_{\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}}|^{2}\big({\mathfrak{M}}_{0}^{2}(s_{0},{\mathtt{b}})\langle\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle^{2s}+{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}^{2}(s,{\mathtt{b}})\langle\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle^{2s_{0}}\big).

We thus have proved (

)-(
6.198

\blacktriangleright

\displaystyle\quad\quad+C(q)\Big({\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf X}^{[n-1]}\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s){\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf Y}^{[n]}\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s_{0})+{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf X}^{[n-1]}\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s_{0}){\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf Y}^{[n]}\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s)\Big).

Proposition 6.9

(Diagonalization of

Under the assumptions of Proposition

,

For any

(ii)

Define the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathtt{KAM},\infty}^{2\gamma,\tau}(i_{0})\triangleq\bigcap_{\kappa\in\{-,+\}}\bigcap_{(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa})^{2}\atop{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0}}\bigg\{(a,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}\,\textnormal{ s.t. }\big|\omega\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[\infty]}(a,\omega;i_{0})-\mu_{j^{\prime},\kappa}^{[\infty]}(a,\omega;i_{0})\big|>\frac{2\gamma\langle j-j^{\prime}\rangle}{\langle\ell\rangle^{\tau}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}^{[\infty]}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}\mathscr{D}_{+}^{[\infty]}&0\\

Proof.

Follows by (

(ii)
Given
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we aim to show that
\mathcal{O}_{\mathtt{KAM},\infty}^{2\gamma,\tau}(i_{0})\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{\mathtt{KAM},n}^{\gamma,\tau}(i_{0})
. When
n=0

\mathcal{O}_{\mathtt{KAM},0}^{\gamma,\tau}(i_{0})=\mathcal{O}
. Take
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
(a,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathtt{KAM},\infty}^{2\gamma,\tau}(i_{0})
. Then, for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
,
(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa})^{2}

and

, we have, by (

\displaystyle\big|\omega\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[n-1]}(a,\omega;i_{0})-\mu_{j^{\prime},\kappa}^{[n-1]}(a,\omega;i_{0})\big|

\displaystyle\geqslant\big|\omega\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[\infty]}(a,\omega;i_{0})-\mu_{j^{\prime},\kappa}^{[\infty]}(a,\omega;i_{0})\big|

(6.263)

\displaystyle\quad-\big|{\mathtt{r}}_{j,\kappa}^{[\infty]}(a,\omega;i_{0})-{\mathtt{r}}_{j,\kappa}^{[n-1]}(a,\omega;i_{0})\big|-\big|{\mathtt{r}}_{j^{\prime},\kappa}^{[\infty]}(a,\omega;i_{0})-{\mathtt{r}}_{j^{\prime},\kappa}^{[n-1]}(a,\omega;i_{0})\big|

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2\gamma\braket{j-j^{\prime}}}{\braket{\ell}^{\tau}}-C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}|j|^{-1}N_{n-2}^{-{\mathtt{a}}}-C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}|j^{\prime}|^{-1}N_{n-2}^{-{\mathtt{a}}}

(6.265)

(6.266)

as soon as

2C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-2}^{-{\mathtt{a}}}\leqslant\frac{\gamma}{\braket{\ell}^{\tau}},\qquad|\ell|\leqslant N_{n-1}

(6.267)

which holds by (

), (
6.188
), for
N_{0}\gg 1
large enough, independently of
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Similarly, for all
(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{+})\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{-})

{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0}

\displaystyle\big|\omega\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,+}^{[n-1]}(a,\omega;i_{0})-\mu_{j^{\prime},-}^{[n-1]}(a,\omega;i_{0})\big|

(6.268)

as soon as (
6.267
) is satisfied. This concludes the proof of the claim.

(iii)
We note that, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, by a convexity argument and (
6.188
), we have

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}\underbrace{\big(1+2C(q)N_{0}^{\bar{\mathtt{a}}}N_{j}^{-\bar{\mathtt{a}}}\big)}_{\geqslant 1}\leqslant\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}{\rm exp}\big(2C(q)N_{0}^{\bar{\mathtt{a}}}N_{j}^{-\bar{\mathtt{a}}}\big)\leqslant{\rm exp}\Big(2C(q)N_{0}^{\bar{\mathtt{a}}}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}N_{j}^{-\bar{\mathtt{a}}}\Big)<\infty.

This implies the convergence of the product. By (

) and (

), (

) in Proposition

, we deduce the existence of the
{\mathcal{D}}^{q}
-
(-1)
-modulo-tame operator

, defined for all

by

\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{\infty}\triangleq\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}+{\bf X}^{[\infty]}\triangleq\lim_{n\to\infty}\big(\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}+{\bf X}^{[n]}\big)=\lim_{n\to\infty}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{n},

(6.270)

such that (
6.261
), (
6.262
) hold for any

by taking the limit in (

) in Proposition

(ii)
. This concludes the proof of item

and of Proposition

6.6
Inversion of the linearized operator

We conclude this section by explicitly constructing a full right inverse for the linearized operator in the normal directions. Owing to the prior introduction of the diagonal operator
{\mathscr{L}}^{[\infty]}
, whose entries are given by Fourier multipliers, the problem reduces to the analysis of two independent diagonal scalar components. We therefore appeal to the argument developed in
[
60
, Proposition 6.6]
and
[
37
, Lemma 12.10]
. The result reads as follows.

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
(\gamma,q,d,\tau_{1},\tau,s_{0},\mu_{2})
as in (
A.1
), (
5.30
), (
6.92
) and (
6.178
). There exists
\sigma\triangleq\sigma(\tau,d,q)\gg 1
large enough such that, if (
5.4
) holds with
\sigma_{\rm max}\geqslant\sigma,

then the following assertions are true:

(i)

Let
{\mathscr{L}}^{[\infty]}
be the operator defined in Proposition
6.9
-
(iii)
. There exists a real, reversible and momentum preserving operator
{\mathtt{T}}
, defined for all
(a,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}
and satisfying, for all
s\in[s_{0},S]
,

\|{\mathtt{T}}\rho\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\|\rho\|_{s+\tau(q+1)+q}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}},

(6.271)

such that for any
(a,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{\tiny{Inv}},\infty}^{\gamma,\tau}(i_{0})
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{\tiny{Inv}},\infty}^{\gamma,\tau}(i_{0})\triangleq\bigcap_{\kappa\in\{-,+\}}\bigcap_{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{*}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa})\atop{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0}}\bigg\{(a,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\big|\omega\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[\infty]}(a,\omega;i_{0})\big|>\frac{\gamma\braket{j}}{\braket{\ell}^{\tau}}\bigg\},

(6.272)

we have

{\mathscr{L}}^{[\infty]}{\mathtt{T}}=\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp}.

(6.273)

(ii)

There exists a real, reversible and momentum preserving operator
{\widehat{{\mathtt{T}}}}
, defined for all
(a,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}
and satisfying, for all
s\in[s_{0},S]
,

\|{\widehat{{\mathtt{T}}}}\rho\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\big(\|\rho\|_{s+\sigma}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}+\|{\mathfrak{I}}_{0}\|_{s+\sigma}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}\|\rho\|_{s+\sigma}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}\big),

(6.274)

such that, for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and for any
(a,\omega)\in{\mathtt{G}}(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau,i_{0})
, where (see (
6.106
), (
6.258
), (
6.272
))

\displaystyle{\mathtt{G}}(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau,i_{0})\triangleq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\rm T},\infty}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})\cap{\mathcal{O}}_{{\tt KAM},\infty}^{2\gamma,\tau}(i_{0})\cap\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{\tiny{Inv}},\infty}^{\gamma,\tau}(i_{0}),

(6.275)

we have

\mathscr{L}_{\omega}{\widehat{{\mathtt{T}}}}=\Pi_{\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{\perp},

(6.276)

with
\mathscr{L}_{\omega}
as in (
6.1
).

Proof.

(i)
By the definition of
{\mathscr{L}}^{[\infty]}
in (
6.261
), (
6.262
) of Proposition

, we have

{\mathscr{L}}^{[\infty]}=\begin{pmatrix}\omega\cdot\partial_{{\varphi}}\Pi_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{+}}^{\perp}+{\rm i}{\mathscr{D}}_{+}^{[\infty]}&0\\
0&\omega\cdot\partial_{{\varphi}}\Pi_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{-}}^{\perp}+{\rm i}{\mathscr{D}}_{-}^{[\infty]}\end{pmatrix}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}{\mathscr{L}}_{+}^{[\infty]}&0\\
0&{\mathscr{L}}_{-}^{[\infty]}\end{pmatrix},

(6.277)

with

\forall\,\kappa\in\{-,+\},\ \ \forall\,(\ell,j)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\setminus\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{\kappa},\quad\mathscr{L}_{\kappa}^{[\infty]}{\bf e}_{\ell,j}={\rm i}\big(\omega\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[\infty]}\big){\bf e}_{\ell,j}.

(6.278)

We define the operator
\mathtt{T}
by

{\mathtt{T}}\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}{\mathtt{T}}_{+}&0\\
0&{\mathtt{T}}_{-}\end{pmatrix},

(6.279)

where, for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
, the diagonal operator
{\mathtt{T}}_{\kappa}
is defined by

h=\sum_{(\ell,j)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\times({\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa})\atop\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0}h_{\ell,j}{\bf e}_{\ell,j},\qquad\mathtt{T}_{\kappa}h\triangleq\sum_{(\ell,j)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\times({\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa})\atop\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0}\frac{\chi\big((\omega\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[\infty]})\upsilon^{-1}\langle\ell\rangle^{\tau}\langle j\rangle^{-1}\big)}{{\rm i}\big(\omega\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[\infty]}\big)}h_{\ell,j}\,{\bf e}_{\ell,j},

(6.280)

where
\chi
is the cut-off function defined in (
3.22
). The operator
{\mathtt{T}}_{\kappa}
in (
6.280
) is real, reversible and momentum preserving by (
6.253
), (
6.254
), (
6.163
), Lemma
3.1
-
(i)
and Lemma
A.3
.
Thus, for
(a,\omega)\in{\mathcal{O}}_{{\rm Inv},\infty}^{\gamma,\tau}(i_{0})
, one deduces (
6.273
).
Since
\|\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[\infty]}\|^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\underset{j\to\infty}{\sim}|j|
, then one easily checks the estimate (
6.271
) directly from (
6.280
) and (
6.272
).

(ii)
The relation between
{\mathscr{L}}_{\omega}
and
{\mathscr{L}}^{[\infty]}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}^{-1}{\mathscr{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{W}={\mathscr{L}}^{[\infty]},\qquad\mathscr{W}\triangleq\mathscr{M}_{\perp}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{\infty},

(6.281)

with
\mathscr{M}_{\perp}
as in (
6.140
) and
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{\infty}
as in Proposition
6.9
-
(iii)
. Note that the map
\mathscr{W}
is invertible by Lemma
6.1
-
(ii)
and Proposition
6.9
-
(iii)
.
In view of item
(i)
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}^{-1}{\mathscr{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{W}{\mathtt{T}}={\mathscr{L}}^{[\infty]}{\mathtt{T}}=\Pi_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0}}^{\perp}.

(6.282)

Conjugating back, we obtain the identity

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{W}{\mathtt{T}}\mathscr{W}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{W}\mathscr{W}^{-1}=\Pi_{\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{0}}^{\perp},

(6.283)

which proves (
6.276
) with
{\widehat{{\mathtt{T}}}}
defined as

{\widehat{{\mathtt{T}}}}\triangleq\mathscr{W}{\mathtt{T}}\mathscr{W}^{-1}.

(6.284)

The corresponding estimate (
6.274
) for
{\widehat{{\mathtt{T}}}}
and the fact that
{\widehat{{\mathtt{T}}}}
is real, reversible and momentum preserving follow from the properties of
{\mathtt{T}}
in item
(i)
, together with those of
\mathscr{M}_{\perp}
and
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{\infty}
established in Lemma
6.1
-
(ii)
and Proposition
6.9
-
(iii)
, respectively, and Proposition
A.2
-
(v)
, Proposition
A.1
-
(ii)
. This ends the proof.
∎

7
Construction of quasi-periodic solutions

We provide, in this last section, a construction of a non-trivial solution to the equation

\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}(i,\alpha)\triangleq\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}(i,\alpha,a,\omega)=0,

(7.1)

with
\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}(i,\alpha,a,\omega)
as in (
4.18
) This is done in two steps.
First, we implement a Nash–Moser iteration, where we find a solution provided that the parameters
(a,\omega)
belong to a suitable Borel set. The latter is constructed as the intersection of the Cantor sets required to invert the linearized operator in the normal modes for all the steps of the procedure. Then we rigidified the frequencies in order to get a solution for the original problem where
\alpha=-{\mathtt{J}}\omega_{\rm Eq}(a)
.
This gives rise to a final set described in terms of
a
that we should estimate its Lebesgue measure. Actually, we prove that it has asymptotically full measure as the parameter
\varepsilon
vanishes.

7.1
Nash-Moser construction of a priori solutions

Here, we perform the Nash-Moser scheme which allows to find a solution of (
7.1
). This method is nowadays classic in the context of quasi-periodic solutions for quasilinear PDEs, see for instance
[
6
,
13
,
17
]
and recent developments
[
15
,
57
,
60
]
. The iterative construction of the approximate solutions is summarized in the following proposition: it follows
[
58
, Prop. 8.1]
(with
[
13
, Prop. 9.2]
to prove the tori are traveling reversible) and therefore we only present the general scheme of the proof, omitting all the explicit estimations.

Proposition 7.1
.

(Nash-Moser scheme)

Let
(\tau_{1},\tau,q,d,S,s_{0})
as in (
A.1
) and (
6.178
). We consider the following parameters

\begin{cases}\tilde{{\mathtt{a}}}=\tau+3,\\
\mu_{1}=3q(\tau+3)+6\overline{\sigma}+6,\\
\nu_{1}=6q(\tau+3)+12\overline{\sigma}+15,\\
\nu_{2}=3q(\tau+3)+6\overline{\sigma}+9,\\
\mathtt{a}=2q(\tau+3)+5\overline{\sigma}+7,\\
s_{h}=s_{0}+4q(\tau+3)+9\overline{\sigma}+11,\\
\kappa_{1}=2s_{h}-s_{0},\end{cases}

(7.2)

where
\bar{\sigma}\triangleq\bar{\sigma}(\tau_{1},\tau,d)>0
is the total loss of regularity given by Theorem
5.1
.
For
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we introduce the finite dimensional subspace
E_{n}
defined by

\displaystyle E_{n}\triangleq\Big\{{\mathfrak{I}}({\varphi})=\big(\Theta({\varphi}),I({\varphi}),z({\varphi})\big)\ \textnormal{s.t. \eqref{rev trav torus} holds}\ :\,\Theta=\Pi_{n}\Theta,\ I=\Pi_{n}I,\ z=\Pi_{n}z\Big\},

(7.3)

where
\Pi_{n}z\triangleq\Pi_{N_{n}}z
as in (
A.8
) with
N_{n}
in (
6.188
), and
\Pi_{n}g({\varphi})\triangleq\sum_{|\ell|\leqslant N_{n}}g_{\ell}e^{{\rm i}\ell\cdot{\varphi}}
.

There exist
C_{*}>0
and
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that, for any
\varepsilon\in[0,\varepsilon_{0}]
, imposing the constraint relating
\gamma
and
N_{0}
to
\varepsilon
,

0<\varrho<(\mathtt{a}+q+1)^{-1},\qquad\gamma\triangleq\varepsilon^{\varrho},\qquad N_{0}\triangleq\gamma^{-1},

(7.4)

then following properties hold true, for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
:

({\mathcal{P}}1)_{n}

There exists a
q
-times differentiable function

{\mathtt{W}}_{n}:{\mathcal{O}}\to E_{n-1}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\qquad\lambda\triangleq(a,\omega)\mapsto\big({\mathfrak{I}}_{n}(a,\omega),\alpha_{n}(a,\omega)-{\mathtt{J}}\omega\big),

(7.5)

satisfying
{\mathtt{W}}_{0}\triangleq 0
and, for
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\|{\mathtt{W}}_{n}\|_{s_{0}+\bar{\sigma}}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}\leqslant C_{*}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\tilde{{\mathtt{a}}}}.

(7.6)

We set
{\mathtt{U}}_{0}\triangleq\big(({\varphi},0,0),-{\mathtt{J}}\omega\big)
and, for
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

{\mathtt{U}}_{n}\triangleq{\mathtt{U}}_{0}+{\mathtt{W}}_{n}=(i_{n},\alpha_{n}),\qquad{\mathtt{H}}_{n}\triangleq{\mathtt{U}}_{n}-{\mathtt{U}}_{n-1}.

(7.7)

Then, for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathtt{H}}_{1}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}C_{*}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\tilde{{\mathtt{a}}}},

(7.8)

\displaystyle\|{\mathtt{H}}_{m}\|_{s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{m-1}^{-\nu_{2}},\quad\forall\,2\leqslant m\leqslant n;

(7.9)

({\mathcal{P}}2)_{n}

We set

\displaystyle i_{n}({\varphi})\triangleq({\varphi},0,0)+{\mathfrak{I}}_{n}({\varphi}),\qquad\gamma_{n}\triangleq\gamma(1+2^{-n})\in[\gamma,2\gamma].

(7.10)

Then
i_{n}
is a traveling reversible torus, that is, according to Definition
4.1
, for any
\varphi\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
and any
y\in\mathbb{T},

\mathfrak{S}i_{n}(\varphi)=i_{n}(-\varphi)\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad\mathfrak{T}_{y}i_{n}(\varphi)=i_{n}(\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y).

(7.11)

We also define

{\mathtt{A}}_{0}^{\gamma}\triangleq{\mathcal{O}},\qquad{\mathtt{A}}_{n+1}^{\gamma}\triangleq{\mathtt{A}}_{n}^{\gamma}\cap{\mathtt{G}}(\gamma_{n+1},\tau_{1},\tau,i_{n})\cap{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{C}}(\gamma_{n+1},\tau),

(7.12)

where
{\mathtt{G}}(\gamma_{n+1},\tau_{1},\tau,i_{n})
and
{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{C}}(\gamma_{n+1},\tau)
are given respectively through (
6.275
), (
5.30
), and for any
{\mathtt{v}}>0
,

\displaystyle{\rm O}_{n}^{{\mathtt{v}}}\triangleq\Big\{\lambda=(a,\omega)\in{\mathcal{O}}\ \textnormal{ s.t. }\ {\rm dist}\big(\lambda,{\mathtt{A}}_{n}^{2\gamma}\big)<{\mathtt{v}}N_{n}^{-\nu_{1}}\Big\},\qquad{\rm dist}(x,{\mathtt{A}})\triangleq\inf_{y\in{\mathtt{A}}}\|x-y\|.

(7.13)

Then we have the following estimate

\|{\mathscr{F}}_{\varepsilon}({\mathtt{U}}_{n})\|_{s_{0}}^{q,\gamma,{\rm O}_{n}^{2\gamma}}\triangleq\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{d+1}\atop|k|\leqslant q}\gamma^{|k|}\sup_{\lambda\in{\rm O}_{n}^{2\gamma}}\|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}({\mathtt{U}}_{n},\lambda)\|_{s_{0}-|k|}\leqslant C_{*}\varepsilon N_{n-1}^{-\nu_{1}};

(7.14)

({\mathcal{P}}3)_{n}

We have the following growth in high regularity norm

\|{\mathtt{W}}_{n}\|_{\kappa_{1}+\bar{\sigma}}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}\leqslant C_{*}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{\mu_{1}}.

(7.15)

Proof.

We briefly sketch the iterative construction of the approximate solution, which is built over an induction argument. The starting point is
{\mathtt{U}}_{0}\triangleq\big(({\varphi},0,0),-{\mathtt{J}}\omega\big)
, which is an approximate zero of
\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}
in (
4.18
), that is up to
\varepsilon
-small error (recall also
i_{\rm triv}=({\varphi},0,0)
in (
4.19
)).
Let now
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and define

L_{n}\triangleq{\rm d}_{i,\alpha}\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}({\mathtt{U}}_{n}),

(7.16)

where
{\mathtt{U}}_{n}
is the approximate solution at the step
n
.
By Theorem
5.1
, we have the existence of the real, reversible and momentum preserving operator
{\rm T}_{n}
, which is an approximate right inverse of
L_{n}
on the Cantor set
{\mathtt{A}}_{n+1}^{\gamma}
, that is, for all
(a,\omega)\in{\mathtt{A}}_{n+1}^{\gamma}
, one has

L_{n}{\rm T}_{n}-{\rm Id}=\mathfrak{E}^{(n)},

(7.17)

where
{\rm T}_{n}

satisfies the estimates

\displaystyle\forall\,s\in[s_{0},S],\quad\|{\rm T}_{n}g\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim_{s,q,d}\gamma^{-1}\big(\|g\|_{s+{\overline{\sigma}}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|{\mathtt{U}}_{n}\|_{s+{\overline{\sigma}}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g\|_{s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big),

(7.18)

and
\mathfrak{E}^{(n)}
satisfies the estimate (
5.40
). At the step
n+1
, we construct the new approximate solution by defining

{\mathtt{U}}_{n+1}\triangleq{\mathtt{U}}_{n}+H_{n+1},\quad H_{n+1}\triangleq(\mathfrak{I}_{n+1},\alpha_{n+1})\triangleq-\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}{\rm T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}_{\varepsilon}({\mathtt{U}}_{n}),

(7.19)

where

\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}(\mathfrak{I},\alpha)\triangleq(\Pi_{n}\mathfrak{I},\alpha),\qquad\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}^{\perp}(\mathfrak{I},\alpha)\triangleq(\Pi_{n}^{\perp}\mathfrak{I},0).

Since
{\rm T}_{n}
is reversible and momentum preserving and since
\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}
is a reversible, space translation invariant vector field, one deduces that
{\mathtt{U}}_{n+1}
is a reversible traveling wave, as is
{\mathtt{U}}_{n}
by induction.
By Taylor formula and the choice of
H_{n+1}
in (
7.19
), we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}({\mathtt{U}}_{n+1})

\displaystyle=\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}({\mathtt{U}}_{n})+L_{n}H_{n+1}+Q_{n}

(7.20)

\displaystyle=\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}({\mathtt{U}}_{n})-L_{n}\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}{\rm T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathcal{F}_{\varepsilon}({\mathtt{U}}_{n})+Q_{n}

(7.21)

\displaystyle=\Pi_{n}^{\perp}\mathscr{F}({\mathtt{U}}_{n})-\Pi_{n}(L_{n}{\rm T}_{n}-{\rm Id})\Pi_{n}\mathscr{F}({\mathtt{U}}_{n})+(L_{n}\mathbf{\Pi}_{n}^{\perp}-\Pi_{n}^{\perp}L_{n}){\rm T}_{n}\Pi_{n}\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}({\mathtt{U}}_{n})+Q_{n},

(7.22)

where

Q_{n}\triangleq\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}({\mathtt{U}}_{n}+H_{n+1})-\mathscr{F}({\mathtt{U}}_{n})-L_{n}H_{n+1}.

The estimate (
7.14
) is obtained by induction using (
7.20
), (
5.40
), Lemma
5.1
Lemma
A.2
and the choice of parameters (
7.2
)-(
7.4
). the estimate (
7.15
) is obtained by a direct estimation using (
7.19
), the induction hypothesis and the choice of parameters (
7.2
). For more details, we refer to
[
60
]
.
∎

The previous iteration procedure converges and allows to find a non trivial reversible
quasi-periodic solution of our problem provided some restriction on

a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]
. More precisely, we have the following result.

Corollary 7.1
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
7.1
, there exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that, for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, the following assertions are true. There exists a
q
-times differentiable function

{\mathtt{U}}_{\infty}:{\mathcal{O}}\to\big({\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\bf H}_{\perp}^{s_{0}}\big)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\quad(a,\omega)\mapsto\big(i_{\infty}(a,\omega),\alpha_{\infty}(a,\omega)\big),

(7.23)

such that, for any
(a,\omega)\in{\mathtt{G}}_{\infty}^{\gamma}
, where
{\mathtt{G}}_{\infty}^{\gamma}
is defined by

{\mathtt{G}}_{\infty}^{\gamma}\triangleq\bigcap_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathtt{A}}_{n}^{\gamma},

(7.24)

with
{\mathtt{A}}_{n}^{\gamma}
as in (
7.12
), we have

\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}\big({\mathtt{U}}_{\infty}(a,\omega)\big)=0.

(7.25)

The torus
i_{\infty}
is traveling and reversible, that is (
4.1
) holds. The vector
\alpha_{\infty}\in W^{q,\infty,\gamma}({\mathcal{O}},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
satisfies

\alpha_{\infty}(a,\omega)=-{\mathtt{J}}\omega+{\rm r}_{\varepsilon}(a,\omega),\qquad\|{\rm r}_{\varepsilon}\|^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\bar{\mathtt{a}}}.

(7.26)

In addition, there exists a
q
-times differentiable function
[a_{0},a_{1}]\ni a\mapsto\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)
implicitly defined by

\alpha_{\infty}\big(a,\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\big)=-{\mathtt{J}}\omega_{\rm Eq}(a),

(7.27)

with

\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)=\omega_{\rm Eq}(a)+\bar{r}_{\varepsilon}(a),\qquad\|\bar{r}_{\varepsilon}\|^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\bar{\mathtt{a}}},

(7.28)

such that, for any
a\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}
,

\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}\big({\mathtt{U}}_{\infty}(a,\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a))\big)=0,

(7.29)

where

\mathscr{C}(\varepsilon)\triangleq\Big\{a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]\ \textnormal{ s.t. }\ \big(a,\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\big)\in{\mathtt{G}}_{\infty}^{\gamma}\Big\}.

(7.30)

Proof.

It follows analogously as in
[
58
, Cor. 8.1]
. In particular,
i_{\infty}
is the traveling reversible torus obtained as the limit of the Cauchy sequence of traveling reversible tori
(i_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
in (
7.7
), (
7.9
) (noting that (
7.11
) holds in the limit), which is a solution of the system by passing in the limit
n\to\infty
in (
7.14
).
∎

7.2
Measure of the final Cantor set

In Corollary
7.1
, we proved the existence of solutions to the equation (
7.1
). In this last section, in order to finally prove Theorem
1.1
, we check that the final Cantor set
\mathscr{C}(\varepsilon)
in the variable
a
given by (
7.30
) is massive set, which proves the existence of non-trivial quasi-periodic solution to our problem. Actually, we prove that the measure of
\mathscr{C}(\varepsilon)
is
\varepsilon
-close to
\big|[a_{0},a_{1}]\big|=a_{1}-a_{0}>0
. One of the main technical ingredient is Rüssmann Theorem
[
89
, Thm. 17.1]
(see also its slight reformulation in
[
58
, Lemma 8.1]
) that will allows to estimate the measure of the sublevel sets for non-degenerate functions.

First, we prove the following proposition, which is the main result of this section. The proof relies of several lemmata that, for clarity of the presentation, we state and prove right after.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
q_{0}
be as in Lemma
3.3
,
\varrho
as in (
7.4
) and assume that (
7.2
), (
7.4
) hold with
q\triangleq q_{0}+2
. Assume the additional conditions

\tau_{1}>dq_{0},\qquad\tau>(d+2\tau_{1}-1)q_{0},\qquad\upsilon\triangleq(4q_{0}+1)^{-1}.

(7.31)

Then there exists
C>0
such that

a_{1}-a_{0}-C\varepsilon^{\frac{\varrho\upsilon}{q_{0}}}\leqslant|\mathscr{C}(\varepsilon)|\leqslant a_{1}-a_{0}.

(7.32)

Proof.

We split the presentation of the proof in several steps.

\blacktriangleright

Decomposition in “nearly-resonant sets”.

The definitions in (
7.24
), (
7.30
) provide the decomposition of the final Cantor set

\mathscr{C}(\varepsilon)=\bigcap_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\varepsilon},\quad\textnormal{where}\quad{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\triangleq\Big\{a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]\ \textnormal{ s.t. }\ \big(a,\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\big)\in{\mathtt{A}}_{n}^{\gamma}\Big\},

(7.33)

with
{\mathtt{A}}_{n}^{\gamma}
as in (
7.12
) and
\omega(a,\varepsilon)
as in (
7.28
). We can write

[a_{0},a_{1}]\setminus\mathscr{C}(\varepsilon)=\big([a_{0},a_{1}]\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{0}^{\varepsilon}\big)\sqcup\bigsqcup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\big({\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}\big).

(7.34)

First, we claim that

[a_{0},a_{1}]\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{0}^{\varepsilon}=\varnothing,\quad\textnormal{that is}\quad{\mathcal{C}}_{0}^{\varepsilon}=[a_{0},a_{1}].

(7.35)

By (
7.33
), (
7.12
), (
5.2
), this is equivalent to prove that

\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}\big([a_{0},a_{1}]\big)\subset B_{R_{0}}(0),

(7.36)

with
R_{0}>0
fixed such that (
5.3
) holds. By (
7.28
) and (
7.4
), we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]}|\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{R_{0}}{2}+C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\bar{\mathtt{a}}}

(7.37)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{R_{0}}{2}+C\varepsilon^{1-\varrho(1+q\bar{\mathtt{a}})}\leqslant R_{0},

(7.38)

which holds for
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
small enough, with
0<\varrho<(1+q\bar{\mathtt{a}})^{-1}
as in (
7.4
). This shows (
7.36
) and the claim is proved. Back to (
7.34
), we have

\big|[a_{0},a_{1}]\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}\big|\leqslant\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\big|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}\big|\triangleq\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}.

(7.39)

With a slight abuse of notation, by (
6.163
), (
6.253
), we denote the perturbed frequencies
associated with the reduced linearized operator at state
i_{n}
in the following way

\displaystyle\mathtt{c}_{\kappa,n}(a)

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathtt{c}_{\kappa}(a,\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a);i_{n}),

(7.40)

\displaystyle\mu_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)

\displaystyle\triangleq\mu_{j,\kappa}^{[\infty]}(a,\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a);i_{n})=\kappa\Omega_{j}(a)+(\mathtt{c}_{\kappa,n}(a)-\kappa a)j+\mathtt{r}_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a),

(7.41)

\displaystyle\mathtt{r}_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathtt{r}_{j,\kappa}^{[\infty]}(a,\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a);i_{n}).

(7.42)

Moreover, by Corollary
7.1
, Proposition
6.9
and Proposition
6.6
, we have the estimates

\displaystyle|\partial_{a}^{k}\vec{r}_{\varepsilon}(a)|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1-k}\,,\quad\forall\,k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket,\quad\textnormal{uniformly in }a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]\,,

(7.43)

\displaystyle|\partial_{a}^{k}\big(\mathtt{c}_{\kappa,n}(a)-\kappa a\big)|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-k},\quad\forall\,k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket,\quad\textnormal{uniformly in }a\in[a_{0},a_{1}],

(7.44)

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa}}|\partial_{a}^{k}\mathtt{r}_{j,\kappa,n}^{[n]}(a)|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1-k},\quad\forall\,k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket,\quad\textnormal{uniformly in }a\in[a_{0},a_{1}].

(7.45)

According to (
7.33
), (
7.12
) and Propositions
6.6
,
6.9
,
6.10
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\bigcup_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}R_{\ell}^{(0,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg]\cup\bigcup_{\kappa\in\{-,+\}}\Bigg[\bigcup_{{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0)\}\atop\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0}}R_{\ell,j,\kappa}^{({\rm Tr},n)}(i_{n})\Bigg]\cup\bigcup_{\kappa\in\{-,+\}}\Bigg[\bigcup_{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{*}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa})\atop{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0}}R_{\ell,j,\kappa}^{(I,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg]

(7.46)

\displaystyle\cup\bigcup_{\kappa\in\{-,+\}}\Bigg[\bigcup_{(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa})^{2}\atop{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0,\,j\neq j^{\prime}}}R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg]\cup\Bigg[\bigcup_{(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{+})\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{-})\atop{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0}}Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg],

(7.47)

where the “nearly-resonant sets” are

\displaystyle R_{\ell}^{(0,n)}(i_{n})

\displaystyle\triangleq\bigg\{a\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad|\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell|\leqslant\frac{4\gamma_{n+1}}{\langle\ell\rangle^{\tau}}\bigg\},

(7.48)

\displaystyle R_{\ell,j,\kappa}^{({\rm Tr},n)}(i_{n})

\displaystyle\triangleq\bigg\{a\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\big|\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+j{\mathtt{c}}_{\kappa,n}(a)\big|\leqslant\frac{4\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}}{\langle\ell\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\bigg\},

(7.49)

\displaystyle R_{\ell,j,k}^{(I,n)}(i_{n})

\displaystyle\triangleq\bigg\{a\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\big|\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)\big|\leqslant\frac{2\gamma_{n+1}|j|}{\langle\ell\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\bigg\},

(7.50)

\displaystyle R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})

\displaystyle\triangleq\bigg\{a\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\big|\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j^{\prime},\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)\big|\leqslant\frac{2\gamma_{n+1}\langle j-j^{\prime}\rangle}{\langle\ell\rangle^{\tau}}\bigg\},

(7.51)

\displaystyle Q_{\ell,j,j}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})

\displaystyle\triangleq\bigg\{a\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\big|\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,+,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j^{\prime},-,n}^{[\infty]}(a)\big|\leqslant\frac{2\gamma_{n+1}\braket{j,j^{\prime}}}{\langle\ell\rangle^{\tau}}\bigg\}.

(7.52)

Note that, in (
7.47
), we require the union for
R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}
only for
j\neq j^{\prime}
because we have
R_{\ell,j,j,\kappa}^{(II,n)}\subset R_{\ell}^{(0,n)}
.

In the sequel, we will always suppose the momentum conditions on the indexes
\ell,j,j^{\prime}
in (
7.47
).

\blacktriangleright

Application of Rüssmann’s Theorem.

Since
W^{q,\infty,\gamma}({\mathcal{O}},{\mathbb{C}})\hookrightarrow C^{q-1}({\mathcal{O}},{\mathbb{C}})
and
q=q_{0}+2
, one obtains, for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and any
k\in\{-,+\}
, the
C^{q_{0}+1}
-regularity of the curves

\displaystyle a\mapsto\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell,

\displaystyle\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\setminus\{0\},

(7.53)

\displaystyle a\mapsto\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+j\big({\mathtt{c}}_{\kappa,n}(a)-\kappa a\big),

\displaystyle(\ell,j)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\{(0,0)\},

(7.54)

\displaystyle a\mapsto\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a),

\displaystyle(\ell,j)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\times({\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\setminus\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{\kappa}),

(7.55)

\displaystyle a\mapsto\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j^{\prime},\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a),

\displaystyle(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\times({\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\setminus\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{\kappa})^{2},

(7.56)

\displaystyle a\mapsto\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,+,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j^{\prime},-,n}^{[\infty]}(a),

\displaystyle(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\times({\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\setminus\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{+})\times({\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\setminus\overline{S}_{-}).

(7.57)

Therefore, applying Rüssmann Theorem
[
89
, Thm. 17.1]
together with the perturbed transversality estimates in Lemma
7.3
and the momentum conditions, we obtain that the measure of the sets (
7.48
)-(
7.52
) satisfy

\displaystyle|R_{\ell}^{(0,n)}(i_{n})|\lesssim\gamma_{n+1}^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\braket{\ell}^{-\frac{\tau+1}{q_{0}}-1},\quad\quad\quad\ |R_{\ell,j}^{({\rm Tr},n)}(i_{n})|\lesssim\gamma_{n+1}^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}\braket{\ell}^{-\frac{\tau_{1}+1}{q_{0}}-1},

(7.58)

\displaystyle|R_{\ell,j}^{(I,n)}(i_{n})|\lesssim\gamma_{n+1}^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\braket{\ell}^{-\frac{\tau}{q_{0}}-1},\quad\quad\quad\ \ \,|R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})|\lesssim\gamma_{n+1}^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\braket{\ell}^{-\frac{\tau}{q_{0}}-1},

(7.59)

\displaystyle|Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})|\lesssim\gamma_{n+1}^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\braket{j,j^{\prime}}^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\braket{\ell}^{-\frac{\tau+1}{q_{0}}-1}.

(7.60)

\blacktriangleright

Estimates of the leading orders
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
,
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
.

We first estimate the size of
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
defined in (
7.47
) and (
7.39
). By (
7.48
), (
7.49
), (
7.50
) and (
7.59
), we have, for
n=0,1
,
\kappa=\{-,+\}

\displaystyle\Big|\bigcup_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}R_{\ell}^{(0,n)}(i_{n})\Big|\leqslant\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}|R_{\ell}^{(0,n)}(i_{n})|\lesssim\gamma_{n+1}^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}\braket{\ell}^{-\frac{\tau+1}{q_{0}}-1}\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}},

(7.61)

\displaystyle\Big|\bigcup_{(\ell,j)\neq(0,0)\atop\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0}R_{\ell,j,\kappa}^{({\rm Tr},n)}(i_{n})\Big|\leqslant\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}|R_{\ell,-\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell,\kappa}^{({\rm Tr},n)}(i_{n})|\lesssim\gamma_{n+1}^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}\braket{\ell}^{-\frac{\tau_{1}+1}{q_{0}}-1}\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}},

(7.62)

\displaystyle\Big|\bigcup_{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{*}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa})\atop{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0}}R_{\ell,j,\kappa}^{(I,n)}(i_{n})\Big|\leqslant\sum_{|j|\leqslant C\braket{\ell}}|R_{\ell,j,\kappa}^{(I,n)}|\lesssim\gamma_{n+1}^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}\braket{\ell}^{-\frac{\tau}{q_{0}}}\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}},

(7.63)

where we used the choice of
\tau
in (
7.31
) to ensure the convergence of the series.
We now estimate the measure, for any
n=0,1

\kappa\in\{-,+\}
, of

\displaystyle\bigcup_{(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa})^{2}\atop{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0,\,j\neq j^{\prime}}}R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})=\Bigg[\bigcup_{{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0,\,j\neq j^{\prime}}\atop j\cdot j^{\prime}<0}R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg]\cup\Bigg[\bigcup_{{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0,\,j\neq j^{\prime}}\atop j\cdot j^{\prime}>0}R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg]\triangleq{\mathtt{I}}_{1,n}\cup{\mathtt{I}}_{2,n}.

(7.64)

We first estimate the measure of
{\mathtt{I}}_{1,n}
. By Lemma
7.2
and (
7.59
), we deduce that, for any
n=0,1

\kappa\in\{-,+\}
,

\displaystyle|{\mathtt{I}}_{1,n}|\leqslant\sum_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\atop|j|,|j^{\prime}|\leqslant C\braket{\ell}}|R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}|\lesssim\gamma_{n+1}^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\sum_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}\braket{\ell}^{-\frac{\tau}{q_{0}}+1}\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}},

(7.65)

where we used the choice of
\tau
in (
7.31
) to ensure the convergence of the series. To estimate the measure of
{\mathtt{I}}_{2,n}
, we need to use Lemma
7.1
-
(i)
. We note that the set of indexes
(\ell,j,j^{\prime})
such that
\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0
and
\min\{|j|,|j^{\prime}|\}<C_{1}\gamma_{n+1}^{-\upsilon}\braket{\ell}^{\tau_{1}}
is included, for
\gamma=\varepsilon^{\varrho}\in(0,1)
small enough, into the set

{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}\triangleq\big\{(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\,:\,\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0,\ |j|,|j^{\prime}|<\gamma_{n+1}^{-2\upsilon}\braket{\ell}^{\tau_{1}}\big\}

(7.66)

because

\max\{|j|,|j^{\prime}|\}\leqslant\min\{|j|,|j^{\prime}|\}+|j-j^{\prime}|<C_{1}\gamma_{n+1}^{-\upsilon}\braket{\ell}^{\tau_{1}}+C\braket{\ell}\leqslant\gamma_{n+1}^{-2\upsilon}\braket{\ell}^{\tau_{1}}.

(7.67)

As a consequence, by Lemma
7.1
-
(i)
, we deduce that

{\mathtt{I}}_{2,n}=\bigcup_{{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0,\,j\neq j^{\prime}}\atop j\cdot j^{\prime}>0}R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\subset\Bigg[\bigcup_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}R_{\ell,-\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell,\kappa}^{({\rm Tr},n)}(i_{n})\Bigg]\cup\Bigg[\bigcup_{(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg].

(7.68)

We compute

\displaystyle\Bigg|\bigcup_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}R_{\ell,-\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell,\kappa}^{({\rm Tr},n)}(i_{n})\Bigg|\leqslant\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}|R_{\ell,-\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell,\kappa}^{({\rm Tr},n)}(i_{n})|\lesssim\gamma_{n+1}^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}\braket{\ell}^{-\frac{\tau_{1}+1}{q_{0}}-1}\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}},

(7.69)

\displaystyle\Bigg|\bigcup_{(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}}R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg|\leqslant\sum_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\atop|j|,|j^{\prime}|<\gamma_{n+1}^{-2\upsilon}\braket{\ell}^{\tau_{1}}}|R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})|\lesssim\gamma_{n+1}^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}-4\upsilon}\sum_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}\braket{\ell}^{-\frac{\tau}{q_{0}}-1+2\tau_{1}}\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}},

(7.70)

where we used the choice of
\tau
and
\upsilon
in (
7.31
) to ensure the convergence of the series, and we obtaing that

|{\mathtt{I}}_{2,n}|\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}.

(7.71)

Finally, we estimate the measure, for any
n=0,1
,
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
, of

\displaystyle\bigcup_{(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{+})\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{-})\atop{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0}}Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})=\Bigg[\bigcup_{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0\atop j\cdot j^{\prime}<0}Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg]\cup\Bigg[\bigcup_{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0\atop j\cdot j^{\prime}>0}Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg].

(7.72)

By Lemma
7.1
-
(ii)
, Lemma
7.2
and (
7.59
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\Bigg|\bigcup_{(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{+})\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{-})\atop{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0}}Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg|\leqslant\sum_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\atop\braket{j,j^{\prime}}\leqslant C\braket{\ell}}|Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}|\lesssim\gamma_{n+1}^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\sum_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}\braket{\ell}^{-\frac{\tau+1}{q_{0}}-1+\frac{1}{q_{0}}+1}\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}},

(7.73)

where we used the choice of
\tau
in (
7.31
) to ensure the convergence of the series. Summing up all the above contributions, we conclude that

|{\mathcal{S}}_{0}|,|{\mathcal{S}}_{1}|\leqslant C\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}.

(7.74)

\blacktriangleright

Estimates of the lower orders
{\mathcal{S}}_{n}
,
n\geqslant 2
, and conclusion.
It remains now to estimate the size of
{\mathcal{S}}_{n}
in (
7.47
) and (
7.39
) for
n\geqslant 2
. In particular, we claim that they can be estimated with decreasing terms of a convergent geometric series: by Lemma
7.4
-
(vi)
one can show that, for
n\geqslant 2

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{n}\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}N_{n-1}^{-{\mathtt{p}}},\quad\textnormal{for some}\quad{\mathtt{p}}\triangleq{\mathtt{p}}(\tau,d)\ll 1.

(7.75)

For instance, we have to estimate, for any
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
and
n\geqslant 2
, using (
7.59
) and (
6.188
),

\displaystyle\Bigg|\bigcup_{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{*}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa})\atop\underset{|\ell|\geqslant N_{n-1}}{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0}}R_{\ell,j,\kappa}^{(I,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma_{n+1}^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\sum_{|j|\leqslant C\braket{\ell}\atop|\ell|\geqslant N_{n-1}}\braket{\ell}^{-\frac{\tau}{q_{0}}-1}

(7.76)

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\sum_{|\ell|\geqslant N_{n-1}}\braket{\ell}^{-\frac{\tau}{q_{0}}}\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}N_{n-1}^{-\frac{\tau}{q_{0}}+1}

(7.77)

having
\frac{\tau}{q_{0}}>d
by (
7.31
). The other terms are estimated analogously with similar arguments as in the previous step, and therefore we omit the details.

Finally, we conclude that the measure in (
7.39
) is given by

\displaystyle|[a_{0},a_{1}]\setminus\mathscr{C}(\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\leqslant{\mathcal{S}}_{0}+{\mathcal{S}}_{1}+\sum_{n\geqslant 2}{\mathcal{S}}_{n}\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}\Big(1+\sum_{n\geqslant 2}N_{n-1}^{-{\mathtt{p}}}\Big)\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}

(7.78)

and we deduce that, together with (
7.4
),

|[a_{0},a_{1}]|\geqslant|\mathscr{C}(\varepsilon)|\geqslant|[a_{0},a_{1}]|-|[a_{0},a_{1}]\setminus\mathscr{C}(\varepsilon)|\geqslant a_{1}-a_{0}-C\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}=a_{1}-a_{0}-C\varepsilon^{\frac{\varrho\upsilon}{q_{0}}}.

(7.79)

The proof of the main claim is concluded.
∎

We now state and prove the lemmata that we used in the above proof for Proposition
7.2
.

Lemma 7.1
.

For any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
small enough, the following assertions are true:

(i)

Let
(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\times({\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{\kappa})^{2}
,
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
. There exists a constant
C_{1}>0
, independent of
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\varepsilon>0
, such that, if
\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0
,
j\cdot j^{\prime}>0
and
\min\{|j|,|j^{\prime}|\}\geqslant C_{1}\gamma_{n+1}^{-\upsilon}\braket{\ell}^{\tau_{1}}
, then
R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\subset R_{\ell,j-j^{\prime},\kappa}^{({\rm Tr},n)}(i_{n})
;

(ii)

Let
(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\times({\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{+})\times({\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{-})
. If
Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}\neq\varnothing
and
j\cdot j^{\prime}>0
, then
\braket{j,j^{\prime}}\leqslant C\braket{\ell}
.

Proof.

(i)
We observe that, by (
3.20
), for any
a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]
,

\displaystyle|\Omega_{j}(a)-ja|=a|j|\bigg|\sqrt{1+\frac{1}{a^{2}j^{2}}}-1\bigg|=\frac{1}{a|j|}\bigg(\sqrt{1+\frac{1}{a^{2}j^{2}}}+1\bigg)^{-1}\leqslant\frac{1}{2a_{0}|j|}\cdot

(7.80)

Let
a\in R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})
. By (
7.51
), (
6.255
), (
7.80
) and (
7.45
) (recalling also the choice of
\upsilon
in Proposition
6.6
and the choice of
\tau>\tau_{1}
in (
7.31
)), we have, for
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
small enough,

\displaystyle\big|\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+(j-j^{\prime}){\mathtt{c}}_{\kappa,n}(a)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\big|\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j^{\prime},\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)\big|

(7.81)

\displaystyle+\big|\kappa(ja-\Omega_{j}(a))-\kappa(j^{\prime}a-\Omega_{j^{\prime}}(a))-{\mathtt{r}}_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)+{\mathtt{r}}_{j^{\prime},\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)\big|

(7.82)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2\gamma_{n+1}\braket{j-j^{\prime}}}{\braket{\ell}^{\tau}}+\Big(\frac{1}{2a_{0}}+C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{|j|}+\frac{1}{|j^{\prime}|}\Big)

(7.83)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\braket{j-j^{\prime}}}{\braket{\ell}^{\tau_{1}}}+\frac{1}{4}\Big(\frac{1}{|j|}+\frac{1}{|j^{\prime}|}\Big)\braket{j-j^{\prime}}\leqslant\frac{4\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\braket{j-j^{\prime}}}{\braket{\ell}^{\tau_{1}}}

(7.84)

if we require that

|j|,|j^{\prime}|\geqslant C_{1}\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\braket{\ell}^{-\tau_{1}},\qquad C_{1}\triangleq 4a_{0},

(7.85)

and the claim is proved.

(ii)
Since
Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}\neq\varnothing
, there exists
a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]
such that, using also (
7.43
), (
7.10
),

\displaystyle\big|\mu_{j,+,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j^{\prime},-,n}^{[\infty]}(a)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{2\gamma_{n+1}\braket{j,j^{\prime}}}{\braket{\ell}^{\tau}}+C|\ell|\leqslant 4\gamma\braket{j,j^{\prime}}+C\braket{\ell}.

(7.86)

By (
7.41
), we have

\displaystyle\mu_{j,+,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j^{\prime},-,n}^{[\infty]}(a)

\displaystyle=\Omega_{j}(a)+\Omega_{j^{\prime}}(a)+j\big({\mathtt{c}}_{+,n}(a)-a\big)-j^{\prime}\big({\mathtt{c}}_{-,n}(a)+a\big)+{\mathfrak{r}}_{j,+,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-{\mathfrak{r}}_{j^{\prime},-,n}^{[\infty]}(a).

(7.87)

Using that
j\cdot j^{\prime}>0
and recalling (
3.20
) (see also Lemma
3.1
-
(iii)
), we get

\displaystyle\big|\Omega_{j}(a)+\Omega_{j^{\prime}}(a)\big|=\Omega_{|j|}(a)+\Omega_{|j^{\prime}|}(a)\geqslant a_{0}\big(|j|+|j^{\prime}|\big)=a_{0}\braket{j,j^{\prime}}.

(7.88)

Therefore, making appeal to the estimates (
7.44
), (
7.45
), we get that

\displaystyle\big|\mu_{j,+,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j^{\prime},-,n}^{[\infty]}(a)\big|

\displaystyle\geqslant\big|\Omega_{j}(a)+\Omega_{j^{\prime}}(a)\big|-C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}

(7.89)

\displaystyle\geqslant(a_{0}-C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1})\braket{j,j^{\prime}}.

Combining (
7.86
) and (
7.89
) and recalling (
7.4
), we conclude the desired result for
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
sufficiently small.
∎

Under the momentum condition, we have some immediate restrictions of the Fourier indexes.

Lemma 7.2
.

If
(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
are such that
\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0
and
j\cdot j^{\prime}<0
, then there exists
C>0
such that
|j|,|j^{\prime}|\leqslant C\braket{\ell}
.

Proof.

For
j\cdot j^{\prime}<0
, the momentum condition reads

j-j^{\prime}={\rm sgn}(j)|j|-{\rm sgn}(j^{\prime})|j^{\prime}|={\rm sgn}(j)(|j|+|j^{\prime}|)=-\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell,

(7.90)

from which we deduce the claim.
∎

The perturbed frequencies satisfy estimates similar to the ones in Lemma
3.3
.

Lemma 7.3

(Perturbed transversality)
.

There exist
\rho_{0}>0
and
q_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that the following hold true.

(i)

For all
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
,

\inf_{a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]}\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{a}^{k}\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell\big|\geqslant\tfrac{\rho_{0}}{2}\langle\ell\rangle.

(ii)

Let
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. For all
(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{*}
, with
\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0
,

\inf_{a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]}\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{a}^{k}\big(\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+j{\mathtt{c}}_{\kappa,n}(a)\big)\big|\geqslant\tfrac{\rho_{0}}{2}\langle\ell\rangle.

(iii)

Let
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. For all
(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{*}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa})
with
\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0,

\inf_{a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]}\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{a}^{k}\big(\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)\big)\big|\geqslant\tfrac{\rho_{0}}{2}\langle\ell\rangle.

(iv)

Let
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. For all
(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{*}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa})^{2}
with
(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\neq(0,j,j)
and
\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0,

\inf_{a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]}\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{a}^{k}\big(\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j^{\prime},\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)\big)\big|\geqslant\tfrac{\rho_{0}}{2}\langle\ell\rangle.

(v)

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. For all
(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{*}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{+})\times(\mathbb{Z}^{*}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{-})
with
\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0
,

\inf_{a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]}\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{a}^{k}\big(\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,+,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j,-,n}^{[\infty]}(a)\big)\big|\geqslant\tfrac{\rho_{0}}{2}\langle\ell\rangle.

Proof.

We prove items
(iv)
,
(v)
. The proofs of items
(i)
,
(ii)
and
(iii)
are similar and therefore omitted. We start with item
(iv)
. By (
7.28
), (
7.41
) we have

\displaystyle\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j^{\prime},\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)

\displaystyle=\omega_{\rm Eq}(a)\cdot\ell+\vec{r}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\kappa\,\Omega_{j}(a)-\kappa\,\Omega_{j^{\prime}}(a)

(7.91)

\displaystyle+(j-j^{\prime})\big({\mathtt{c}}_{\kappa,n}(a)-\kappa a\big)+{\mathtt{r}}_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-{\mathtt{r}}_{j^{\prime},\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a).

(7.92)

By (
7.92
), (
7.43
), (
7.44
), (
7.45
) and the momentum condition
\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0
, we have that, for any
q\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket
,

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{a}^{q}\big(\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j^{\prime},\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)\big)\big|\geqslant\big|\partial_{a}^{q}\big(\omega_{\rm Eq}(a)\cdot\ell+\kappa\,\Omega_{j}(a)-\kappa\,\Omega_{j^{\prime}}(a)\big)\big|-C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1-q_{0}}\braket{\ell}.

(7.93)

Therefore, by Lemma
3.3
-
(iv)
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\inf_{a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]}\max_{q\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{a}^{q}\big(\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j,\kappa,n}^{[\infty]}(a)\big)\big|\geqslant\rho_{0}\braket{\ell}-C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1-q_{0}}\braket{\ell}\geqslant\tfrac{\rho_{0}}{2}\braket{\ell}

(7.94)

for
\varepsilon>0
small enough. This proves item
(iv)
.

We now prove item
(v)
. By (
7.28
), (
7.41
) we have

\displaystyle\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,+,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j^{\prime},-,n}^{[\infty]}(a)

\displaystyle=\omega_{\rm Eq}(a)\cdot\ell+\vec{r}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\Omega_{j}(a)+\Omega_{j^{\prime}}(a)

(7.95)

\displaystyle\quad+j\big({\mathtt{c}}_{+,n}(a)-a\big)-j^{\prime}\big({\mathtt{c}}_{-,n}(a)+a\big)+{\mathtt{r}}_{j,+,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-{\mathtt{r}}_{j^{\prime},-,n}^{[\infty]}(a).

(7.96)

Note that we can restrict to indexes
\ell,j,j^{\prime}
such that
|j|,|j^{\prime}|\leqslant C\braket{\ell}

(when
j\cdot j^{\prime}>0
by Lemma
7.1
, otherwise
Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}=\varnothing
; when
j\cdot j^{\prime}<0
by Lemma
7.2
, using the momentum condition). By (
7.96
), (
7.43
), (
7.44
), (
7.45
) and the restriction
|j|,|j^{\prime}|\leqslant C\braket{\ell}
, we have that, for any
q\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket
,

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{a}^{q}\big(\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,+,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j^{\prime},-,n}^{[\infty]}(a)\big)\big|\geqslant\big|\partial_{a}^{q}\big(\omega_{\rm Eq}(a)\cdot\ell+\Omega_{j}(a)+\Omega_{j^{\prime}}(a)\big)\big|-C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1-q_{0}}\braket{\ell}.

(7.97)

Therefore, by Lemma
3.3
-
(v)
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\inf_{a\in[a_{0},a_{1}]}\max_{q\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{a}^{q}\big(\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)\cdot\ell+\mu_{j,+}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j,-}^{[\infty]}(a)\big)\big|\geqslant\rho_{0}\braket{\ell}-C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1-q_{0}}\braket{\ell}\geqslant\tfrac{\rho_{0}}{2}\braket{\ell}

(7.98)

for
\varepsilon>0
small enough. This proves item
(v)
and concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

In the following lemma, we show that some of the “nearly-resonant set” are empty for lower Fourier modes.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0,1\}
and
\kappa\in\{-,+\}
. The following assertions hold:

(i)

For
\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
, with
|\ell|\leqslant N_{n-1}
, we have
R_{\ell}^{(0,n)}(i_{n})=\varnothing
;

(ii)

For
(\ell,j)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
, with
|\ell|\leqslant N_{n-1}
,
we have
R_{\ell,j,\kappa}^{({\rm Tr},n)}(i_{n})=\varnothing
;

(iii)

For
(\ell,j)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\times({\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{\kappa})
, with
|\ell|\leqslant N_{n-1}
,
we have
R_{\ell,j,\kappa}^{(I,n)}(i_{n})=\varnothing
;

(iv)

For
(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\times({\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{\kappa})^{2}
, with
|\ell|\leqslant N_{n-1}

and
(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\neq(0,j,j)
, we have
R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})=\varnothing
;

(v)

For
(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\times({\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{+})\times({\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\overline{{\mathbb{S}}}_{-})
, with
|\ell|\leqslant N_{n-1}
,
we have
Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})=\varnothing
;

(vi)

The splitting of
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}
in (
7.47
) reduces to

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\bigcup_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{0\}\atop|\ell|\geqslant N_{n-1}}R_{\ell}^{(0,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg]\cup\bigcup_{\kappa\in\{-,+\}}\Bigg[\bigcup_{\underset{|\ell|\geqslant N_{n-1}}{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0)\}\atop\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0}}R_{\ell,j,\kappa}^{({\rm Tr},n)}(i_{n})\Bigg]\cup\bigcup_{\kappa\in\{-,+\}}\Bigg[\bigcup_{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{*}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa})\atop\underset{|\ell|\geqslant N_{n-1}}{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0}}R_{\ell,j,\kappa}^{(I,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg]

(7.99)

\displaystyle\cup\bigcup_{\kappa\in\{-,+\}}\Bigg[\bigcup_{(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{\kappa})^{2}\atop\underset{|\ell|\geqslant N_{n-1}}{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0,\,j\neq j^{\prime}}}R_{\ell,j,j^{\prime},\kappa}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg]\cup\Bigg[\bigcup_{(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{+})\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\overline{\mathbb{S}}_{-})\atop\underset{|\ell|\geqslant N_{n-1}}{\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0}}Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\Bigg].

(7.100)

Proof.

Item
(vi)
is a direct consequence of (
7.47
) and items
(i)
-
(v)
. We prove now item
(v)
. The proofs of items
(i)
-
(iv)
are similar and therefore omitted. First, by Proposition
7.1
-
({\mathcal{P}}2)_{n}
, we have, for any
n\geqslant 2
,

\|i_{n}-i_{n-1}\|_{s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}\leqslant\|{\mathtt{U}}_{n}-{\mathtt{U}}_{n-1}\|_{s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}=\|{\mathtt{H}}_{n}\|_{s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}\leqslant C_{*}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{-\nu_{2}}.

(7.101)

To conclude the claim it is sufficient to prove that
Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\subset Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n-1)}(i_{n-1})
. Indeed, if this holds, then

Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\subset Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})\cap Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n-1)}(i_{n-1})\subset({\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon})\cap({\mathcal{C}}_{n-1}^{\varepsilon}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\varepsilon})=\varnothing.

(7.102)

Let
a\in Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n)}(i_{n})
. By (
7.41
) and triangle inequality, we have that

\big|\vec{\omega}_{\varepsilon}(a)+\mu_{j,+,n-1}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j^{\prime},-,n-1}^{[\infty]}(a)\big|\leqslant\frac{2\gamma_{n+1}\braket{j,j^{\prime}}}{\braket{\ell}^{\tau}}+\varrho_{j,j^{\prime},n}(a,\varepsilon),

(7.103)

where, recalling (
7.41
), (
6.95
) in Proposition
6.6
-
(i)
, (
6.256
) in Proposition
6.9
-
(i)
and (
7.101
),

\displaystyle\varrho_{j,j^{\prime},n}(a,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\triangleq\big|\mu_{j,+,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j,+,n-1}^{[\infty]}(a)-\big(\mu_{j^{\prime},-,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-\mu_{j^{\prime},-,n-1}^{[\infty]}(a)\big)\big|

(7.104)

\displaystyle\leqslant|j|\big|{\mathtt{c}}_{+,n}(a)-{\mathtt{c}}_{+,n-1}(a)\big|+|j^{\prime}|\big|{\mathtt{c}}_{-,n}(a)-{\mathtt{c}}_{-,n-1}(a)\big|

(7.105)

\displaystyle\quad+\big|{\mathtt{r}}_{j,+,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-{\mathtt{r}}_{j,+,n-1}^{[\infty]}(a)\big|+\big|{\mathtt{r}}_{j^{\prime},-,n}^{[\infty]}(a)-{\mathtt{r}}_{j^{\prime},-,n-1}^{[\infty]}(a)\big|

(7.106)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\braket{j,j^{\prime}}\|i_{n}-i_{n-1}\|_{s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{-\nu_{2}}\braket{j,j^{\prime}}.

(7.107)

Therefore, by (
7.103
) and (
7.107
), we deduce that
a\in Q_{\ell,j,j^{\prime}}^{(II,n-1)}(i_{n-1})
if it holds, for any
|\ell|\leqslant N_{n-1}
,

\displaystyle 2\gamma_{n+1}\braket{\ell}^{-\tau}+C\varepsilon^{2}\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{-\nu_{2}}\leqslant 2\gamma_{n}\braket{\ell}^{-\tau},

(7.108)

which is equivalent to ask, by (
7.10
) and for any
|\ell|\leqslant N_{n-1}
, that

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}C(\varepsilon\gamma^{-1})^{2}N_{n-1}^{\tau-\nu_{2}}\leqslant 2^{-n}-2^{-(n+1)}=2^{-(n+1)}.

(7.109)

Recalling the choice of parameters in (
7.2
), (
7.4
) and (
6.188
), the condition in (
7.109
) is equivalent, by taking the logarithm, to

\frac{(\frac{3}{2})^{n-1}}{n+1}(a_{2}-\tau)\log(N_{0})\geqslant\log(2),

(7.110)

which is satisfied for
N_{0}>1
large enough, uniformly in
n\geqslant 2
. This concludes the proof of Lemma
7.4
.
∎

Appendix A
Functional tools

In this appendix, we gather all the technical material used throughout the document. In particular, we introduce the functions spaces, the pseudo-differential calculus and we discuss a useful Egorov Theorem for homogeneous operators.

Along the manuscript, we use the notations

\mathbb{N}\triangleq\{0,1,2,\ldots\},\qquad\mathbb{N}\triangleq\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\},\qquad\mathbb{Z}\triangleq\mathbb{N}\cup(-\mathbb{N}),\qquad\mathbb{Z}\triangleq\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}

and for
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*},

\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket\triangleq\{1,2,\ldots,N\}.
