Throughout the whole article, the following parameters are fixed.

d,q\in\mathbb{N}^{*},\qquad\gamma\in(0,1),\qquad S\gg s_{0}\geqslant\frac{d+1}{2}+q+2.

(A.1)

Let us consider
\mathcal{O}
an open bounded subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d+1}.
The set
\mathcal{O}
is precised in (
5.2
).
Along the manuscript, we use the notation
A\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta,...}B
to designate
A\leqslant CB
, with
C
a constant depending only on the parameters
\alpha,\beta,...

A.1
Function spaces

We consider the space
L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C})
of square integrable functions, which is a Hilbert space endowed with the Hermitian scalar product

\forall u_{1},u_{2}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C}),\quad\langle u_{1},u_{2}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C})}\triangleq\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d+1}}u_{1}(\varphi,x)\overline{u_{2}(\varphi,x)}{\rm d}\varphi{\rm d}x.

This space admits a Hilbert basis
(\mathbf{e}_{\ell,j})_{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}}
given by

\mathbf{e}_{\ell,j}(\varphi,x)\triangleq e^{{\rm i}(\ell\cdot\varphi+jx)}.

A function
u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C})
can be decomposed into Fourier series

u=\sum_{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}}u_{\ell,j}\mathbf{e}_{\ell,j},\qquad u_{\ell,j}\triangleq\langle u,\mathbf{e}_{\ell,j}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C})}.

(A.2)

For a given
s\geqslant 0,
we define the Sobolove space of regularity
s
by

H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C})\triangleq\Big\{u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C})\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\|u\|_{s}<\infty\Big\},\qquad\|u\|_{s}^{2}\triangleq\sum_{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}}\langle\ell,j\rangle^{2s}|u_{\ell,j}|^{2}.

(A.3)

Here for
\ell=(\ell_{1},...,\ell_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
j\in\mathbb{Z},
we denote

|\ell|\triangleq|\ell_{1}|+...+|\ell_{d}|\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad\langle\ell,j\rangle\triangleq\max(1,|l|,|j|).

The closed sub-vector space of real valued functions is denoted

\displaystyle H^{s}\triangleq H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{R})

\displaystyle\triangleq\left\{\rho\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C})\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\forall\,(\varphi,x)\in\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\,u(\varphi,x)=\overline{u(\varphi,x)}\right\}

\displaystyle=\Big\{u\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C})\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\forall\,(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1},\,u_{-\ell,-j}=\overline{u_{\ell,j}}\Big\}.

We shall also consider functions depending on a parameter
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d+1}
and taken among the following function spaces

\displaystyle W^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big(\mathcal{O},H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C})\big)

\displaystyle\triangleq\big\{u:\mathcal{O}\to H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C})\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\|u\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}<\infty\big\},

\displaystyle W^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{O},\mathbb{C})

\displaystyle\triangleq\big\{u:\mathcal{O}\to\mathbb{C}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\|u\|^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}<\infty\big\},

associated with the norms

\displaystyle\|u\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\triangleq\max_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1}\atop|\alpha|\leqslant q}\gamma^{|\alpha|}\sup_{\lambda\in\mathcal{O}}\|(\partial_{\lambda}^{\alpha}u)(\lambda,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{s-|\alpha|},\quad\|u\|^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\triangleq\max_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1}\atop|\alpha|\leqslant q}\gamma^{|\alpha|}\sup_{\lambda\in\mathcal{O}}|(\partial_{\lambda}^{\alpha}u)(\lambda)|.

The following lemma gathers the classical estimates.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
(d,\gamma,q,s_{0})
as in (
A.1
) and
s\geqslant s_{0}
. Take
u_{1},u_{2}\in W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H^{s}).
Then the following properties hold true:

(i)

(Product law): We have
u_{1}u_{2}\in W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H^{s})
and

\|u_{1}u_{2}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|u_{1}\|_{s_{0}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|u_{2}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|u_{1}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|u_{2}\|_{s_{0}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}};

(ii)

(Composition law): For
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{O}\times\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R})
, if there exists
\mathtt{M}>0
such that

\|u_{1}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},\|u_{2}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\mathtt{M},

then
f(u_{1})-f(u_{2})\in W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H^{s})
with

\|f(u_{1})-f(u_{2})\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C(s,d,q,f,\mathtt{M})\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

where we used the notation

\forall\,(\lambda,\varphi,x)\in\mathcal{O}\times\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\quad f(u)\equiv f(u)(\lambda,\varphi,x)\triangleq f(\lambda,u(\lambda,\varphi,x)).

A.2
Reversible and traveling quasi-periodic waves

Let
\mathscr{S}
by the involution defined in (
3.14
) acting on the real variable
r=(r_{+},r_{-})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
.

Definition A.1
.

A function
r:\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
is called:

•

reversible if, for any
{\varphi}\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
,
\mathscr{S}r({\varphi},\,\cdot\,)=r(-{\varphi},\,\cdot\,)
;

•

anti-reversible if, for any
{\varphi}\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
,
-\mathscr{S}r({\varphi},\,\cdot\,)=r(-{\varphi},\,\cdot\,)
.

With slight modifications, we recall the definition of traveling quasi-periodic waves given in Definition
1.1

Definition A.2
.

(Quasi-periodic traveling waves)

Let
d\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
and
\vec{\jmath}\triangleq(\bar{\jmath}_{1},...,\bar{\jmath}_{d})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
. A function
r:{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times\mathbb{T}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{K}
,
K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
, is called quasi-periodic traveling with velocity vector
\vec{\jmath}
if there exists a
(2\pi)^{d}
-periodic function
R:\mathbb{T}^{d}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{K}
such that
r({\varphi},x)=R({\varphi}-\vec{\jmath}x)
.

Comparing with Definition
1.1
, we find convenient to call
traveling quasi-periodic
wave both the function
r({\varphi},x)=R({\varphi}-\vec{\jmath}x)
and the function of time
r(t,x)=R(\omega t-\vec{\jmath}x)
. Quasi-periodic traveling waves are characterized by the relation

\forall\,y\in{\mathbb{R}},\quad r({\varphi}-\vec{\jmath}y,\,\cdot\,)=\mathscr{T}_{y}r,

(A.4)

where
\mathscr{T}_{y}
is the translation operator in (
3.14
). Product and composition of quasi-periodic traveling waves are quasi-periodic traveling waves.
Expanded in Fourier series as in (
A.2
), a quasi-periodic traveling wave has the form

r({\varphi},x)=\sum_{(\ell,j)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d+1}\atop\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0}r_{\ell,j}{\bf e}_{\ell,j}({\varphi},x),

(A.5)

namely, comparing with Definition
A.2
,

r({\varphi},x)=R({\varphi}-\vec{\jmath}x),\qquad R(\psi)=\sum_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}R_{\ell}e^{{\rm i}\ell\cdot\psi},\qquad R_{\ell}=r_{\ell,-\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell}.

(A.6)

The traveling waves
r({\varphi},x)=R({\varphi}-\vec{\jmath}x)
, where
R(\,\cdot\,)
belongs to the Sobolev space
H^{s}({\mathbb{T}}^{d},{\mathbb{C}}^{K})
as in (
1.14
) (with values in
{\mathbb{C}}^{K}
,
K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
)
form a subspace of the Sobolev space
H^{s}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+1},{\mathbb{C}}^{K})
as in (
A.3
) (with values in
{\mathbb{C}}^{K}
,
K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
). Note the equivalence of the norms (use (
A.6
))

\|r\|_{H^{s}({\mathbb{T}}_{{\varphi}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{T}}_{x})}\simeq_{s}\|R\|_{H^{s}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}.

(A.7)

For
N\geqslant 1
, we define the smoothing operator
\Pi_{N}
on the traveling waves

\Pi_{N}:r=\sum_{(\ell,j)\in\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d+1}\atop\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0}r_{\ell,j}{\bf e}_{\ell,j}\mapsto\Pi_{N}r\triangleq\sum_{(\ell,j)\in\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d+1},\ \braket{\ell}\leqslant N\atop\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j=0}r_{\ell,j}{\bf e}_{\ell,j},\qquad\Pi_{N}^{\perp}\triangleq{\rm Id}-\Pi_{N}.

(A.8)

Note that, writing a traveling wave as in (
A.6
), the projector
\Pi_{N}
in (
A.8
) is equal to

(\Pi_{N}r)({\varphi},x)=R_{N}({\varphi}-\vec{\jmath}x),\qquad R_{N}(\psi)\triangleq\sum_{\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\atop\braket{\ell}\leqslant N}R_{\ell}e^{{\rm i}\ell\cdot\psi}.

(A.9)

The following smoothing estimates are classical.

Lemma A.2
.

(Smoothing).

Let
(d,\gamma,q)
as in (
A.1
). For any
N\geqslant 1
and any traveling wave
r
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{N}r\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant N^{\alpha}\|r\|_{s-\alpha}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}},\quad 0\leqslant\alpha\leqslant s,

(A.10)

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{N}^{\perp}r\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant N^{-\alpha}\|r\|_{s+\alpha}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}},\quad\alpha\geqslant 0.

(A.11)

A.3
Linear operators

Here we give the basic notations for our functional framework together. Then, we introduce the notion of
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-
\sigma
-tame and
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-modulo-tame operators useful for the reduction of the linearized operator.

A.3.1
Basic notations for functions and operators

Throughout the document, we consider
\varphi
-dependent families of operators acting on
L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C})
functions as follows

(Au)(\varphi,x)\triangleq\big(A(\varphi)u(\varphi,\cdot)\big)(x).

In Fourier, we can write this action

(Au)(\varphi,x)=\sum_{(\ell,\ell^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2}\atop(j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}A_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell-\ell^{\prime})u_{\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}}e^{{\rm i}(\ell\cdot\varphi+jx)}.

(A.12)

This allows to identify the operator
A
with the matrix
\big(A_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell-\ell^{\prime})\big)_{(\ell,\ell^{\prime},j,j^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}}
which is Töplitz with respect to the index
\ell.
Given
A
and
B
two operators, we denote:

•

[A,B]\triangleq AB-BA
the commutator and
[A,B]_{\rm a}\triangleq AB+BA
the anti-commutator;

•

|A|
the majorant operator, defined by

(|A|u)(\varphi,x)\triangleq\sum_{(\ell,\ell^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2}\atop(j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\big|A_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell-\ell^{\prime})\big|u_{\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}}e^{{\rm i}(\ell\cdot\varphi+jx)};

•

\lfloor A\rfloor
the diagonal component, defined by

\left(\lfloor A\rfloor u\right)(\varphi,x)\triangleq\sum_{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}}A_{j}^{j}(0)u_{\ell,j}e^{{\rm i}(\ell\cdot\varphi+jx)};

(A.13)

•

Given
N\in\mathbb{N}
, the projected operator
\Pi_{N}A
, defined by

(\Pi_{N}Au)(\varphi,x)\triangleq\sum_{(\ell,\ell^{\prime},j,j^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}\atop|\ell-\ell^{\prime}|\leqslant N}A_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell-\ell^{\prime})u_{\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}}e^{{\rm i}(\ell\cdot\varphi+jx)};

(A.14)

•

Given
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
k\in\llbracket 0,d\rrbracket,
the operators
\partial_{\varphi_{k}}^{l}A
and
\langle\partial_{\varphi}\rangle^{l}A
, defined respectively by

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{\varphi_{k}}^{l}Au\big)(\varphi,x)

\displaystyle\triangleq\sum_{(\ell,\ell^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2}\atop(j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(\ell_{k}-\ell_{k}^{\prime})^{l}A_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell-\ell^{\prime})u_{\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}}e^{{\rm i}(\ell\cdot\varphi+jx)},

(A.15)

\displaystyle\big(\langle\partial_{\varphi}\rangle^{l}Au\big)(\varphi,x)

\displaystyle\triangleq\sum_{(\ell,\ell^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2}\atop(j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\langle\ell-\ell^{\prime}\rangle^{l}A_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell-\ell^{\prime})u_{\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}}e^{{\rm i}(\ell\cdot\varphi+jx)};

(A.16)

•

Given
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{R},
the operator
\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}A\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}
, defined by

\left(\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}A\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}u\right)(\varphi,x)\triangleq\sum_{(\ell,\ell^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2}\atop(j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\langle j\rangle^{n_{1}}A_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell-\ell^{\prime})\langle j^{\prime}\rangle^{n_{2}}u_{\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}}e^{{\rm i}(\ell\cdot\varphi+jx)}.

We distinguish symmetry properties of linear operators based on reality properties, and on their behavious with the involution
\mathscr{S}
and the translation operator
\mathscr{T}_{y}
,
y\in{\mathbb{R}}
, (see their definition in (
3.14
)).

Definition A.3
.

A
\varphi
-dependent family of operators
\varphi\mapsto A(\varphi)
is said to be:

•

real if, for any
u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C})
such that
\overline{u}=u
, it holds
\overline{Au}=Au
;

•

reversible if, for any
{\varphi}\in{\mathbb{T}}
, it holds
A(-\varphi)\circ\mathscr{S}=-\mathscr{S}\circ A(\varphi)
;

•

reversibility preserving if, for any
{\varphi}\in{\mathbb{T}}
, it holds
A(-\varphi)\circ\mathscr{S}=\mathscr{S}\circ A(\varphi)
;

•

momentum preserving if, for any
{\varphi}\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
and
y\in{\mathbb{R}}
, it holds
A(\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y)\circ\mathscr{T}_{y}=\mathscr{T}_{y}\circ A(\varphi)
.

Remark A.1
.

Let us make the following remarks:

•

Note that a reversible operator sends reversible function onto anti-reversible functions and vice-versa, in the sense of Definition
A.1
. Conversely, a reversibility preserving operator let invariant such structures. Besides, a momentum preserving operator sends quasi-periodic traveling waves with velocity vector
\vec{\jmath}
onto quasi-periodic traveling waves with velocity vector
\vec{\jmath}
.

•

The composition of two reversible or reversibility preserving operators is reversibility preserving. The composition of a reversible operator with a reversibility preserving one is reversible. The composition of two momentum preserving operators is momentum preserving.

Operators with symmetries as in Definition
A.3
are characterized with in Fourier by means of the expansion in (
A.12
).

Lemma A.3
.

A
\varphi
-dependent family of operators
\varphi\mapsto A(\varphi)
is:

•

real if and only if, for all
(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, it holds
A_{-j}^{-j^{\prime}}(-\ell)=\overline{A_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell)}
;

•

reversible if and only if, for all
(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, it holds
A_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell)=-\overline{A_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell)}
;

•

reversibility preserving if and only if, for all
(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, it holds
A_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell)=\overline{A_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell)}
;

•

momentum preserving if and only if

\forall\,(\ell,j,j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2},\qquad A_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell)\neq 0\quad\Rightarrow\quad\vec{\jmath}\cdot\ell+j-j^{\prime}=0.

(A.17)

In our analysis, we shall also consider matrices of operators acting on

\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C}^{2})\triangleq L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C})\times L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1},\mathbb{C})

and taking the form

\mathbf{A}=\begin{pmatrix}A_{+}^{(d)}&A_{+}^{(o)}\vskip 2.84544pt\\
A_{-}^{(o)}&A_{-}^{(d)}\end{pmatrix}.

(A.18)

All the previous definitions naturally extend to matrix operators
{\bf A}
as in (
A.18
), in particular:

•

A matrix operator
\mathbf{A}
in the form (
A.18
) is said to be
real
(resp.
reversible
,
reversibility preserving
,
momentum preserving
) if each entry
A_{+}^{(d)},

A_{+}^{(o)},

A_{-}^{(o)},

A_{-}^{(d)}
is real (resp. reversible, reversibility preserving, momentum preserving);

•

We denote

\lfloor\mathbf{A}\rfloor\triangleq\begin{pmatrix}\lfloor A_{+}^{(d)}\rfloor&0\\
0&\lfloor A_{-}^{(d)}\rfloor\end{pmatrix},

(A.19)

and, for
N\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
,

\Pi_{N}\mathbf{A}=\begin{pmatrix}\Pi_{N}A_{+}^{(d)}&\Pi_{N}A_{+}^{(o)}\vskip 2.84544pt\\
\Pi_{N}A_{-}^{(o)}&\Pi_{N}A_{-}^{(d)}\end{pmatrix}.

(A.20)

A.3.2
\mathcal{D}^{q}
(-modulo) tame operators

In our analysis, the operators might depend on an external parameter
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}.
We shall emphasize this dependence by denoting
A=A(\lambda).
In this subsection, we recall the formalism of (modulo)-tame operator with
q
derivatives with respect to the external parameter
\lambda
. This formalism was introduced in
[
17
, Section 2.2]
and is used for the KAM reduction of Section
6.5
. It is a rather general framework capturing the tame estimates for operators, essential property for running KAM reduction schemes.

Definition A.4
.

(
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-
\sigma
-tame operator)

Let
\sigma\geqslant 0.
We say that a linear operator
A
is
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-
\sigma
-tame if there exists a non-decreasing function

\begin{array}[]{rcl}[s_{0},S]&\rightarrow&\mathbb{R}_{+}\\
s&\mapsto&\mathfrak{M}_{A}(q,\sigma,s)\triangleq\mathfrak{M}_{A}(s)\end{array}

such that for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
and any
u\in H^{s+\sigma}
, we have

\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1}\atop|\alpha|\leqslant q}\sup_{\lambda\in\mathcal{O}}\gamma^{|\alpha|}\big\|\big(\partial_{\lambda}^{\alpha}A(\lambda)\big)u\big\|_{s}\leqslant\mathfrak{M}_{A}(s_{0})\|u\|_{s+\sigma}+\mathfrak{M}_{A}(s)\|u\|_{s_{0}+\sigma}.

In this case, the function
\mathfrak{M}_{A}
is called tame constant of the operator
A
. The
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-
0
-tame operators are simply called
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-tame operators.

Remark A.2
.

Representing
A
by its
matrix elements
\big(A_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell-\ell^{\prime})\big)_{(\ell,\ell^{\prime},j,j^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}}
as in (
A.12
), we have for all
|k|\leqslant q,

j^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\ell^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},

\gamma^{2|k|}\sum_{(\ell,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}}\langle\ell,j\rangle^{2s}|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}A_{j}^{j^{\prime}}(\ell-\ell^{\prime})|^{2}\leqslant 2{\mathfrak{M}}_{A}^{2}(s_{0})\langle\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle^{2(s+\sigma)}+2{\mathfrak{M}}_{A}^{2}(s)\langle\ell^{\prime},j^{\prime}\rangle^{2(s_{0}+\sigma)}.

(A.21)

Proposition A.1
.

(Properties of
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-
\sigma
-tame operators)

The following properties hold true:

(i)

(Composition): Let
A
(resp.
B
) be a
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-
\sigma_{A}
-tame (resp.
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-
\sigma_{B}
-tame) operator. Then
AB
is a
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-
\sigma_{A}+\sigma_{B}
-tame operator with

\mathfrak{M}_{AB}(s)\lesssim_{q}\mathfrak{M}_{A}(s_{0})\mathfrak{M}_{B}(s+\sigma_{A})+\mathfrak{M}_{A}(s)\mathfrak{M}_{B}(s_{0}+\sigma_{A}).

(ii)

(Link with operator norm): For any
u\in W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},H^{s+\sigma})
, we have

\|Au\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim_{q}\mathfrak{M}_{A}(s_{0})\|u\|_{s+\sigma}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\mathfrak{M}_{A}(s)\|u\|_{s_{0}+\sigma}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

This means

\|A\|_{\mathcal{L}\big(W^{q,\gamma,\infty}(\mathcal{O},H^{s+\sigma}),W^{q,\gamma,\infty}(\mathcal{O},H^{s})\big)}\lesssim\mathfrak{M}_{A}(s).

Definition A.5
.

(
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-
(-1)
-modulo-tame operator)

We say that a linear operator
A=A(\lambda)
is
{\mathcal{D}}^{q}
-
(-1)
-modulo-tame if there exists a non-decreasing function

\begin{array}[]{rcl}[s_{0},S]&\rightarrow&\mathbb{R}_{+},\\
s&\mapsto&{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}A\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s),\end{array}

(A.22)

such that, for all
k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{d+1}
,
|k|\leqslant q
, the majorant operator
\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}A\big|\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}
satisfies, for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
and any
u\in H^{s}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d+1}\atop|\alpha|\leqslant q}\sup_{\lambda\in\mathcal{O}}\gamma^{|\alpha|}\big\|\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}A\big|\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}u\big\|_{s}\leqslant{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}A\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s_{0})\|u\|_{s}+{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}A\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s)\|u\|_{s_{0}}.

(A.23)

In this case, the function
\mathfrak{M}_{\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}A\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}
is called modulo-tame constant of the operator
A
. For a matrix
{\bf A}
as in (
A.18
), we denote

{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf A}\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s)\triangleq\max_{\kappa\in\{-,+\}}\Big\{{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{\kappa}^{(d)}\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s),{\mathfrak{M}}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{\kappa}^{(o)}\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s)\Big\}.

(A.24)

Proposition A.2
.

(Properties of
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-modulo-tame operators)

Let
A
and
B
be two
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-modulo-tame operators. Then, the following properties hold true:

(i)

(Sum) The operator
A+B
is
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-(
-1
)-modulo-tame with, for any
s\in[s_{0},S],

\mathfrak{M}_{\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}(A+B)\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s)\leqslant\mathfrak{M}_{\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}A\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s)+\mathfrak{M}_{\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}B\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s);

(ii)

(Composition) For any
\mathtt{b}\in\mathbb{N}
, the operator
\langle\partial_{\varphi}\rangle^{\mathtt{b}}(AB)
is
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-(
-1
)-modulo-tame with, for any
s\in[s_{0},S],

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\partial_{\varphi}\rangle^{\mathtt{b}}(AB)\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{q,\mathtt{b}}\mathfrak{M}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\partial_{\varphi}\rangle^{\mathtt{b}}A\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s)\mathfrak{M}_{\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}B\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s_{0})+\mathfrak{M}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\partial_{\varphi}\rangle^{\mathtt{b}}A\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s_{0})\mathfrak{M}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}B\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s)

(A.25)

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{M}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}A\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s)\mathfrak{M}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\partial_{\varphi}\rangle^{\mathtt{b}}B\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s_{0})+\mathfrak{M}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}A\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s_{0})\mathfrak{M}_{\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\partial_{\varphi}\rangle^{\mathtt{b}}B\braket{D}^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s);

(A.26)

(iii)

(Projection) For any
N\in\mathbb{N},
the operator
\Pi_{N}^{\perp}A
is
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-(
-1
)-modulo-tame with, for any
\mathtt{b}\in\mathbb{N}
and any
s\in[s_{0},S],

\mathfrak{M}_{\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\Pi_{N}^{\perp}A\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s)\leqslant N^{-\mathtt{b}}\mathfrak{M}_{\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\partial_{\varphi}\rangle^{\mathtt{b}}A\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s);

(iv)

A
is also a
{\mathcal{D}}^{q}
-tame operator, with, for any
s\in[s_{0},S],

{\mathfrak{M}}_{A}(s)\leqslant{\mathfrak{M}}_{\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}A\langle D\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}^{\sharp}(s).

A.4
Pseudo-differential calculus

In this last subsection, we introduce the basic definitions and properties for pseudo-differential operators. We also focus on the particular class of pseudo-differential operators, useful in our study, given by homogeneous expansions. Finally, we recall the Egorov theorem for homogeneous operators.

A.4.1
Pseudo-differential operators

Definition A.6
.

A pseudo-differential symbol
\mathtt{a}(\varphi,x,j)
of order
m
is the restriction to
\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}
of a function
\mathtt{a}(\varphi,x,\xi)
which is of class
C^{\infty}
on
\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}
,
2\pi
-periodic in
x
, and satisfying

\forall\,(\alpha,\beta)\in\mathbb{N}^{2},\qquad\exists\,C_{\alpha,\beta}>0,\qquad\Big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\mathtt{a}(\varphi,x,\xi)\Big|\leqslant C_{\alpha,\beta}\langle\xi\rangle^{m-\beta}.

We denote by
S^{m}
the class of symbols of order
m
and
S^{-\infty}=\displaystyle\bigcap_{m\in\mathbb{N}}S^{m}.
To a symbol
\mathtt{a}(x,\xi)
in
S^{m}
we associate its quantization
\textnormal{Op}(\mathtt{a})
defined by

u(\varphi,x)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}u_{j}(\varphi)e^{{\rm i}jx}\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\big[\textnormal{Op}(\mathtt{a})u\big](\varphi,x)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathtt{a}(\varphi,x,j)u_{j}(\varphi)e^{{\rm i}jx}.

Throughout this paper, we may denote either
\textnormal{Op}(\mathtt{a})
or
\mathtt{a}(x,D)
the pseudo-differential operator associated with the symbol
\mathtt{a}(x,\xi).

We denote by
OPS^{m}
the set of pseudo-differential operators of order
m
and
OPS^{-\infty}=\displaystyle\bigcap_{m\in\mathbb{R}}OPS^{m}.

\|A\|_{m,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\triangleq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\atop|k|\leqslant q}\gamma^{|k|}\sup_{\lambda\in\mathcal{O}}\|\partial_{\lambda}^{k}A(\lambda)\|_{m,s-|k|,\alpha},\qquad\|A(\lambda)\|_{m,s,\alpha}\triangleq\max_{0\leqslant\beta\leqslant\alpha}\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}\,\langle\xi\rangle^{\beta-m}\|\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\mathtt{a}(\lambda;\cdot,\cdot,\xi)\|_{H^{s}}.

(A.27)

We also say that a
2\times 2
-matrix operator
{\bf A}
in the form (
A.18
)
is a pseudo-differential operator in the
OPS^{m}
if
A_{+}^{(d)},A_{+}^{(o)},A_{-}^{(o)},A_{-}^{(d)}\in OPS^{m}
, and its norm is given by

\|{\bf A}\|_{m,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma{\mathcal{O}}}\triangleq\max\big\{\|A_{\kappa}^{(\nu)}\|_{m,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,{\mathcal{O}}},\,\,\kappa\in\{-,+\},\,\,\nu\in\{o,d\}\big\}.

(A.28)

In the next proposition, we gather all the important algebraic and analytic properties of pseudo-differential operators which are applied all along the Section
3
. For their proofs, we refer for instance to
[
17
, Section 2]
and
[
13
, Section 3]
.

Proposition A.3
.

Let
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}.
Consider two pseudo-differential operators
A(\lambda)=\textnormal{Op}\big(\mathtt{a}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi)\big)\in OPS^{m}
and
B(\lambda)=\textnormal{Op}\big(\mathtt{b}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi)\big)\in OPS^{m^{\prime}}.
The following properties hold:

(i)

(Composition) The operator
AB\triangleq A\circ B
is pseudo-differential with symbol
\mathtt{a}\#\mathtt{b}\in S^{m+m^{\prime}}
in the form : for any
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*},

\mathtt{a}\#\mathtt{b}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi)\triangleq\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\frac{1}{k!{\rm i}^{k}}\partial_{\xi}^{k}\mathtt{a}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi)\partial_{x}^{k}\mathtt{b}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi)+r_{N}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi),\qquad r_{N}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi)\in S^{m+m^{\prime}-N}.

In addition, denoting
R_{N}\triangleq\textnormal{Op}(r_{N}),
we have for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
and any
\alpha\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle\|AB\|_{m+m^{\prime},s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|A\|_{m,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|B\|_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+\alpha+|m|,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|A\|_{m,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|B\|_{m^{\prime},s+\alpha+|m|,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(A.29)

\displaystyle\|R_{N}\|_{m+m^{\prime}-N,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{N!}\Big(\|A\|_{m,s,N+\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|B\|_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+2N+\alpha+|m|,N+\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|A\|_{m,s,N+\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|B\|_{m^{\prime},s+2N+\alpha+|m|,N+\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big).

Finally, the remainder symbol expresses as

r_{N}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi)=\frac{1}{{\rm i}^{N}(N-1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\tau)^{N-1}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}(\partial_{\xi}^{N}\mathtt{a})(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi+\tau j)(\widehat{\partial_{x}^{N}\mathtt{b}})(\lambda;\varphi,j,\xi)e^{{\rm i}jx}{\rm d}\tau;

(ii)

(Commutator) The operator
[A,B]\triangleq AB-BA
is pseudo-differential with symbol
\mathtt{a}\star\mathtt{b}\in S^{m+m^{\prime}-1}
in the form : for any
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*},

\mathtt{a}\star\mathtt{b}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi)\triangleq-{\rm i}\{\mathtt{a},\mathtt{b}\}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi)+\widetilde{r}_{2}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi),\qquad\{\mathtt{a},\mathtt{b}\}\triangleq\partial_{\xi}\mathtt{a}\partial_{x}\mathtt{b}-\partial_{x}\mathtt{a}\partial_{\xi}\mathtt{b},\qquad\widetilde{r}_{2}\in S^{m+m^{\prime}-2}.

In addition, we have for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
and any
\alpha\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle\|[A,B]\|_{m+m^{\prime}-1,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|A\|_{m,s+|m^{\prime}|+\alpha+2,\alpha+1}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|B\|_{m^{\prime},s_{0}+|m|+\alpha+2,\alpha+1}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|A\|_{m,s+|m^{\prime}|+\alpha+2,\alpha+1}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|B\|_{m^{\prime},s+|m|+\alpha+2,\alpha+1}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}};

(iii)

(Multiplication operator) If
\mathtt{a}(\lambda;\varphi,x,D)=\mathtt{a}(\lambda;\varphi,x),
then for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
and any
\alpha\in\mathbb{N},

\|A\|_{0,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}=\|\mathtt{a}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(A.30)

(iv)

(Fourier multiplier) If
\mathtt{a}(\lambda;\varphi,x,D)=\mathtt{a}(\lambda;D),
then for any
s\in[s_{0},S]
and any
\alpha\in\mathbb{N},

\|A\|_{m,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}=\|A\|_{m,0,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}};

(A.31)

(v)

(Link with tame constants) For any
s\in[s_{0},S],

\mathfrak{M}_{A}(s)\lesssim_{s}\|A\|_{0,s,0}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}};

(vi)

(Reversibility and momentum preserving properties)

•

The operator
A
is reversible if and only if

\mathtt{a}(\lambda;-\varphi,-x,\xi)=-\overline{\mathtt{a}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi)};

•

The operator
A
is reversibility preserving if and only if

\mathtt{a}(\lambda;-\varphi,-x,\xi)=\overline{\mathtt{a}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi)};

•

The operator
A
is momentum preserving if and only if

\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\mathtt{a}(\lambda;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,x,\xi)=\mathtt{a}(\lambda;\varphi,x+y,\xi).

We now introduce the notion of homogeneous symbols/operators used throughout the article.

Definition A.7
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}.
Consider a pseudo-differential operator
A(\lambda)=\textnormal{Op}\big(\mathtt{a}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi)\big)\in OPS^{m}
. We say that:

(i)

A
is homogeneous of degree
m
if it can be written as

A=\mathfrak{p}\partial_{x}^{m},\qquad\textnormal{i.e.}\qquad\mathtt{a}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi)=\mathfrak{p}(\lambda;\varphi,x)\chi(\xi)({\rm i}\xi)^{m},

with
\mathfrak{p}
a real valued function;

(ii)

A
has an homogeneous expansion of degree
m
up to order
m-N
if it can be written as

A=\sum_{k=0}^{N}\mathfrak{p}_{m-k}\partial_{x}^{m-k}\qquad\textnormal{i.e.}\qquad\mathtt{a}(\lambda;\varphi,x,\xi)=\sum_{k=0}^{N}\mathfrak{p}_{m-k}(\lambda;\varphi,x)\chi(\xi)({\rm i}\xi)^{m-k},

with
(\mathfrak{p}_{m-k})_{0\leqslant k\leqslant N}
real valued functions.

One has the following result.

Lemma A.4
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}.
Consider a pseudo-differential operator
A

in the form

A=\sum_{k=0}^{N}\mathfrak{p}_{m-k}\partial_{x}^{m-k}+R_{N},

(A.32)

with
(\mathfrak{p}_{m-k})_{0\leqslant k\leqslant N}
real valued functions and
R_{N}={\rm Op}({\mathtt{r}}_{n}(\lambda;{\varphi},x,\xi))\in OPS^{m-N-1}
.
Then:

•

The operator
A
is reversible if and only if

\displaystyle\forall\,k\in\llbracket 0,N\rrbracket,\quad

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{m-k}(\lambda;-\varphi,-x)=(-1)^{m-k+1}\mathfrak{p}_{m-k}(\lambda;\varphi,x),

(A.33)

and

\displaystyle{\mathtt{r}}_{N}(\lambda;-{\varphi},-x,\xi)=-\overline{{\mathtt{r}}_{N}(\lambda;{\varphi},x,\xi)};

(A.34)

•

The operator
A
is reversibility preserving if and only if

\displaystyle\forall\,k\in\llbracket 0,N\rrbracket,\quad

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{m-k}(\lambda;-\varphi,-x)=(-1)^{m-k}\mathfrak{p}_{m-k}(\lambda;\varphi,x),

(A.35)

and

\displaystyle{\mathtt{r}}_{N}(\lambda;-{\varphi},-x,\xi)=\overline{{\mathtt{r}}_{N}(\lambda;{\varphi},x,\xi)};

(A.36)

•

The operator
A
is momentum preserving if and only if

\displaystyle\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\forall\,k\in\llbracket 0,N\rrbracket,\quad

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{m-k}(\lambda;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,x)=\mathfrak{p}_{m-k}(\lambda;\varphi,x+y),

(A.37)

and

\displaystyle{\mathtt{r}}_{N}(\lambda;{\varphi},-\vec{\jmath}y,x,\xi)={\mathtt{r}}_{N}(\lambda;{\varphi},x+y,\xi).

(A.38)

Proof.

We only prove item
(i)
, as the proof for items
(ii)
and
(iii)
follow similarly. Moreover, for sake of simplicity, in what follows we omit to write the dependence on
\lambda
that plays no role here. Moreover, we prove only the direct implication, the converse one being trivial. The operator
A
in (
A.32
) is a pseudo-differential operator
A={\rm Op}({\mathtt{a}}({\varphi},x,\xi))\in OPS^{m}
with symbol

{\mathtt{a}}({\varphi},x,\xi)\triangleq\sum_{k=0}^{N}{\mathfrak{p}}_{m-k}({\varphi},x)({\rm i}\xi)^{m-k}+{\mathtt{r}}_{N}({\varphi},x,\xi).

(A.39)

Since we are assuming that
A
is reversible, then, by Proposition
A.3
-
(vi)
, we have that

{\mathtt{a}}(-{\varphi},x,\xi)=-\overline{{\mathtt{a}}({\varphi},x,\xi)}.

(A.40)

In particular, it holds that

\lim_{\xi\to\infty}({\rm i}\xi)^{-m}\big({\mathtt{a}}(-{\varphi},x,\xi)+\overline{{\mathtt{a}}({\varphi},x,\xi)}\big)=0.

(A.41)

By (
A.39
), it follows that

\displaystyle({\rm i}\xi)^{-m}\big({\mathtt{a}}(-{\varphi},x,\xi)+\overline{{\mathtt{a}}({\varphi},x,\xi)}\big)

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{p}}_{m}(-{\varphi},-x)+(-1)^{m}{\mathfrak{p}}_{m}({\varphi},x)

(A.42)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{N}\big({\mathfrak{p}}_{m-k}(-{\varphi},-x)+(-1)^{m-k}{\mathfrak{p}}_{m-k}({\varphi},x)\big)({\rm i}\xi)^{-k}

(A.43)

\displaystyle\quad+({\rm i}\xi)^{-m}\big({\mathtt{r}}_{N}(-{\varphi},-x,\xi)+\overline{{\mathtt{r}}_{N}({\varphi},x,\xi)}\big).

(A.44)

From (
A.41
) and Definition
A.6
, we deduce that

{\mathfrak{p}}_{m}(-{\varphi},-x)=(-1)^{m+1}{\mathfrak{p}}_{m}({\varphi},x).

(A.45)

Repeating this process with multiplicative factor
({\rm i}\xi)^{k-m}
for
k\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket
, we get

{\mathfrak{p}}_{m-k}(-{\varphi},-x)=(-1)^{m-k+1}{\mathfrak{p}}_{m-k}({\varphi},x).

With this in hand, we come back to (
A.39
) to obtain

{\mathtt{r}}_{N}(-{\varphi},-x,\xi)=-\overline{{\mathtt{r}}_{N}({\varphi},x,\xi)}.

This ends the proof of Lemma
A.4
.
∎

The following result is taken from
[
16
, Prop. 2.11]
(up to slight modifications to fit with our functional framework and purposes). It states that the class of homogeneous expansions is stable under composition and (anti-)commutation up to smoothing operators. The proof is based on Proposition
A.3
-1.

Proposition A.4
.

Consider two homogeneous operators
\mathfrak{p}_{1}\partial_{x}^{m}
and
\mathfrak{p}_{2}\partial_{x}^{m^{\prime}}
of degree
m\in\mathbb{Z}
and
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
, respectively. Then, there exist combinatorial constants given by
(C_{k,m})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
with

C_{0,m}\triangleq 1,\qquad\forall k\in\mathbb{N}^{*},\quad C_{k,m}\triangleq\frac{1}{k!}\prod_{p=1}^{k}(m-p+1),

such that:

(i)

for any
N\in\mathbb{N},
the composition admits an homogeneous expansion of degree
m+m^{\prime}
up to order
m+m^{\prime}-N
up to smoothing pseudo-differential oprators in
OPS^{m+m^{\prime}-N-1}
. More precisely,

\mathfrak{p}_{1}\partial_{x}^{m}\circ\mathfrak{p}_{2}\partial_{x}^{m^{\prime}}=\sum_{k=0}^{N}C_{k,m}\,\mathfrak{p}_{1}(\partial_{x}^{k}\mathfrak{p}_{2})\partial_{x}^{m+m^{\prime}-k}+\mathfrak{R}_{N},

where
\mathfrak{R}_{N}
is in
OPS^{m+m^{\prime}-N-1}
with the bound, for any
s\geqslant s_{0}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N},

\|\mathfrak{R}_{N}\|_{m+m^{\prime}-N-1,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim_{m,m^{\prime},N,\alpha,s}\|\mathfrak{p}_{1}\|_{s+\sigma(\alpha,m)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathfrak{p}_{2}\|_{s_{0}+\sigma(\alpha,m)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{p}_{1}\|_{s_{0}+\sigma(\alpha,m)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathfrak{p}_{2}\|_{s+\sigma(\alpha,m)}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

(A.46)

for some
\sigma(\alpha,m)>0;

(ii)

for any
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*},
the commutator admits an homogeneous expansion of degree
m+m^{\prime}-1
up to order
m+m^{\prime}-N
up to smoothing pseudo-differential oprators in
OPS^{m+m^{\prime}-N-1}
. More precisely,

[\mathfrak{p}_{1}\partial_{x}^{m},\mathfrak{p}_{2}\partial_{x}^{m^{\prime}}]=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\big(C_{k,m}\,\mathfrak{p}_{1}(\partial_{x}^{k}\mathfrak{p}_{2})-C_{k,m^{\prime}}\,(\partial_{x}^{k}\mathfrak{p}_{1})\mathfrak{p}_{2}\big)\partial_{x}^{m+m^{\prime}-k}+\mathfrak{R}_{N},

where
\mathfrak{R}_{N}
is in
OPS^{m+m^{\prime}-N-1}
with the bound, for any
s\geqslant s_{0}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N},

\|\mathfrak{R}_{N}\|_{m+m^{\prime}-N-1,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim_{m,m^{\prime},N,\alpha,s}\|\mathfrak{p}_{1}\|_{s+\sigma(\alpha,m,m^{\prime})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathfrak{p}_{2}\|_{s_{0}+\sigma(\alpha,m,m^{\prime})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{p}_{1}\|_{s_{0}+\sigma(\alpha,m,m^{\prime})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathfrak{p}_{2}\|_{s+\sigma(\alpha,m,m^{\prime})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

for some
\sigma(\alpha,m,m^{\prime})>0;

(iii)

for any
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*},
the anti-commutator admits an homogeneous expansion of degree
m+m^{\prime}
up to order
m+m^{\prime}-N
up to smoothing pseudo-differential oprators in
OPS^{m+m^{\prime}-N-1}
. More precisely,

[\mathfrak{p}_{1}\partial_{x}^{m},\mathfrak{p}_{2}\partial_{x}^{m^{\prime}}]_{\rm a}=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\big(C_{k,m}\,\mathfrak{p}_{1}(\partial_{x}^{k}\mathfrak{p}_{2})+C_{k,m^{\prime}}\,(\partial_{x}^{k}\mathfrak{p}_{1})\mathfrak{p}_{2}\big)\partial_{x}^{m+m^{\prime}-k}+\mathfrak{R}_{N},

where
\mathfrak{R}_{N}
is in
OPS^{m+m^{\prime}-N-1}
with the bound, for any
s\geqslant s_{0}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N},

\|\mathfrak{R}_{N}\|_{m+m^{\prime}-N-1,s,\alpha}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim_{m,m^{\prime},N,\alpha,s}\|\mathfrak{p}_{1}\|_{s+\sigma(\alpha,m,m^{\prime})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathfrak{p}_{2}\|_{s_{0}+\sigma(\alpha,m,m^{\prime})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{p}_{1}\|_{s_{0}+\sigma(\alpha,m,m^{\prime})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathfrak{p}_{2}\|_{s+\sigma(\alpha,m,m^{\prime})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

for some
\sigma(\alpha,m,m^{\prime})>0.

A.4.2
Symplectic diffeomorphisms of the torus and Egorov’s theorem

In this last section, we give the definition and properties of symplectic diffeomorphisms on the torus, used in our analysis for the transport reduction in Section
6.3
. Then, we discuss a quantitative Egorov-type theorem adapted to our functional context. This latter deals with the conjugation of pseudo-differential operators with symplectic diffeomorphisms.

A symplectic diffeomorphism on the torus
\mathbb{T}
is a transformation
\mathscr{B}
in the form

\displaystyle(\mathscr{B}u)(\lambda;\varphi,x)

\displaystyle\triangleq\big(1+\partial_{x}\beta(\lambda;\varphi,x)\big)({\mathcal{B}}u)(\lambda;{\varphi},x),\qquad(\lambda;\varphi,x)\in\mathcal{O}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{T},

(A.47)

\displaystyle({\mathcal{B}}u)(\lambda;{\varphi},x)

\displaystyle\triangleq u\big(\lambda,\varphi,x+\beta(\lambda;\varphi,x)\big).

The following proposition is a collection of standard properties of the map
{\mathscr{B}}
in (
A.47
), see for instance
[
60
, Lemma 6.1, Lemma 6.2]
and
[
13
, Lemma 3.30]
.

Proposition A.5
.

(Basic properties of symplectic diffeomorphisms on the torus)

There exist
\sigma,\delta>0
such that if

\|\beta\|_{s_{0}+\sigma}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\delta,

(A.48)

then, the following properties hold true:

(i)

The operator
\mathscr{B}
is invertible with inverse

\displaystyle(\mathscr{B}^{-1}u)(\lambda;\varphi,x)

\displaystyle=\big(1+\partial_{x}{\widehat{\beta}}(\lambda;\varphi,x)\big)({\mathcal{B}}^{-1}u)(\lambda;{\varphi},x),

(A.49)

\displaystyle({\mathcal{B}}^{-1}u)(\lambda;{\varphi},x)

\displaystyle=u\big(\lambda,\varphi,x+{\widehat{\beta}}(\lambda;\varphi,x)\big).

(A.50)

In addition,

\|\widehat{\beta}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim_{s,q,d}\|\beta\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

and the following estimates occur

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}^{\pm 1}u\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathscr{B}^{\pm 1}u\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim_{s,q,d}\|u\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\beta\|_{s+1}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|u\|_{s_{0}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(A.51)

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{B}^{\pm 1}-\textnormal{Id})u\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|(\mathscr{B}^{\pm 1}-\textnormal{Id})u\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim_{s,q,d}\|\beta\|_{s_{0}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|u\|_{s+1}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\beta\|_{s+1}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|u\|_{s_{0}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}};

(A.52)

(ii)

The operator
{\mathscr{B}}
preserves the average in space, namely

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}({\mathscr{B}}u)({\varphi},x)\,{\rm d}{x}=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}(1+\partial_{x}\beta({\varphi},x))u({\varphi},x+\beta({\varphi},x))\,{\rm d}{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle y=x+\beta({\varphi},x)}}{{=}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}u({\varphi},y)\,{\rm d}{y}.

(A.53)

In particular, functions with zero average in space are preserved by the action of the operator;

(iii)

Denoting by
{\mathscr{B}}^{\star}
the
L_{x}^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
-adjoint of
{\mathscr{B}}
, then
{\mathscr{B}}^{\star}={\mathcal{B}}^{-1}
;

(iv)

Let
\beta_{1},\beta_{2}
satisfying (
A.48
), then for any
s\in[s_{0},S],

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\widehat{\beta}\|_{s}\lesssim_{s,d}\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{s}+\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{s_{0}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|\beta_{k}\|_{s+1}.

(A.54)

(v)

The operator
\mathscr{B}
(or
\mathcal{B}
) is:

(a)

reversibility preserving if and only if

\beta(\lambda;-\varphi,-x)=-\beta(\lambda;\varphi,x);

(b)

momentum preserving if and only if

\forall\,y\in\mathbb{T},\quad\beta(\lambda;\varphi-\vec{\jmath}y,x)=-\beta(\lambda;\varphi,x+y).

We consider

\mathscr{B}^{\tau}u(\lambda;\varphi,x)\triangleq\big(1+\tau\partial_{x}\beta(\lambda;\varphi,x)\big)u\big(\lambda;\varphi,x+\tau\beta(\lambda;\varphi,x)\big).

(A.55)

Observe that
\mathscr{B}^{\tau}
is the flow associated with
\partial_{x}\circ b=\textnormal{Op}({\rm i}b\xi+\partial_{x}b)
with

b(\tau,\lambda;\varphi,x)\triangleq\frac{\beta(\lambda;\varphi,x)}{1+\tau\partial_{x}\beta(\lambda;\varphi,x)}\cdot

Namely, we have

\partial_{\tau}\big(\mathscr{B}^{\tau}u\big)=\partial_{x}\big(b\mathscr{B}^{\tau}u\big)\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad\mathscr{B}^{0}=\textnormal{Id}.

The following result states the stability of the class of operator admitting an homogeneous expansion under conjugation by a diffeomorphism of the torus. The proof is a small variation of
[
16
, Prop. 2.28]
.In particular the point
(iv)
can be obtained tracking the corresponding symmetries along the constructive proof in the given reference.

Proposition A.6
.

(Egorov Theorem for homogeneous operators)

Let
N,l_{0}\in\mathbb{N},

m\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}(\lambda,\varphi,x)
be a smooth function. Let us simply denote
\mathscr{B}\triangleq\mathscr{B}^{1}
the time
1
of the flow introduced in (
A.55
). There exist
C_{0},\sigma_{N},\sigma_{N}(l_{0})>0
and
\delta\triangleq\delta(S,N,l_{0},q)\in(0,1)
such that, if

\|\beta\|_{s_{0}+\sigma_{N}(l_{0})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\delta,\qquad\|\mathfrak{p}\|_{s_{0}+\sigma_{N}(l_{0})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C_{0},

(A.56)

then the conjugation of
\mathfrak{p}\partial_{x}^{m}
by the diffeomorphism
\mathscr{B}
is a pseudo-differential operator of order
m
admitting an expansion in the form

\mathscr{B}^{-1}\big(\mathfrak{p}\partial_{x}^{m}\big)\mathscr{B}=\sum_{k=0}^{N}\mathfrak{p}_{m-k}\partial_{x}^{m-k}+R_{N},

with the following properties:

(i)

The principal symbol
\mathfrak{p}_{m}
is

\mathfrak{p}_{m}(\lambda;\varphi,x)=\Big[\big(1+\partial_{\eta}\widehat{\beta}(\lambda;\varphi,y)\big)^{m}\mathfrak{p}(\lambda;\varphi,y)\Big]|_{y=x+\beta(\lambda;\varphi,x)}

and we have the estimates

\forall\,s\geqslant s_{0},\quad\forall\,k\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket,\quad\|\mathfrak{p}_{m}-\mathfrak{p}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{p}_{m-k}\|_{s}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim_{s,N}\|\beta\|_{s+\sigma_{N}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{p}\|_{s+\sigma_{N}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\beta\|_{s_{0}+\sigma_{N}}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}};

(A.57)

(ii)

The remainder term
R_{N}
is a pseudo-differential operator in
OPS^{m-N-1}.
Moreover, for any
l\in\mathbb{N}^{d},
with
|l|\leqslant l_{0}
and for any
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
with
n_{1}+n_{2}+l_{0}\leqslant N-q-m
, the operator
\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}R_{N}\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}
is
\mathcal{D}^{q}
-tame and satisfies the estimate, for any
s\in[s_{0},S]

\mathfrak{M}_{\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}R_{N}\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}}(s)\lesssim_{s,q,d,N,l_{0}}\|\beta\|_{s+\sigma_{N}(l_{0})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{p}\|_{s+\sigma_{N}(l_{0})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\beta\|_{s_{0}+\sigma_{N}(l_{0})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}};

(A.58)

(iii)

Let
s_{1}\geqslant s_{0}.
Consider
\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\mathfrak{p}^{[1]},\mathfrak{p}^{[2]}
satisfying the additional smallness condition

\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|\beta_{k}\|_{s_{1}+\sigma_{N}(l_{0})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\delta,\qquad\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathfrak{p}^{[k]}\|_{s_{1}+\sigma_{N}(l_{0})}^{q,\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C_{0},

then

\forall\,k\in\llbracket 0,N\rrbracket,\quad\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{p}_{m-k}\|_{s_{1}}\lesssim_{s_{1},d,N}\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{p}\|_{s_{1}+\sigma_{N}}+\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{s_{1}+\sigma_{N}}

(A.59)

and for any
l\in\mathbb{N}^{d},
with
|l|\leqslant l_{0}
and for any
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
with
n_{1}+n_{2}+l_{0}\leqslant N-q-m,

\|\langle D\rangle^{n_{1}}\partial_{\varphi}^{l}\Delta_{12}R_{N}\langle D\rangle^{n_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{L}(H^{s_{1}})}\lesssim_{s_{1},d,N,n_{1},n_{2},l_{0}}\|\Delta_{12}\mathfrak{p}\|_{s_{1}+\sigma_{N}(l_{0})}+\|\Delta_{12}\beta\|_{s_{1}+\sigma_{N}(l_{0})}.

(A.60)
